# Continuity State

## Opening canon

- The story opens in 2042 at Kestrel Station and the Salt Meridian. Elias Rook is 35, a Havenport Rescue Service field technician, and began Volume 01 at **Stage 0: Unborrowed**.
- Elias is competent with ordinary rigging, confined-space entry, thermal imaging, breathing gear, seam safety, and human retreat lines. His central movement is from solitary command toward disclosure, delegation, and bounded promises; he is not a passive vessel or an instant super-rescuer.
- Nell Rook is 27, a rail signal engineer inside Kestrel when the collapse begins. Her direct pre-collapse alarm is separate from the later clipped signal. Mara Okafor, Tomas Vale, Joon Park, Inez Sato, and Sable Arden enter with the roles fixed in the character bible. Elias and Mara are professional partners, not romantic partners, at the opening, at the end of Batch 0001, and at the end of Batch 0002.

## Day map for Volume 01

The collapse and Chapters 1–10 fall on a **Saturday**. Batch 0002 then runs:

- **Sunday** — Chapter 11 (care), Chapter 12 (the public comparison, the 1600 archive notice), Chapter 13 (the west apron relay, against the 1600 deadline). Chapter 14 opens at **16:00 the same Sunday**, at the transfer.
- **Monday** — Chapter 14 (the supervised document exchange in the morning, the medical ledger, and the witness statements through to 16:00), then Chapter 15 (the vote, seven in the evening).
- **Tuesday** — Chapter 16 (the clinic wall, the call, and the transit operations desk, ending in the hall that evening).
- **Tuesday night** — Chapter 17 (Tomas and the relay, from half past midnight).
- **Wednesday** — Chapter 18 (the survey, 09:00, stopped at 12:40), Chapter 19 (the Office at the gate in the afternoon; the ninety-six-hour hold is accepted in the late afternoon).
- **Thursday** — Chapter 20 (the working session; Hollis Vane reads the originals in the morning and writes his finding the same day).

Batch 0003 runs:

- **Friday** — Chapter 21, **day six**, the hold's third day. This is Perry Dunne's *original* date and it falls inside the hold, one day before expiry.
- **Saturday** — Chapter 22, **day seven**, the hold's fourth day. The review board sits in the afternoon. The hold expires at midnight and the site reverts to the standing designation.
- **Sunday** — Chapter 23, **day eight**, the listening campaign.
- **Monday** — Chapter 24, **day nine**, the depot.
- **Tuesday** — Chapter 25, **day ten**, the day after the corridor was used.
- **Wednesday into Thursday** — Chapter 26, **days eleven and twelve**, the slower plan.
- **Friday** — Chapter 27, **day thirteen**, the crew reduction, on the date the Chapter 21 conversation produced.
- **Friday night** — Chapter 28, **day thirteen**, Adare.
- **Saturday** — Chapter 29, **day fourteen**, the records.
- **Sunday** — Chapter 30, **day fifteen**, the working shift.
- **Monday** — Chapter 31, **day sixteen**, the descent the Chapter 30 board promised.
- **Tuesday** — Chapter 32, **day seventeen**, the eleventh day. Three clocks land here.
- Chapters 33–40 run Tuesday night (17), Wednesday (18), Thursday (19), Friday (20), Saturday (21), Saturday night (21), Sunday (22), Monday (23).

**Batch 0004 is written against that map and the map is now fixed prose, not a plan.** Chapter 31 is Monday day sixteen; Chapter 32 is Tuesday day seventeen and is simultaneously the board's eleventh day; Chapter 33 is Tuesday night day seventeen; 34 is Wednesday day eighteen; 35 is Thursday day nineteen; 36 is Friday day twenty; 37 is Saturday day twenty-one; 38 is Saturday night day twenty-one; 39 is Sunday day twenty-two; 40 is Monday day twenty-three. Chapter 40's board books a **thermal survey for Wednesday morning, day twenty-five**, which is the first scheduled event of Batch 0005. **Four frozen counters were corrected in the first pass of this batch** — Chapter 31 had the wall man counting *eleven* days and *three weeks* for events that were sixteen and twelve days old, Chapter 32 had the file wrong for *twenty-three* days on day seventeen, and Chapter 39 had the wall man at *twenty-three* days on day twenty-two. **Use day-relative language by default and re-derive nothing.**

**Two counting bases are permanently in play and are never the same quantity.** *Day 17* is seventeen days since the collapse. *The eleventh day* is the review board's count from the collapse Saturday, and it expired on the same Tuesday. Chapter 32 puts both on the board with a line saying so, and no later chapter may call one the other.

If a weekday is used in later chapters it must be on this map. **Every elapsed-time phrase is counted from the collapse on Saturday, day zero**, and a later batch may not reuse a counter that its own date has outrun — Chapters 28, 29 and 30 originally said *nine days* on days thirteen, fourteen and fifteen, and Chapter 29 said *four weeks* on day fourteen. Prefer day-relative language.

## The eleven-day condition

The review board of Chapter 22 converted the citizens' request into a condition: **a joint structural inspection, or a written statement of refusal, on the eleventh day counting from Saturday, which is a Tuesday.** Chapter 30 is the Sunday before it, and the descent back down to the man at the wall is the **Monday** after it, which is Chapter 31. Three clocks land on that Tuesday: the site-held refusal is filed, **Ilse Brauer** rescans the aisle ledger with all four columns, and the routine shuttle-six dispatch confirmation is due. A written refusal lapses the joint-inspection condition, which means the resident observer and the residents' copy of every measurement lapse with it, and the standing designation is unchallenged for a cycle.

## Timeline and physical evidence

- The first Kestrel rail alarm is recorded at **05:40**. The changed service door becomes visible at **05:42**. The pre-collapse route entry is timestamped **05:17:11**. Sable’s equipment-failure form is timestamped **05:24**, before the first alarm and before the door observation. The rescue report was filed at **06:27**.
- A routine Meridian patrol logged a real fault on the west signal cabinet at **04:50**. A two-person Continuity inspection team signed out for west apron signal cabinet 4C at **05:02** and returned at **06:50**. First signature: **A. Vashti**. Second signature: filled in on the original, blank on the Office copy. The holder field for sector 4C reads *transferred — west apron maintenance group — holder field not carried forward at renumbering*.
- The west apron relay’s mechanical fault tape (Chapter 13) carries two ticks before any alarm, **05:17:11** and **05:24:40**, and the team holds a graphite impression of that tape. The paper route log was already absent from the cabinet’s felt slot when they arrived, cleanly removed by a hand that knew the retaining clip; the Office transfer clerk recorded **LOG ABSENT PRIOR TO TRANSFER**.
- At 16:00 the Office transferred Kestrel’s site records into archive crates. The structure was not physically sealed at that point; the containment and seal escalation comes later, in Chapter 19, and ends in a ninety-six-hour hold rather than a closure.
- The service-door aftermath remains physically evidenced by tool marks, measurements, wet footprints, a photographed open-door record, survivor memories, and Joon’s raw audio. After the third wheel stops, the door is **structurally unusable** and drops out of the immediate Civic Spine route index. It has not been proven physically absent; shifted, unsafe, or otherwise branch-affected remains possible. **Chapter 25 owns the stronger physical-absence proof, and it proves it for the corridor, not the door.**
- Chapter 12’s page **SERVICE DOOR — WHAT IS PROVEN** records three separate states: **UNSAFE** (proven), **SHIFTED** (possible), **PHYSICALLY ABSENT** (unproven). Do not collapse them. The Civic Spine’s `Suggested correction` offered to delete the 05:42–06:18 entries to make the index agree; it was refused and is on the public record. **In Chapter 25 this page comes down** — Elias takes it off the wall, Joon files it under a cover sheet stating that it is still true about the service door and is not to be read into the Chapter 25 finding. It is not withdrawn and not wrong.
- **The service door and the lower service corridor are two different physical elements.** The *door* is the narrow leaf in the bowed frame at the far end of the corridor, forty-one inches at the frame and twenty-seven at the narrowest, with a lintel leaning about six degrees, a bright scrape mark, and two handles at two heights. The *corridor* — called the **lower service run** on the survey sheets and **4C service run** in a 2029 section drawing — is the run of damp emergency-stripped concrete from the service landing to that door and beyond it, which Groups A, B and C walked during the Still Hand window, and which Chapter 9 found ending at the altered wall.
- **The lower service corridor's geometry is now fixed and may not be changed without a measurement scene:** loading lip at **0**; a storm-runoff fall (Ru Sabar and Emil Baros call it a dip) at **9 m**; a scuff arc at **10 m**; a floor anchor hole at ankle height; a painted band at shoulder height, four colors in eleven years; termination at **11.0 m**; an inspection niche at **12 m** with a shelf, a tin of washers and a bar of soap; the service door at **31.0 m**, unreachable. A second emergency-strip fitting has been out since the winter of 2039 and is on nobody's work order. The 2029 section drawing marks the far stretch *unventilated, not maintained*.
- **Chapter 25 proves physical absence for the far twenty meters of the corridor** and nothing else. Residue at the 11.0 m boundary: continuous original concrete with no joint, a square-cut paint band, a length of strip conduit ending in midair with a bright unweathered end face, two meters of floor polished by boots, a scuff arc, a boot brace in the dust of an anchor hole, and a greasy right handprint at 1.40 m. The inspection niche at 12 m and the survey sheet clipped inside it on Saturday are gone with it. There is no rubble, no fall, and no sign of an event.
- **The service door's three states are unchanged after Chapter 25** and the Chapter 25 page says so in terms: the door is not in the table, its status is unchanged, and it is not to be settled by anything on that page. The door is now simply unreachable from the near end, so it cannot be re-measured, and its **PHYSICALLY ABSENT** state remains unproven for the door. Do not let a later chapter collapse the corridor's proven absence into the door's unproven state.
- **What Chapter 25 can and cannot prove.** It can prove that the span of the run that has ever carried anybody in a record coincides with the window in which the Still Hand loan was open, because the return condition is a wheel and the time it stopped is in a medical ledger the Office has countersigned. It cannot prove cause, and it does not write one. The honest finding is worse than a cause: **there is no version of Saturday in which sixteen people come out of Car 3 and the maintenance pocket without the loan**, so the route depended on a future that the return had already altered. Nobody broke anything; it did not stay.
- The route index’s report that Car 3 carried no passengers conflicts with the physical rescue. Do not treat the survivors or the route as imaginary. **The current route index has no entry for the lower service run at all** and reports `First recorded instance: 12:31` of the week in which the team surveyed it, because the team's own survey was the first system that ever held it.


## Rescue count and locations

- **Group A:** Fifteen people physically from Car 3, including Tarin Voss, Niko Senn, and Anja Ro.
- **Group B:** Oren Pike, one person on the Car 3 manifest who was not in the first physical count.
- Groups A and B made a paper count of **sixteen** at the first refuge.
- **Group C:** Seven people in the service alcove behind the changed wall, including **Nadi Bell** and **Ansel Duarte**, who cannot walk; also Ruta Amsel (broken wrist), **Petar Lisk** (a nine-year-old carrying a fire extinguisher), and an older man with white hair. Nadi carries a child's school coat. They were not in Car 3.
- The paper count is **twenty-three** and is unchanged through Batch 0002. Care moved, nobody was transferred out, and the Office's count attempt was refused.
- **Group D:** Two deeper heat signatures farther inside Kestrel. Not rescued in Batch 0001 or Batch 0002. After the Chapter 18 survey: **one human-sized signature confirmed by sound** (a person answering three knocks in the route-unsafe code), **one unconfirmed second reading** at roughly nine meters that may be a person or a warm pipe. They are not counted, not extracted, and not named. Group D remains separate from Group C.

## Still Hand lock and aftermath

- The first loan is future Mara’s bounded **Still Hand**: keep one named living body, Tarin Voss, from failing while present Mara repairs him. It cannot heal, resurrect, protect Elias from crushing force, or solve the tunnel. The Chapter 2 offer names the gift in a `Gift name` field, so **Still Hand** may be used freely in later prose.
- Elias disclosed the offer, target, boundary, and return before acceptance. Present Mara consented to ordinary emergency treatment and her own repair work under emergency rules, explicitly not on behalf of future Mara.
- Future Mara’s branch-specific reason is a rescue in which a living person was left behind while a cleaner route was pursued. Her boundary forbids killing, forced treatment, and abandonment. The third maintenance wheel is the concrete automatic return trigger.
- The Still Hand faded when the third wheel stopped. Elias cannot voluntarily release, extend, or renew it. Chapter 9's possible extension was refused by the same future Mara for the same reason, and Chapter 7 produced a deliberate `No answer` to a request to reopen the exchange. **No new loan has been accepted anywhere in Volume 01 so far.**
- Elias has Stage 1: First Witness knowledge. He cannot reopen this loan, summon Mara’s voice, or treat a branch memory as a command.
- Elias loses sensation and fine control in his left hand. A triage medic in Chapter 4 requires him to say out loud when the hand fails so another person can take the job. This is a standing practice and it now appears repeatedly in his own speech; it is a physical limit, not a symbol.

## Consent and voices

- The present Mara’s consent covers present treatment and repair, not future Mara’s capacity. Elias’s Chapter 4 incomplete disclosure is on the record, and Chapter 14 puts the whole exchange into a signed medical entry that the Office has countersigned, including the undisclosed afterimage and the refusal of an extension.
- **A named subject may withdraw the named field of a record without withdrawing the event, and the withdrawal is entered as a finding and not as an absence.** This is Finding 3 in the Office’s own file and it was used twice in Chapter 21: **Tarin Voss** withdrew the field with his name on it, and **Niko Senn** withdrew a second page because its note said *declined publication* instead of the wording he dictated. The medical page’s offer, boundary, return, and countersignature survive without a name, and **Chapter 25’s proof depends on the return being intact without a name on it.** Nobody has suggested putting a name back and nobody may.
- **The possible-Elias voice** appears only as preserved raw audio played in public in Chapter 12, carrying the same warning as Chapters 6 and 7. It offers no gift, no route command, no identity, and no proof of a faction. It is not Elias Forty-Seven and is not identified as the Returning Hand in Volume 01. The raw file is preserved in five buildings. **In Chapter 30 the copy in the sealed Office node is 0.4 seconds longer than the team’s, with a flat carrier gap in the middle of the file.** Joon recorded the fact and refused, out loud in the room, to put a name or a meaning on it, and asked the room and Director Arden not to. **This is a concrete artifact, not a mechanic, and not evidence of institutional editing.** If it returns it must stay a fact unless a later batch deliberately earns a meaning for it.
- **Halla Dren** is a possible person, not a lender. Shaft and outfall foreman, nineteen years ahead, in a branch where an unsealed shaft is the only air for about four hundred people. She gave **no gift**; her notice read `No loan offered` and `Warning: the branch is changing`. She gave the location of the 2031 repair pour, warned that the branch is changing and warnings stop being free, and asked that the shaft not be named in any consolidated list, because a named location becomes one to be closed. Her boundary: if the crew opens the shaft or moves anyone out of the Quarter without telling the people there first, she stops answering. **Joon recorded the request and refused it, in the same hand on the same page.** Nobody asked her a second question and the refusal was not retried. **In Chapter 23 the man behind the wall told the team not to go and ask her, and Elias said so out loud, and the Chapter 23 log reads *no contact with H. Dren sought or offered.*** The cost of honoring the rule is now explicit: she is the only person who could tell the team why the corridor shortened, and the team may not ask.
- **Nell’s clipped transmission** is a present-day, branch-crossed signal carrying her present voice and a rail engineer’s code. It is not a future Nell echo, not a pre-collapse recording, not a safe location, and not proof that she wants extraction. Fragments: the north stair warning, the people-behind-the-wall warning, **the east branch holds** (Chapter 20), and **do not use the west run** (Chapter 29, four words, 02:04, worst compression yet).
- **The signal carrier and the 05:17 route-entry carrier are the same physical path.** The old maintenance route under Kestrel carries both. This is a carrier comparison, not proof of a location, and it must not become a retrieval command. As of Chapter 29 the carrier path runs through twenty meters of the lower service run that are no longer there, and Joon recorded that as a carrier fact and refused to interpret it.
- **The live question, unanswered:** is the old maintenance route a way to reach Nell, or a way to close the branch she is keeping open? Every decision the team takes about it is a decision about her and not about the ground, and nobody in the hall is willing to write an answer until there is more.
- Elias’s reply to Nell is unsent. It lives in his notebook marked **NOT FILED. NOT SENT. A PROMISE I HAVE NOT EARNED THE RIGHT TO MAKE YET.** In Chapter 29 he wanted to file it, was refused out loud by Joon and by Mara in front of witnesses, and did not. The reason he gave is honest and recorded: the only line in the world that could carry a reply is the one she has just told him not to use. His second, uglier reason — that he is afraid of a survey — is on the board too. Nobody has received the envelope.
- No one is used as an anchor without knowing the risk. Inez’s refuge records resident consent, refusal, and withdrawal; it is not a shared-load circuit or a new power stage.
- **Group D’s first member is now a voice, not a signature.** In Chapter 23 a present-day man behind the altered wall spoke through the bore line and gave no name, no count and no condition. He is **not a future echo, not a lender, and not a source of a gift**, and he must not be converted into one. The second thermal reading at roughly nine meters remains unconfirmed and untouched. Group D is still not rescued and still not counted. The record reads *voice at the wall*.
- **What the man behind the wall wants, in his own words:** keep the run on the paper in every building the team can reach and in the one it cannot; send nobody in; and write nothing more than *voice at the wall* — no name, no count, no condition. **His stated boundary has teeth:** if one person goes into the run it shuts, because a run with a body in it is a run that frightened people close, and he has watched it happen to others. **His reason for answering:** the team published its own objection, so there was a record on his side of the concrete that could be argued with. **His want, as Chapter 30 states it:** the run is in use by people who are not on any list, and a run that is only in a list gets closed by somebody who never had to walk it. **He has been behind the wall eight days, not eleven: Chapter 23 is day eight, and he came this way on the Saturday of the collapse.** He is also not the man who notices the road is shorter — that is the Chapter 25 finding — and nothing may be attributed to him before it is said on the page. The team goes back down on the **Monday** of Chapter 31, which is what the Chapter 30 board now says, and asks him what he wants to do about a road that is now eleven meters long, and then does what he says — **and does not go and get him, whatever he says.**


## Record and custody

- **`MNT-4C-17`** is a pre-2035 class-three internal maintenance credential for west apron sector 4C, issued early in that sector’s sequence, and it is not a Civic Spine author record. Class three permits local lockout, local feeder isolation, a maintenance door state, a temporary shoring permit, and an unindexed local route entry. It does not permit a public alarm or any change to a passenger record. Its owner is **unknown and unattributable** because the 2035 renumbering did not carry the holder field forward.
- **As of Chapter 24 the credential also appears as a signature.** The second signature line of the 05:02 dispatch sheet original is the string `MNT-4C-17` in the box that asks for a person. A class-three maintenance credential cannot lawfully close a two-person Continuity inspection, so either the credential was misused or the sheet was closed with the wrong instrument, and both are institutional problems rather than personal ones. The 05:02 sign-out and the 05:17 route entry are therefore **the same credential in the same morning**, fifteen minutes apart. **They are the same credential, not provably the same hand** — Hollis Vane insists on that distinction and it must be preserved.
- The relay card was read once; corroded contacts mean a second read is not available. The card holds route entry, sector, credential, sequence four, and 05:17:11. Earlier same-morning entries in that sector are gone from the buffer and were never on the Spine.
- The public sheet carries the code, the sector, the sequence, the time, the bell, and the sentence **holder unknown — roster held at the Spine depot, not in this cabinet / no individual identified.** Joon refused to let the code sit beside Sable’s form without that sentence.
- Custody after Chapter 19: **Elias** holds the original notebook; **Mara** holds the medical ledger; **Joon** holds the raw audio, the manifest, the measurements, the witness statements, and the Bell file; **Inez** holds the residents’ lock-box with the credential copy, the 2034 report copy, and the Bell file; **the Office** holds copies only, plus a sealed node containing the survey data. A fifth copy of the Bell file is in the residents’ file, initialled by Ru Sabar, and the fifth copy is the only one that was never in the same building as an Office vehicle. Nothing in the custody chain is duplicated in a single place, and nothing may be consolidated into one list at any point in this volume.
- **The 05:02 dispatch original is box 214, aisle nine, archive floor, Spine depot.** Produced to Hollis Vane on Monday under the review board’s order; out at 11:04 and back at 11:41, both on the room camera and both on Joon’s. Copies number **four**: Joon, the residents’ file, the Office, and Vane, and Vane counted them out loud in the freight bay. It is four and not five, and the Office’s copy is inside the four. Production means production, and Vane says explicitly that he is not on the team’s side.
- **The lower service run’s record has a fixed copy scheme and the number may not drift.** **Three places the team controls as of Monday** — the survey sheet in Joon’s custody, Joon’s custody log, and the hall board in Inez’s chalk — and **a fourth from Tuesday, the residents’ file**, which the board’s own survey condition requires. The **Office’s copy is in the sealed node and is not counted as ours.** On Sunday, Chapter 23, Elias promised the man at the wall that it would be in four places by Tuesday. Chapter 25’s Saturday column reads *four places, and a copy in the Office node*. Chapter 29 puts **three paper copies** on the hall table — the original sheet, the residents’ file copy, and the node’s print — and the node’s is the one that disagrees. **Anja Ro read the number off the survey sheet in the niche at 12 m twice on Monday, and the niche and the sheet are gone as of Tuesday.**
- **The aisle log for aisle nine has two entries on the collapse Saturday: 09:40 (*Kestrel site records, 4C, material pending transfer*, complete) and 14:12 (*Rout. arch. rev. — aisle 9 — 214*) with the subject field blank.** The crates from the west apron went to a different aisle and the site records are still sealed four boxes down, so whoever came at 14:12 came for the day-file box and not for the site records. **A field that does not exist cannot be blank; a field that can be blank can be filled later by somebody who was not there, accurately about their own afternoon and silent about somebody else’s.** That is Vane’s formulation and it is now the volume’s governing sentence about institutions.
- **Brauer’s single-pass flatbed scan of the bound, single-sided aisle ledger cut off the fourth column.** The defect is **on the page in Chapter 24**: she did the Office’s copy of the log page in the Spine archive room on Monday while the room was still arguing about the blank field, because a page produced under a board’s order goes into the node that afternoon whether anybody is reading it or not. It took about ninety seconds. The bed takes one page at a time, the operator does not turn the book over, and the fourth column runs past the edge of the glass. She initialed the scan time and did not look at the frame, because she was filing it and not reading it, and nobody else in the room was either. It is discovered in Chapter 29. The Office’s copy of the log therefore has no subject column at all, and no one in that building can see the absence. Brauer will redo it on **Tuesday**, the eleventh day. Nobody may treat this as an accident without a scene that earns it, and nobody may treat it as evidence of editing without one either.
- **Three institutional patterns, named in Chapter 29 and unfixed:** a field that does not exist (the 4C holder, 2035); a field left blank (the aisle log, 14:12); a field never designed for the thing in question (the Office baseline survey, which has no keeper field and a single *person present* line filled by the Office’s own person, so **Emil Baros is present in free-text remarks and absent from every field query**). The written conclusion is: *in all three the name is not erased, the name is not present.*
- **Nadi Bell’s missing family row remains missing.** Yolanda Bell confirmed the family and the west apron run-sheet series **9-1-7-4** from memory; the physical carbon stub in Nadi’s possession carries the same four digits. The official list says four people in the building; there were five. Yolanda’s name is unpublished at Nadi’s insistence, and Joon — not Elias — is the one who asks her anything. A routine dispatch confirmation for shuttle six was filed without an incident reference and will take about nine days; it is **due Tuesday**, it is a smaller question than the team wanted, and **it is allowed to answer with nothing.**
- **R. Adare**, a broker working the west apron counter, offered to restore the digital row, sell an unlogged line, and move seven people out of the Quarter that night using the seven of them as anchors. Nadi refused and had the refusal written, dated, and signed. **He returned on the second Friday night with a four-anchor offer, east-bank accommodation with powered medical power for Osei’s wife, and a broker license application lodged inside the Kestrel classification.** Nadi stopped Elias answering and made him write the whole offer down a second time in the same form, and the refusal was signed again. The broker is refused, is not a new faction, is not identified with any organization, and **his threat is a form in three weeks in a neighbor’s hand, not violence.** A scarecrow with a form on it is standing at the gate. Whether the offer is criminal or licensed is open and the Office has to answer it.


## History Tomas Vale has disclosed

- The 2034 Kestrel collapse began with the same unexpected east-leg signal-cabinet draw and the same slab movement cycle as 2042, and the driving source is a buried paired node under the west apron, written off in 2035 with Tomas’s signature second on the page.
- Tomas sat on the 2029 Meridian panel that wrote the one-voice safety rule — one instruction, one route, no contradiction in the running order, with a unilateral senior-safety-officer reroute power. He signed the reroute on the day Samuel Rook died. He states that the rule was written by frightened competent people to stop a real thing, and he has not yet admitted that his own caution may be part of the problem.
- Tomas has an undisclosed notebook of admissions in a box with the 2034 report. It has not been read by anyone. It may be produced in a later batch.
- **Do not** extend this into the Mercy Array, the First Silence’s cause, the Returning Hand, the Choir, or the Compact. A redacted line in the 2034 report refers to *legacy communications equipment of unknown configuration*; that is all Volume 01 says.

## The local authority structure

- The Cinder Quarter refuge is a physical and civic tool: batteries, lamps, seam glass in a wooden frame, analog maps, and mechanical barriers. It cannot stabilize Kestrel, open a seam, or substitute for a safe re-entry plan.
- **It now has a dry end.** A block wall, a door, a lock and a records room, built on the **Monday of Chapter 24, day nine**, from four in the afternoon to midnight, out of nine pallets of block carried through the lower service run at 15:00, in a room that keeps every copy of everything that has happened in a week. It cost the **1,100 dollars that were left in the money** and came out of a second somebody in the Third Ward who takes cash, does not ask what it is for, and is not good and is fast. Inez put the number on the page in Chapter 22: the wall is **a quarter** of a **4,400-dollar** requirement to make the building a building, and the other 3,300 is on a list she says nobody would enjoy reading. The cable in the same scene is 240 m at **8,800** against a card price of 11,100. It is four inches off square.
- The first vote (Chapter 15) returned 31 consent, 9 refusal, 4 abstain, with refusals read aloud. The second vote (Chapter 20) returned 26 consent, 7 refusal, 1 abstention. The **Chapters 26–30 standing count is 42 cards in, 22 consent, 14 refusal, 6 abstention**, one of the 42 being a first-time cardholder who wanted to say no out loud in a room whose walls were covered in paper. All refusals are read aloud. Inez reads the whole board at seven every evening and Petar Lisk reads the new lines.
- Standing conditions: no borrowed circuit; no resident as an anchor in any sense; any person may withdraw their own card, lamp, and name with no reason and no meeting; the Office gets a power summary and not the residents’ log, lamp cards, or consent cards; a blackout means the team goes dark on its own batteries within ninety seconds and nobody is asked to stay in the dark.
- **THE STRAWB MAN PLAN** (Chapter 26, named by Ru Sabar over everybody’s objection) is the current plan and it is not a survey. Four separate carded power draws a day instead of one committed block, each revocable while running, each announced in the hall and on Ash Street. **The hall is dark from 0200 to 0400 every day**: no lights in the dry end, the seam-glass frame off, Petar walking it with a hand lamp. Ru Sabar withdrew her laundrette circuit permanently over it. A target list with no sequence, five domains and no coordinator, a stop page with an expiry, and a standing rule that a proposal from anybody who was not in the room counts the same as one from the man who was.
- The authority split is on the hall wall: Mara medical triage and the stop on moving any patient; Tomas structural limits and the stop threshold, written before anybody goes; Joon records, custody, and publication; Inez local power and the right to cut it; residents consent to the space; **Elias physical movement, and no sole command.**
- **The five-name stop plan.** Elias, Tomas, Mara, Joon, and Inez may each call a stop, and so may the hall, by sending anybody out the door. Each survey gets a fresh page and the page carries an expiry date at Elias’s request, so it cannot become a procedure with his name on it.
- **The five-signature promise.** Elias will not go below the west apron alone, and not at all unless four of five agree in the same week; if one says no he does not go, does not seek an overrule, and does not mention that he went. The hall may stop it. Signed by Elias, Mara, Tomas, Joon, and Inez, and copied out by Petar Lisk.
- **Two further promises, both signed in Chapter 30 and neither expiring.** First: *I am not going to be the last person to speak on any of this, and the last word does not close the item.* Second: **Ansel Duarte may break any plan of Elias’s by saying “that is a rescue,” in the hall, in front of anybody, without giving a reason and without having to be right**, and if he says it Elias does not go, does not put a plan, does not seek an overrule from the Office, and does not go looking for somebody with more standing than a nineteen-year-old on a mat. Petar Lisk wrote it out, Elias initialed it, Ansel initialed it, and **Inez initialed it as the holder of the copy.**
- **The site is held.** Chapter 30’s decision: a written statement of refusal is filed on the eleventh day, Tuesday, with the reasons on the page. The cost is named beside the gain: the joint-inspection condition lapses, the standing designation is unchallenged for a cycle, the 2031 node is not examined, the sealed node becomes the only version of that ground, and the man behind the wall has no way in.
- The 2031 repair pour in the apron slab runs eleven meters north-north-east and terminates six meters short of where the cameras point; a brick lining sits about a meter and a half beyond a gap at seven meters on the bore line.
- Ansel Duarte’s condition, accepted by the team: if the team goes back down, the purpose is **a conversation, not an extraction**, and *almost* is not to be written in any document.
- Mara’s condition, accepted by the team: **no medic on the line.** A medic stays at the top, and if somebody is found, treatment happens where they are found, in their air, at their pace. **Second medical refusal, added in Chapter 26: no predicted outcome, no number and no category, anywhere in any document about that wall — and nothing at all about what happens if somebody is found, because they do not know and would not learn by writing it down in advance.**


## The Office and the ninety-six-hour hold

- Sable Arden’s safety argument is credible and specific: the 2019 Salt Crossing outfall, where an unlicensed crew opened a shaft and six people died; Meridian 2029, eleven dead; a 2031 contractor survey finding a migrating void two hundred and ten meters from the Quarter’s clinic wall; and the site’s designated-seam-site classification pack.
- The **ninety-six-hour hold** accepted in Chapter 19 expired at midnight on the Saturday of Chapter 22. What it bought is spent. The site reverted to the standing designation, which puts the pre-ticked cause box back in the file.
- **The review board** sat on the hold's fourth day, Saturday, for two hours and ten minutes. It was Sable in the chair, **Osei Kwarteng** for the Meridian labor side, **Dr. Ilona Brecht** as independent structural engineer, the clinic's medical adviser, and a clerk with a recorder. It **granted**: the citizens' request converted into a survey condition (joint inspection, resident observer, every measurement copied to the residents' file); the 05:02 dispatch original to be produced to Vane in the hall; and the minutes to carry Vane's Finding 4 wording. It **withheld**: no seal, no clearance, no hold extension, classification still provisional, and a condition with a date. Sable stated in the room that the site is still hers to seal, and she rejected Finding 4 **by name** on the grounds that a request is not a finding and that treating one as the other would mean the institution taking instructions from a union hall. She will answer all four findings in writing, and agrees with three.
- **Hollis Vane** is the Office archivist: about sixty, lean, quiet, thorough, and unimpressed. He has been custodian of the file for nineteen years. He writes his findings in the room because the hold said so, he records a withdrawn page as a finding rather than as an absence, he is the one who has to read his own institution's contradiction aloud, and he is **not a friend, not an ally and not a convert.** He gave the team a copy of the dispatch original because the board ordered production, and said in the same breath that they will be tempted to treat him as being on their side and that he is not. He made the board more dangerous, not less.
- **Ilse Brauer** is the Spine depot's archive floor supervisor, the Office's field observer for the corridor survey, and the person whose single-pass scan of the aisle ledger is missing its fourth column. She reads instruments aloud before she hands them over. She is a bureaucrat doing a job and is not to be softened.
- The Office’s answer in the **1800 civic digest** is public, courteous, and cites the eleven real fatalities behind *one approved outcome*. It is pinned on the hall wall directly beneath Vane's Finding 1, eleven inches apart, so the institution is on the record disagreeing with itself in one building.
- **Perry Dunne** asked for the crew to be gone by the Friday inside the hold. That Friday could not be honoured without breaking a signed term, because the board sat Saturday afternoon with a team seat. His clean week moved to the following Friday, and **the crew reduction actually happened on that date** (Chapter 27), with Rafi and Kellan recalled to the city’s gas main. **The city has four certified confined-space rope technicians and three of them were in the hall. Doss remains, so the hall has one ticket and no pair, which is a different and worse problem than having none, and no replacement has been assigned.** The hall has not lost its rope competence outright; do not write that. Perry refused to be thanked and required both dates on the board with his name against them.
- Elias has never surrendered the original notebook, and the Office has copies only. He refused a controlled re-entry in exchange for the originals in Chapter 14 and offered a supervised reading instead.

## The eleven-day condition — SPENT

The condition lapsed at **ten past nine on Chapter 32's Tuesday, the eleventh day**, when the written statement of refusal was filed in Inez's hand with four reasons and five signatures and forty-two residents' cards listed on a second page. **What is gone with it:** the joint inspection, the resident observer appointed by the board, and the residents' copy of every measurement taken under it. **What is unchanged:** the lower service run at 11.0 m, the far twenty meters, the service door's three separate states, the 2031 node, the 2038 pour, the group inside, and the man behind the wall. **A thermal survey for the unconfirmed second reading at roughly nine meters is on the board for Wednesday morning, day twenty-five**, and a survey for a living group and a survey for a void are two different things and the rule on the wall says they may not be confused.

## Sable Arden's answers, the license, and the ninety-day clock

- **Finding 1 accepted with a mechanism:** a provisional classification can no longer carry a cause no observer entered. It will take **nine weeks** to clear the desks. Say nine weeks and not fixed.
- **Finding 2 done, and it produced nine:** sector 4C, class three, early sequence, against the 2035 renumbering — **nine credentials, nine holder fields not carried forward.** The mapping that would put nine names next to nine credentials was consolidated in 2039 into a **regional archive in Cordell** that does not answer to the Havenport office. **The answer moved one building over and no name exists.** A letter went Tuesday and will be read Wednesday by somebody with a queue. **The holder is still unknown and unattributable and no later chapter may convert nine into a person without a scene that earns it.**
- **Finding 3 adopted as office practice**, in writing. Every form for five years has a withdrawal line, and a clerk handed a withdrawal must enter it as a finding and not as an absence, and about a third of her clerks will get that wrong for a year.
- **Finding 4 rejected by name**, with the procedural reason and the worse one, and with a number attached: **a provisional classification may not stand ninety days without either a seal or a finding.** That is Sable's number, it is not negotiable, and it means the standing designation resolves on a clock whether or not anybody wants it to.
- **R. Adare is licensed**, effective Chapter 33's Tuesday, inside the Kestrel provisional classification. He held the routing log and the continuity chain from Saturday morning and did not declare them in the application; Sable approved anyway because the log is the best record of that ground in existence. Conditions: consent recorded, witness recorded, return recorded, a routing log with time, place and broker of record, **notification to the local residents' body within seventy-two hours**, and **an Office observer entitled to attend any routing event unannounced, with a hand on the log.** The coercion is the seventy-two hours, in Sable's own words: a routing event is over before a hall can be convened, and a clause that can be broken by a clock says *tell them afterwards, if you can*. The observer clause says *may*, not *will*, and no observer attended either of the two events that have happened.
- **The license has been used twice.** One subject at **21:50 on Chapter 33's Tuesday**, three subjects at **04:10 on Chapter 34's Wednesday**. The form has a box for a number, a box for consent, a box for a witness, a box for a return, **and no box for a street.**

## The broker's circuit

Nine people in a room above a shuttered print shop on Calder Street, standing in a closed ring about two meters across with their feet on a line of tape, between eleven at night and six in the morning, told they are waiting for a bed. A man in the middle with a clipboard calls the number *the pair*. A kettle, a bench, a portable heater, a chair with two people on it. The room gets easier when a man puts a hand on the outside of the ring and says a number. **A ring of people holding a branch route open is the same physics as a Door Memory, and Chapter 39 puts that parallel on the record.** It is a load, not a loan: no future person is involved, and no future person may be introduced into it.

- **Wendell Pryce**, fifty-eight, a retired signalman, has been in one of these in Cordell in 2036, knows exactly what the ring is, has said so out loud on the pavement, has asked to keep paying ninety dollars a night, and has said he is not asking permission but telling the team so that nobody can later write that nobody was asked. **He is not a resource and he may not be broken out of it by the team, because the team has no standing over him.**
- **Gil Trejo**, fifty-four, came out of the ring with a bus ticket on Chapter 35's Thursday, having told nobody on the pavement that he had been standing in it for three nights telling himself it was a queue.
- **Bess Ferran**, thirty-one, a night-shift phlebotomist at the Meridian hospital, was routed at 04:10 on Chapter 34's Wednesday and came to the hall the next morning. She signed a paper at the door reading *voluntary participation in a group stability arrangement* and had not read it, because she has never in her life read the second line of anything. **Somebody wrote *Ash Street* on a paper in her own handwriting and gave it to her at the door. Nobody in the building knows whose idea that was**, and Joon pinned a card saying so.
- **Teodor** bought a crossing on Chapter 35's Tuesday night for one hundred and forty dollars, with his own name on the paper, to reach his mother **Marija Teodor** in a branch apartment on the far side of the Meridian. At 01:20 on Thursday the load dropped and the route went out and he came back on his hands and knees on the Ash Street loading lip. **He is not grateful and was never asked to be.** His sentence is on the wall and is to stay there: **nobody asked me if I wanted to be a door.**

## The Friday entry and the 2038 pour

The Office's controlled re-entry ran on Chapter 36's Friday **at eight in the morning, not first light, because Teodor said on Thursday night that first light is when things happen to people on their way to work and Inez put it on the board**, and Sable's office agreed in forty minutes without ever saying no. Office instruments, an Office continuity chain, two named technicians from the regional pool (**Ruzicka and Ohno**), the hall's resident observer, and an entry limit written by Tomas and accepted by the technicians without a word, to a stated depth of **sixty centimeters**.

- The **2031 repair pour** is where the 2038 contractor survey said, terminating six meters short of the camera target.
- **At thirty-eight centimeters there is a second pour, stamped 2038.** No drawing in any of the four buildings holds a pour at that depth. **A hairline crack runs out of its corner about a meter and a half long.** It was photographed with a scale before anybody in the room had decided they wanted it, and **nothing has touched it and Tomas's standing number is that nothing touches it until somebody knows what is under it.** Nothing in this batch connects the 2038 pour to the corridor, to the relay, to Nell, or to anything before 2042.
- The entry instruments were run off **the clinic's generator margin**, which the hall shares with the east ward under a second-week agreement, and **Comfort Osei's powered frame went dark at 09:20.** The records room went on a lamp for a day.

## The counteroffer and Tomas's name

A continuity chain needs a named individual. **Tomas Vale filed a nine-line structural disclosure in his own words, under his own signature, before the entry**, at Mara's insistence and not the Office's: the 2034 collapse began with the same east-leg draw and the same slab cycle, the buried paired node under the west apron was written off in 2035 with his signature second on the page, he sat on the 2029 Meridian panel that wrote the one-voice safety rule, and he signed the reroute on the day Samuel Rook died. **He admitted in the same room that he had told the hall about the collapse for eight days and had not told it that he signed off the node the collapse is about.**

**The cost of that disclosure is on the record and it is permanent.** The 2034 write-off is now cross-indexed in the Office file to the chain that placed him under the apron, and the Office has asked him **in writing** to confirm whether he intends to be available to give evidence at any future inquiry. He could not answer that day. **Joon refused to let the room be comforted about it: *we made him do this.*** In five years the first thing anybody can say about the ground under that street is *Vale*. **Tomas's own notebook of admissions, stored with the 2034 report, has still not been read by anyone and has not moved.** The disclosure is not the notebook.

## The population of record and the movement record

The Office's daily return for a designated site demands a **count of persons present** and states that a count is the only return it will accept. **Nadi Bell refused in her own name and did not put anybody else's name on it.** Her reasoning, on the record: a count cannot lose anybody, a name can, and her mother's sentence about a hospital list in nineteen forty-nine is still true. She substituted a **movement record** — dated, one line per person, in / out / here, and where to and by what ordinary road and whether they chose it, and in the person's own words why they are staying, including one word and including no words at all. **Nothing leaves the building without the person saying it can.** The movement record is *not acceptable to the Office*, and Nadi named that as the second reason she wants it, because it puts two versions of the truth in two buildings so the one that erases people is outvoted by the one that does not.

**The Office's population-of-record ask is still open** and is paired with the offer of a named, publishable, unannounced Office observer on the Quarter's street and on any carded draw. It is the volume's genuinely ambiguous institutional offer and it is unanswered.

**The paper count is twenty-two.** It moved **once, on Chapter 37's Saturday, at five in the afternoon** — Niko Senn said it out loud at four that afternoon and the record was updated at ten past five with him standing there holding his coat — **and not before. He left on the Sunday morning after, day twenty-two**, for his sister on the east bank, on a bus, by ordinary road, by his own choice. His stated reason is that every conversation in the hall is about a road that used to be there and he wanted one decision that was his and did not go in a file. **His Chapter 21 precedent held and he used it from a bus: the record says *declined*, not *not used*.** No chapter may say the count moved on Chapter 33's Tuesday, four days early.

## The 0.4 seconds, restated after Chapter 38

**The eleven seconds in the two copies are byte-identical and the room tone is the same room.** The file is 0.4 seconds longer and the extra 0.4 seconds are in the middle and **they are not silence: they are a relay handoff, and they carry a Civic Spine stamp — `K-7Q` and four digits — in a format that is not in the Havenport stamp set indexed to that file.** Joon has seen that format once before in the last three weeks and will not place it, and he said so out loud in front of witnesses rather than sitting on it. **It is a fact. It has no meaning, no name, no faction, and no location, and it must not become evidence of institutional editing without a batch that earns that.** Joon's refusal of a meaning is not a refusal of a fact, and the difference was made explicit by **Petar Lisk**: *you told us not to say what it means, so we will not say what it means — can we still write down the number?* The possible-Elias voice remains unidentified, offers no power, and gained no new words in this batch.

## The man behind the wall after Chapter 31

**Sixteen days in as of Chapter 31, twenty-two as of Chapter 39**, counted from the collapse Saturday, day zero. **Three wants, in his own words, and none of them is a rescue and none of them is a favor:** the eleven meters goes on the paper as eleven meters, with thirty-one and the dates and the line between and his own account of finding out with a cart; the days he has said he will be there go on the board, **explicitly not as appointments**, because the line is live when he picks up a handset and not when a calendar says so; and **nobody runs up the ramp to tell him the road has changed**, which he called *procedure* and not mercy, because he has had mercy from people who were storing it. **He gave one fact for free and refused the other, and said the refusal was the point.** He is not a voice on a relay and never will be. He is a person in a room heard through concrete, and the only line that carries him is a cable and a handset and eleven meters of corridor.

**On Chapter 35's Thursday the team did not come, told nobody, and the man was not there, and that is the promise working.** Elias told him so on Chapter 40's Monday, and the man said *that is the correct sentence* and *it is also the reason I am still on this wire.*

**He has never been past the wall on this side and has volunteered exactly one thing about what is beyond it**, on Chapter 40's Monday, before putting the handset down mid-sentence: *it is warm on that side, in February, under the water.* **He has not been asked about the relay and the team is not going to ask him, because he said on Chapter 31 that he would answer one thing and he has not been asked.** Joon's own line: *he knows where he is standing, and those are different, and you know they are different, because you have spent a fortnight being taught the difference by a sixty-eight-year-old man with a bar of soap in his coat.*

## The Door Memory — offered, refused, and closed as a loan

- **At 22:14 on Chapter 39's Sunday, on a licensed broker's branch circuit, with the broker asleep**, the terminal produced an offer. It is the only interface block in the batch that concerns a future person, and it is a record, not an event.
- **The want, the boundary, and the reason, in her own words.** Want: keep the exit on her side open — not for her, for the people standing near it and the ones no list has. Boundary: if he comes through, it comes through; the door is held by people because nobody has made it official, and the moment it is official it closes for them and not for him; **do not make her road into an Office document, or she will stop answering and will not be able to say why.** Reason she answered: *you published your own objection.*
- **The clue is brief and incomplete.** It is not the door the team has been walking. It is on the far side of the wall the corridor ends at, and it is **under the water**, because the water is the only thing nobody on her side is allowed to touch. She told them not to count and said why in a sentence about a count taking a person off a list. **The relay is therefore not at eleven meters and not under the apron; it is past the wall, below the water line, and the man behind the wall has been standing on top of it for twenty-two days.**
- **IT WAS REFUSED. The refusal is Elias's own, made first and out loud, before anybody had finished arguing, and it was made by using Ansel Duarte's sentence on himself.** The record reads: *no loan offered by this hall; no capacity taken; no boundary stretched, amended, or asked about; nothing signed by anybody who was not asked; the gift was not accepted as a loan and the route is not accepted as a route.*
- **The return condition was *when you have written down that you did not use it*, and Joon's distinction is on the page: the return is not invoked, it is performed.** A later chapter that treats the return as pending, or that describes a deal, contradicts the record. **No loan may be accepted in Volume 01. A future Nell who answers again must make a new offer with a new boundary, and it may not be read as a consequence of a bargain.**
- **The envelope is still unsent**, and the reason has changed and is on the record: the road is available, the broker would carry a message for a fee, and a message from Elias to his sister costs a night of somebody's feet in a ring on Calder Street. The old reason — that he was afraid of a survey — was honest and is worse than the new one.

## Chapters 41–50 — the chamber, the boundary, the approved sequence, and the seal

**The thermal survey (Chapter 42, day twenty-five)** ran at first light with Ruzicka and Ohno, the Office chain, Petar Lisk reading the limits, and **Teodor as the residents' observer on the chain**, which nobody expected and Teodor did not want. **Teodor used his stop for the first time**, on the auger, over the 2038 pour, with no reason given, and Ruzicka put the case down herself.

**A brick lining crosses the lower run at 9.2 m** — old lime mortar, floor to ceiling, about a meter thick, on no drawing in any of the four buildings, warm on the far side. Behind it is a **brick chamber 9 m × 4 m × 2.5 m with a curved ceiling, four degrees warmer than the corridor.** It is not a corridor. The lower service run's **11.0 m mark is beyond the lining**, and both facts are true at once, and Doss asked for the recording to say so.

**The count: eight heat sources, seven people.** Four large and person-shaped, two smaller at the far end (children, or something the technician would not name), one rectangle the size of a small stove, and **one man sitting up against the far end who is the only source the camera can see moving.** Ruzicka's signed figure is **four confirmed, one probable, one unknown** — six — and the difference between her six and the machine's eight is *a stove and a man who will not lie still*. **Every later chapter uses seven as the group.** The flat seventeen-day CO2 curve is explained on the page as *a room with something in it replacing the air*, and nothing is said about what.

**The people and their positions: six of the seven came out.** Out: **Sena Ambrose**, 11, first out at 07:14 on Sunday; **Othman Bak**, 50, at 08:40; **Halvard Renn**, at 10:10; a fourth, forty-one, who came through at 12:20 and **died at 13:10 that afternoon in a hall on Ash Street with a blanket on him**; a **fifty-eight-year-old man at 20:20 on Monday who weighed fifty-one pounds and said his own name twice**, once into Lena Mir's ear and once to nobody in particular; and **Ivy Ambrose**, 7, Sena's sister, at 21:06 on Tuesday. **Perpetua Oyelaran, 34, remains under Ash Street at the time of sealing**, and her name is on the wall in Inez Sato's chalk.

**Nothing touched the 2038 pour and Tomas's number stands.** He refused to move the shoring at the chamber's near face and wrote **nine lines** on the wall where anybody at the lip could read them, ending with the ninth read off the board and then said: *there is one person left in that chamber and I am not going to get her, and I want the other half of it in my hand as well — the plan expired at ten minutes to six on Sunday morning, ten minutes before the brick came down, and nobody noticed for seven hours, and nobody has written a second one, and we have worked three days since on a plan that does not exist, and the plan did not stop anything, and what stopped was the drawing of another one, and the reason there is not another one is that he would not draw it and nobody else would draw it over him.* **The plan had two triggers and it expired on the first: 05:50 Sunday, ten minutes before the wall was opened, with nobody watching the clock. The second trigger, the fourth person through the wall, is the one the hall names as the moment it died, because that is the one that was watched, at 13:10, by a nine-year-old reading a board. The plan expired on the page and is no longer treated as running. The team worked three more days with nothing on the wall behind it, and that is now part of the record rather than a contradiction in it.** **Tomas's notebook of admissions was not produced in this batch. It is still in the 2034 box and nobody has read it.**

**The Still Hand offer of Chapter 46** was **Mara Okafor twenty-one years ahead, from a changed branch**, boundary *one body*, return *when the fourth person is through the wall* — **a return that was already satisfied four hours before the offer arrived**, which Joon recorded and deferred. **No loan was accepted anywhere in this batch.** Elias refused it out loud and first, then asked one question in front of witnesses about whether the boundary holds for a second body; the answer was *one body* and *no answer*. **Chapter 46 carries one interface notice only** — the offer — and the withdrawal that follows is rendered in prose as four fields, of which the third is empty. **Joon's record is the batch's spine: the refusal is the record and the question is also the record; no boundary was stretched because stretching it would have required asking, and the asking is what ended it; the return was not invoked because there was nothing to invoke.** Elias stated **this is not a malfunction** and asked for it in two voices. **Mara's cost is said once, in the corridor at 21:20 on the Sunday, and is not repeated: the first time in this month a future person withdrew instead of giving, it was because of something we did.** **She then did not say the fifth person would go at first light; she said the fifth goes as soon as the regional pair is on the street, and named that it would not be tomorrow morning, which is what happened.**

**The approved sequence of Chapter 48** came on two coercive headings, `Recommended re-entry` and a gift field, with the boundary **the order is obeyed, not answered, nobody in your hall is asked**, and a return offering the branch-side relay and a message Elias could put in her hand himself. **It is the same voice as the eleven seconds of raw audio, said by Joon and refused as an interpretation. It is not identified as anything.** **Elias refused it, said the second reason in front of the whole room — that it is the only thing anybody has offered him this month that gets him his sister — and did not need Ansel's sentence.** The refusal cost the **four-C local route line**, out of the records room at 12:41 on Tuesday, and with it **all four fragments of Nell's present-day carry**; Petar Lisk initialed the time because Joon asked for somebody who was not him. **The line was out for twenty-seven hours and thirty-nine minutes** — 12:41 Tuesday to 16:20 Wednesday, and that interval is the only correct figure — and came back **degraded**.

**Chapter 49 opens with the seventh attempt, in the present tense.** At 06:10 on Wednesday the crew goes down with no plan behind them, Ruzicka's camera finds Perpetua Oyelaran standing and holding the wall with her left hand and unable to take a step without it, and the medical answer is that a board put under her in the chamber chooses which way she goes before she chooses anything. **She is not attempted further and is not moved, and the decision to stop is made by Doss out loud and put in Lena Mir's page, not in a corridor.** The chapter now carries the extraction card in the present tense rather than in summary.

**The recovered signal, Chapter 49:** a `Warning: the branch is changing` and **eleven words — *I am not the road. I am the person who lives beside it. Do not count us.*** — on a four-tenths of a second relay handoff stamped **`K-7Q` and four digits, the second time that stamp has ever been seen.** Joon wrote down that it is the second time and **did not write down what it means, and said so out loud in daylight.** The stamp stays a fact with no meaning. The voice is a woman's, and Joon refused to be the man who says *do not answer her* and refused to be the man who says *go to her*.

**The man behind the wall after Chapter 50.** He is **thirty-three days in**, and that is Chapter 50's day number and not a standing figure: he came this way on the collapse Saturday, so the count in any later chapter is the chapter's own day number. He is **inside the sealed ground and is not in the seal**, because at four o'clock on the last Thursday on the schedule **he asked the hall not to record him and Elias did not argue**, and the hall's book carries the refusal, the reason, the date, Elias's name and no name. He has never been a future version, an echo, a lender, or a voice on a relay, and he is not one now. He said three new things and gave away nothing else: **it is not warm on his side and has not been since Monday, and he noticed at eleven o'clock in the morning**; **he will not be there on a Thursday after this one**; and **eleven meters is a length and not a number, and put the day on it.** He is unwritten by his own request and is not resolved. **The team's standing rule about the one question they may not ask him is stated on the page in Chapter 47, and the reason for it is named: Halla Dren on the fourteenth day, a shaft they were not to name, a refusal, and a rule against asking twice.**

**The hall is a ward.** On Chapter 45's Saturday the twenty-two decided what the building is: a place people are brought to, maintained by the people in it, with a name against each function. **Emil Baros is on the sheet, by a vote taken one name at a time after Inez said yes and made the room say it — and Lena Mir then refused him the keeper's line and not the sheet**, on the ground that the line is eleven meters from the building and thirteen days at the end of a corridor is not the same as being at the end of it. **He accepted that without arguing and is to be at the loading lip at first light, which is a job he can walk to and not the same job. Do not give him the keeper's line.** **Nadi Bell's movement record was extended to people the hall has never met**, in a past tense, in advance, on her own authority on Monday night. **The paper count of residents is still twenty-two and the building holds twenty-eight**, and the board says both are true and only one is a count.

**The Kestrel Tunnel is sealed** at 18:00 on Chapter 50's Thursday, and the collapse is classified as *catastrophic progressive structural failure attributable to legacy installation condition* — **nine words**. **Sable Arden signed it and is right on the evidence she holds.** Hollis Vane entered it by hand into a file containing his own Finding 1, **accepted sixteen days earlier on the seventeenth**, and he distinguishes the count of persons the site produced from the count of people who were in the ground **without naming a number**, because no number in the file can carry both. The evidence the team kept: eleven boxes out of the building on a handcart on the Thursday of the seal, **plus a copy of the parallel record that went out of the city on a bus on the Sunday morning of the twenty-ninth, four days earlier, by an ordinary road** — duplicate measurements, every patient name in Lena Mir's hand, every survey frame, both of Tomas Vale's nine-line documents, the complete refusal log, and the eleven words with a line saying the meaning is not written down. **Open and not load-bearing: how many of the eleven left the city.** Chapter 50 says *one of those boxes*, which cannot be literally true of a Thursday handcart, so the ledger may be carrying a twelfth object rather than disagreeing by one. **No chapter may print a count of the boxes that are not in the building.**

**Elias released the claim that a successful rescue means bringing her back next**, in his own hand, in a sentence he crossed out twice and did not cross out the third time. **He did not make the promise the wall asked for** — that he would not force a future person to answer when that future person says no — and did not say no, because making it would be forcing a version of himself that had not been asked. **Elias and Mara's working rule: any offer, any boundary, any refusal, and any condition goes to the people who pay for it, in the words it was said in, before it is acted on; and nobody inherits her.** Nothing has happened between them and no future version of either of them has promised them anything.

**The envelope to Nell is still unsent** and the reason is now four words: *the road is sealed.* **The broker's license named the sealed site**, a license that names a sealed site names a wall, nobody told R. Adare anything, and the room above the print shop on Calder Street had eight people in it on Wednesday night and none of them knew why.

**Elias remains at Stage 1: First Witness.** The refuge is batteries, lamps, seam glass, analog maps, a corridor light, a laundry and a feeder residents can cut, under per-circuit consent cards. It is not a shared-load circuit and not a stage. His left hand remains numb and by Chapter 50 it has gone completely, which is why he asks for the promise to be said twice.

## Volume 01 handoff

**Volume 01 is complete at Chapter 50 and the next prose phase is Volume 02, Chapters 51–100, *The Quiet Registry*.** Carry the Chapter 25 reversal forward intact: the lower service run is **11.0 m**, the far twenty meters are not there, the inspection niche at twelve is gone, and **the relay is under the water and past the wall, not at eleven meters and not under the apron.** The service door's three states remain separate and unproven, and the corridor's proven absence is not read into the door. **No loan has been accepted anywhere in Volume 01**; the Door Memory is refused as a route and refused as a loan and its return was performed by the refusal; the Chapter 46 Still Hand was refused, asked one question, and expired in answer to the question. **Halla Dren is not to be asked again.** Do not convert the man behind the wall into a future version, a Branchread subject, or a gift, and do not have the hall quietly add him to a record he declined. Keep the Mercy Array, the First Silence, the Returning Hand, the One-Future Choir, and the One-Future Compact unrevealed. **Finding 2's nine credentials and the Cordell archive are the volume's live institutional thread and are not resolved.** The `K-7Q` prefix stays a fact with no meaning. **Preserve the planned ending in `outline/ending.md`.**
