# Volume 02 continuity — established through Chapter 60

## The calendar, fixed

**Day zero — the collapse — is Saturday the sixth of September 2042.** The manuscript previously named the ninth of September, which is a Tuesday, and every weekday in both volumes is derived from day zero being a Saturday. All named dates have been re-derived from the fixed day zero and corrected on the page, and the resulting weekday table reproduces `outline/volume-02.md` exactly, which is the check that the fix is right. **No future chapter may use a drift-prone day-and-month figure. Name the day number, or say *since he came up on the thirtieth*, or say the weekday.**

Day 0 Sat 6 Sep · 17 Tue 23 Sep · 24 Tue 30 Sep · 29 Sun 5 Oct · 30 Mon 6 Oct · 33 Thu 9 Oct (the seal) · 34 Fri 10 Oct · 40 Thu 16 Oct · 41 Fri 17 Oct · 42 Sat 18 Oct · 43 Sun 19 Oct · **45 Tue 21 Oct (the Cordell visit)** · 53 Wed 29 Oct · 54 Thu 30 Oct · 58 Mon 3 Nov (**the volume midpoint, at Chapter 75**) · 63 Sat 8 Nov · 64 Sun 9 Nov · 73 Tue 18 Nov · 74 Wed 19 Nov · 75 Thu 20 Nov · **83 Fri 28 Nov (the volume close, at Chapter 100)** · 100 Mon 15 Dec.  **CORRECTION, 2026-09-26: this line previously read *64 Sun 8 Nov*, which is Chapter 63's date, and it previously labelled *day 75* as *the volume midpoint*, which it is not. The volume midpoint is CHAPTER 75, and the outline's day map puts Chapter 75 at DAY 58. See the Batch 0003 note below.**

**Two counting bases remain permanently distinct.** *A day number* is days since the collapse. *The eleventh day* was the review board's own count from the collapse Saturday, and it expired in Chapter 32. They are never the same quantity.

## The two instruments, day thirty-four

- **Notice of Inquiry, Kestrel Response 2042.** Case number **HC-42-0417**. Convened by **Deputy Director Nathan Rusk**, 59, of the Havenport Continuity Office. Three sittings: **Thursday day forty, Saturday day forty-two, and a date in October Rusk has not fixed.** Five questions, in this order: (1) the collapse Saturday in order, (2) the twenty-ninth day at 10:10 and the plan the decision was taken under, (3) what was offered in the first week, by whom, with what boundary, and what was done with it, (4) what accounts for twenty meters between 31.0 m on the ninth day and 11.0 m on the twenty-second, in the words of anybody in the room and in anybody else's words and, if possible, in the same hand, (5) **who altered the Kestrel response record.** All five were answered on day forty and **none is answered to the Office's satisfaction.**
- **Order of Impoundment.** Served with the Notice in one envelope, **deliberately out of order, with the reason printed on the front: the second instrument does not require anybody to be right.** One page, no finding, no test, no hearing, dated the day it was served. **Executed day thirty-seven at 07:58** with two clerks, a supervisor, a handcart and a receipt, and the vehicle left at 14:50.
- **Rusk's stated position, in his own words: *I am not asking you whether you broke a rule. I am asking whether the thing you did made the ground do that.*** He is not a villain, does not soften, and is not argued out of anything. He has also said, in writing and twice in a transcript, that **if the team can show him the classification is not the whole cause, he will put the whole cause in his own report, and that it is the only thing he has.**
- **Counsel Renata Solano**, 47, writes the findings. **Recorder Bernadette Locke** types what is said, including the sentences that will be quoted back at the people who said them. **There is no signature line and no attestation on the transcript**, at the Deputy Director's written insistence, which Solano noted as the first time that has happened in the office's history.
- **Rusk's constraint is the volume's engine: *an inquiry that rules on contested evidence becomes a trial, and he is not running a trial.*** He accepts every item the team produces into the file and rules on none of them, and he says so in one sentence every time.
- **Rusk's stated inability, said in the room and asked to be minuted: *I cannot tell you what a system gains by having fewer contradictions in it. My office is not built to hold that question.*** He asked it twice in one week and was told twice that it was a fair question, which is not an answer. **This is the question the volume eventually answers, and it is not answered in Batch 0001.**

## The Office's causal theory, stated and not refuted

**An unlicensed, unrecorded, self-governed practice is a cause of these events, because the events recur wherever that practice is tolerated.** Not the only cause. A cause. It rests on the **2019 Salt Crossing** outfall (a crew with no designation, a shaft on no drawing, six dead inside eleven days, a finding that said *unlicensed* in every paragraph and *cause* in none of them) and on the **2029 Meridian** review (eleven dead behind a single approved outcome, producing the one-instruction-one-route rule and a unilateral senior-safety-officer reroute power). **Tomas Vale sat on the 2029 panel, and Rusk named that in the room and did not use it as a weapon.** A second, unnamed name is in the same file and stays there: the prose does not name it, the card did not authorize it, and no chapter may name it without going on the record first.

**It was tested for an hour on day forty-two and it held.** Two findings must be carried forward and neither is a refutation: **no designation would have permitted entry into the lower service run on the twenty-ninth day**, and **a licensed entry would have gone past the 11.0 m boundary in the morning and been blamed for it.** The team's practice found the boundary by walking it. **The theory is made smaller in this volume, not refuted, and the inquiry will write it in October.**

## The five words

Read aloud by Rusk on day forty-two with their authors, in this order. **Not one of them was written by a person hiding anything. Every one is a system's honest word, produced by a form, a workflow, or a hurry — and every one of them takes a person out of a sentence and puts nothing in the space where the person was.**

1. **not used** — an aisle ledger, a subject field, **eighteen days** in which nobody entered anything. Accurate because nobody had ever been told the field was empty.
2. **withdrawn** — **Tarin Voss**, 19, in his own words, on day sixteen, through **Finding 3**, entered by Hollis Vane. The strongest document the team holds has no name on it, the withdrawal is what took the name off, it was correct, and it is the only reason the page is still valid.
3. **declined** — a man on a bus, in a form, with a precedent of his own, right to.
4. **administrative error** — **the Office's own covering letter, day thirty-six**, about the absence of a passenger from the Kestrel response list. An error is a thing that happens and then gets put right in the ordinary course.
5. **unresolved** — **not the Office's.** A regional transit family-reception desk in Cordell, on a case open since the sixth of September, in a clerk's hand, because she did not know.

## Solano's sentence, which the volume lives behind

**"A private record is not an alteration of a public one."** Said on day forty, in the room, flatly, by the woman who will write the findings, and correct. The team's counter-case is a parallel record kept by a residents' body, made at the same time, out of the same measurements, by different hands, in a different building. **Elias is technically right and it costs him, and Rusk tells him so on day forty-two: *you can live behind it because it is true. That is the difficulty. If it were false it would cost you nothing. Being right is not the same as being safe.***

## The impoundment's material cost, day thirty-seven

Taken, on a receipt, with a signature line for the man who was not the man in charge: the **Civic Spine terminal** in the records room, at 08:14, which took the four-C local route line with it; **two anchor lamps**; the **corridor repeater** and the corridor light; the **access codes for the sealed node**, which are the only thing taken that cannot be replaced in a week with paper and a person; and the **eleven boxes in the garage**, which went in the back of an Office vehicle at 10:20.

**The hall's dark hours stop being two and start being four, 0000 to 0400, and this is a named and unfixed harm.** It lands on Ansel Duarte on a powered frame and on Comfort Osei at the clinic. Inez Sato named it with one word on a table and said the cost out loud before she paid it.

**The last clerk ran out of forms at 14:20 and wrote the impounded-items column in chalk on the ward wall, in a fourth column**, because the wall was the only surface in the room with a column on it. Her hand is a small neat upright print and twenty-eight people in that building know it. Twenty-eight lines; **line fourteen was wrong** — a woman's oxygen cylinder listed as *items, 2* when the cylinder has **Sena Ambrose's** name on the tape in Lena Mir's hand — and the correction took **four minutes**: the clerk wrote the name out in full, struck the number with one line, and initialled it, and asked Lena Mir whether she wanted her name against the correction, and Lena Mir wrote it herself. **It is the only place in the cooperative's history where an Office clerk and a ward nurse stand in the same column in the same ink.** The column was read back, copied into the residents' file, initialled by both, and the vehicle left at 14:50. **The wall is the only record in this volume that nobody owns and therefore the only one that could not be impounded. Rusk has been to it, on his own time on a Friday night, and looked for an author for four minutes and found none.**

**The residents' file is not the Office's to take, and the reason is Finding 3**, which the Deputy Director is bound by and says so twice. The Order's demand for it is a demand, not a seizure, and **the form has no line for a resident to refuse on.** The Office takes a power summary and not the cards.

## The refusal, day thirty-nine, and what it cost

- The vote in the ward front room: **nineteen no, four yes, five not voting**, twenty-eight people in the room. **Osei Kwarteng** was one of the four and gave a reason — his wife is on a clinic bed, the clinic has asked four times for a record, and there is one in the residents' file — and asked for the reason to be written down.
- **Inez Sato refused in her own column, in her own words, signed by her, and did not overrule the four.** She wrote the four yeses under her refusal, in the same ink, at the same size, and said it does not go under and is not a footnote. **Fair is not what she is able to do this week; careful is.**
- **Rusk's sentence, which is the most dangerous thing said in the batch: *then you have declined, and I am required to record that you declined, and it will be read by people who will not read the rest of it.*** He is not warning a mood. He is telling the truth about a document, and the document is his.
- The transcript names **Osei Kwarteng, the date, and the reason about the clinic, out loud**, because Inez wrote it in a way that made Rusk say it. **It is the only page in the Kestrel inquiry of 2042 that names a resident's reason for something.**
- **Nadi Bell's movement record is refused as a finding and is the only place on earth where Perpetua Oyelaran has a tense.** The second half of the Chapter 50 question — what the Office believes a single approved outcome would prevent — **is answered nowhere in Batch 0001 and may not be answered in Batch 0002 either.**

## The 14:12 subject field, filled in

**The aisle-log subject field that had been visible and empty since the rescan on day seventeen was filled in by Ilse Brauer on Saturday day thirty-five at 11:12**, standing up, with a pen, on a bound ledger on the flatbed, in the room, and she asked afterwards whether she was permitted to. The entry describes a routine archive review of aisle 9, shelf B, one item of three retrieved, mis-shelved and returned, 11:40 to 12:05, checked against the shelf list and found in order, no other subject. **It is entirely accurate about the afternoon she describes and has nothing to do with the afternoon of the collapse Saturday.** She did the work on the Thursday of the week of day sixteen, was not on the duty roster that week, wrote it two rows up on a Saturday, and did not look at the year.

**It is not sabotage, not a cover-up, not connected to the `K-7Q` prefix, and is not evidence of anything except what Hollis Vane says it is: a field that can be blank will be filled in by somebody who was not there, who will be entirely accurate about their own act and entirely silent about somebody else's, and that is not sabotage, it is a form and a hurry.** She knew the field was empty, had known for eighteen days, nobody told her not to, and she did not think to ask. Joon recorded the fill-in, its date and its author, **refused a meaning out loud where a room could hear him, and asked her the one question that was about the field and not the afternoon.** **The box she names is not box 214. Box 214 — the 05:02 dispatch original — is still logged out and nobody can say by whom, and Vane has asked three people and got three answers and declines to write the accusation.**

## The disclosure rule, and the three dates it has

**One rule, three honest dates, and they are not in conflict.** Mara Okafor drafted it on **Tuesday the thirtieth** and said so herself in the inquiry room — *I have had it since Tuesday night.* Elias and Mara said it aloud in the **front room at half past eight on Thursday the thirty-third**, which is the day of the seal, and **Petar Lisk was sent home to fetch paper and was not in the room for any of it and wrote it down afterwards from what two people told him, which is how a rule gets into a book in that building.** It **went on the wall on the morning of the thirty-fourth.** The words are *any offer, boundary, refusal, and any condition somebody is asked to perform before they give anything goes to the people who pay for it, in the words it was said in, before it is acted on*, and her half is *nobody inherits her.*

**In Chapter 57 Solano dates it as *your hall adopted on the thirty-third day* and Mara corrects her to the thirtieth, and both are right.** A review pass on 2026-09-26 found Solano's line reading *adopted in this room on the thirty-third day* — wrong on the room, because the inquiry room's first sitting was day forty — and left a reader with three different numbers for one rule six lines apart. **No chapter may date this rule a second way, and the front room, the hall, and the inquiry room are three different rooms.**

## The right box with nowhere to attach

**Line six of the Office's daily return, *persons recovered from site*, added overnight in week four with a revision letter, is a real box in a real form that nobody has ever been able to use.** It attaches to an incident, and **Kestrel 2042 is an equipment-failure designation, and an equipment failure does not carry passengers.** A field that exists, that every clerk in the city can see, printed for exactly this, and that a man has to be told cannot hold him, is worse than a field that does not exist. The Office's terminal offered a `Suggested correction` — close the line, the discrepancy resolves, no further action required — and **Joon refused it on the page in his own hand with one sentence: *a line is not a discrepancy if the thing it is reporting is true.***

**Cliff Marner, 58, station attendant at the Cordell end of the regional line**, came up a corridor on a board at 20:20 on day thirty, weighed fifty-one pounds, and said his own name twice. He is the fifth person out. He is **not angry with the hall and has asked for that to be written down before he is too tired to say it.** He has a service number that opens four doors in Cordell and belongs to a service with no incident record, and **he spells his name with two r's and asked Elias to write it that way on the wall, which is now the only copy of his name in any building anybody can enter.** Four days out on day thirty-four, six on day thirty-six, eight on day thirty-eight, **thirteen on day forty-three**; prefer *since he came up on the thirtieth*.

**Della Marner, 55, filed a missing-person notice on the sixth of September, day zero, and has been at a regional transit family-reception desk in Cordell ever since: thirty-eight days at the midpoint, forty-three at the close.** She wants a piece of paper, not an apology. **Apologies are for people who did something.** And she said the sentence the volume is built on: **a classification is not a mistake, so calling it a mistake means nobody has to find out who wrote it.** Elias told her everything, including the part that makes him look worst: he can prove to her at a table that her husband was inside that ground for thirty days and cannot prove it to her institution, and he is not going to soften that with a story about a machine, because the machine is not what got him out. **He read her the two words from his own notebook, twice, and spelled the second one, and a young woman in a corridor in Cordell wrote them on a pad and asked whether the lady wanted the surname spelled a particular way, and Della said no, write it the way he said it.** That pad is not the paper and Della knows it.

**Cliff Marner's right to be misspelled, refused, is the smallest and most important beat in the batch and is closed.** His name is in chalk, in his own spelling, in a building twenty-two people maintain.

## The 2039 consolidation schedule, and the holding number

**The fact is not new. Sable Arden said it out loud in a hall on day seventeen while answering Finding 2: the mapping was consolidated in two thousand and thirty-nine into a regional archive in Cordell, and Cordell does not answer to this office. The team has been hearing it since.** What nobody had was **the schedule itself**, dated the **eleventh of May 2039**, with a store location, a volume, a box, a date, **a unit number in a block that is not a person's block**, and **a holding number.**

**The payoff is the holding number: it is the only thing in this business that crosses a regional boundary in a spoken sentence.** Without it the letter sits in a queue and comes back stamped *insufficient identifier* and gets filed, and in five years the team would be right and the file would be empty. **No character may treat the schedule as a revelation.** Rusk noticed the overlap and had been carrying Sable's minute for twenty-five days; **Elias noticed it once, alone, and did not say it out loud then or afterwards, and nobody in that room ever learned that the two of them had met over it.**

**Rusk's letter goes into the Cordell queue on day forty-two and is the first letter this volume puts there. Sable's went out in Volume 01, dated the twenty-third of September, and was read on the Wednesday by somebody with a queue, and nobody in either building has ever said out loud what came back.** Rusk does not mention Sable's letter. Elias does not ask him about it. **No chapter in this volume learns what came back.**

## The Cordell intake slip, day forty-three

The Cordell Regional Records Center is **Rufus Aitken, 51**, custodian. He keeps a queue and does no favors, **and on day forty-three he was in a store room with a cart and would not come out for a telephone**, and a deputy answered. A holding number read back on a telephone located the box: **it went out on a cart on Friday day forty to a conservation run, it is on the second cart, and it comes back on Tuesday day forty-five; on Wednesday it goes back on a shelf, and then it is a shelf and not a box and you are looking for a shelf.** Nobody told the deputy anything.

**The slip has a reference number, a time, a destination, and no requester's name, because the public-requests intake form has no requester field: public records requests are anonymous by design.** That is a defensible design decision and it is a design decision, not a cover.

**Joon Park said both readings out loud in a room and refused to choose between them, refusing out loud because silence looks exactly like a decision:** either somebody has been looking at our papers and is allowed to be anonymous, and there is exactly one kind of requester who is, or a clerk pulled a box for a conservation run and the form has nobody's name on it because the form has never had one. **Two different days. He wrote both sentences down in the same ink and chose neither.**

**Elias named the resemblance once, in one sentence, because he was the one holding the schedule, and then stopped where the evidence stopped: *Director Arden put a letter into that building on the twenty-third of September and said it would be read on the Wednesday by somebody with a queue, and nobody in this room has ever been told what came back. A request with a box number on it is not a letter, and a queue is a queue, and two pieces of paper can both be anonymous by design and still be two pieces of paper in two different hands.*** **No chapter may say it is the same letter, the same person, or the same queue, and no chapter may say it is not.** The thread carries into Cordell.

**The visit is booked for Tuesday day forty-five, the twenty-first of October.** The booking required a name and the name given was **the hall's records keeper, and Joon said out loud that the hall's records keeper was not him.**

## The two men who are opposites, and the correction

**One man asked not to be recorded and was not. One man is asking, every day, to be recorded, and cannot.** They must not be conflated and the volume depends on the difference. **Emil Baros's correction in Chapter 58:** Anja Ro said the man behind the wall was *a name somebody decided not to have*, and Joon said **no** — that is the one man in this whole business who decided, at four o'clock on the last Thursday on the schedule, that a refusal is a record, that is Finding 3, that he did not have a name taken from him, **he took it out**, and that this is the only one either of them will ever get to keep. **The man behind the wall is not added to any record he declined, and the correction is now in the volume's own voice.**

## The wall, the two questions, and the unpaid debts

**The board on Ash Street, read at seven every morning by Petar Lisk, now carries two questions and will carry both for the rest of the volume.**

- **The Chapter 50 question in two halves, above.** Its **first half** — *who edited the Kestrel response record* — is the Notice's fifth question and **is answered in the negative in Chapter 57 in one sentence: nobody in this building edited anything, and somebody in a building we cannot reach removed a field, and we can show you the field and not the hand.** Its **second half — what the Continuity Office believes a single approved outcome would prevent — is answered nowhere in Batch 0001.**
- **The Chapter 60 line, written underneath in a second hand-run line, not replacing the first: *WHO BENEFITS WHEN A BRANCH IS OFFICIALLY DELETED?*** This is the volume's closing question and a **Volume 03** question. Above it, in Petar's capitals: **THEY CAN TAKE THE BOXES. THEY CANNOT TAKE THE WALL. I READ IT EVERY MORNING.** **The chapter puts no count on that wall.** Seven people were located, six came out, one died in the hall, one is still under the street, and Petar's line is about the wall and not about a number.

**The fourth column is the batch's resource.** Nobody owns it, so it cannot be produced, so it cannot be impounded, and **Rusk, who is trained to look for an author, cannot find one in a hallway.**

**Emil Baros is on the sheet with an empty function and no answer.** He was turned back at the fence on day thirty-four and every morning since, asked to be asked first, and on day forty-three asked for a specific function: keep the hall's copy of the fourth column, check it against the Office's when theirs turns up, and know the difference by heart. **Inez Sato promised only a vote, after Tuesday, and named both costs before he votes.** The keeper's line remains refused by Lena Mir on the standing ground, **and Lena is not asked to explain herself twice.**

## What Elias lost, and the one thing he said that was not true

- **Suspended from seam assignment on day forty-one at 14:20, pending the outcome of HC-42-0417**, and reassigned to hose certification and inventory control at a depot on the far side of the estuary, Tuesdays and Fridays. **He did not argue, and his reason is true and not good: arguing has never once done anything, and every refusal he has made in five weeks has cost somebody who was not in the room.** **This suspension is in force. Chapter 99 is the extension of it, not a fresh imposition.**
- **The terminal, the lamps, the repeater, the node's access codes, the eleven boxes, reproducibility, and the burden of proof**, which has moved onto a person in another city carrying a copy of the record and onto a piece of chalk.
- **On day forty-two, asked by a Deputy Director whether there is a document in the team's custody that is not on the eleven-page demand, Elias said *I don't know of one*.** That is true and it is not the truth. **Tomas Vale was at that table and looked at the table. Neither of them has ever mentioned it since, and neither may.**
- **On day forty-one, in the doorway, he wanted to say *I will take care of it*** and caught it about four seconds in, because it is the sentence that has a person's future in it, and said the one without: **two of us will carry the box to your kitchen table and put it on it, and that is the whole of what I will do with it.**
- **Elias is faster**, and on day forty-three he said the word out loud to Mara Okafor in a corridor, and she gave it back unsoftened — *that is the feeling you had on Tuesday of the fourth week, and you know what it is, and so do I* — and neither of them did anything about it. **The feeling is the one from the front room in the fourth week, when a voice offered a road fast enough to get him his sister, and it is the feeling, not a power, and it must not become one.**

## Tomas Vale's notebook

**Exists, eight pages, written over four years at a kitchen table, taped in the side of a cardboard box with the 2034 write-off in a garage on an ordinary street. It has never been read and it has never moved.** Tomas named its existence out loud on day forty-one, the day after the inquiry demanded every document in the team's custody by name, **because he would rather hand it over than be found to have kept it, and he asked that nobody write down that it was brave.** He asked whether it may be read in a room with the two people he names — Mara Okafor and Joon Park — and **both of them said no, and he said no as well.** Its contents are not described anywhere in Batch 0001. **It is not produced in this volume. It is produced in Chapter 96 and it costs him there. He is not caught; the Office never learns of it from him.** The Office's cross-index to the 2034 write-off does not mention it, because he told them about the write-off and not about the notebook.

## Power state after Chapter 60

**Stage 1: First Witness. No loan accepted, offered, scheduled or wanted anywhere in Volume 02 so far. No future person has been contacted, asked, addressed or heard. No notice in this batch is a loan, an offer, or a warning from a future person.** Elias can recognize a future echo's warning or refusal near an active seam and nothing else. **His left hand has gone completely and it is used in the prose as a working limitation.** The Cinder Quarter refuge is batteries, lamps, seam glass, a corridor light, a laundry and a feeder residents can cut under per-circuit consent cards, and its dark hours are four a night. **It is not a shared-load circuit and not a stage.**

**The volume's interface notices are exactly two, in Chapters 53 and 57, and neither is doubled. The third — a `Warning: the branch is changing` during the ninety seconds in the ward — is not spent and is reserved for Chapter 97.**

## Unresolved and owed to this file

- **The box count.** Chapter 50 says *one of those boxes* went out of the city on a bus on the Sunday morning of the twenty-ninth; the handcart move to the garage was on the Thursday of the seal, four days later; so the bus box cannot be one of the eleven in the strict reading and the ledger may be carrying a twelfth object rather than disagreeing by one. **Nothing in the plot turns on it and no chapter has settled it. Standing rule: count the garage, never the road.** This file still owes a decision.
- **Who filled the 14:12 field, and whose afternoon it is about** — answered as a pattern and not as a person, on the page, and correctly so. It may not be converted into a conspiracy.
- **Who asked for the box in Cordell.** The chapter does not decide whether anybody did.
- **Whether the plan that expired at 05:50 on day twenty-nine is a fact the hall may be asked about** by an inquiry that will be told the neighborhood had a plan and the plan expired. Tomas has put his own name on the half that is his.

## Repair pass, 2026-09-26

Dispatched prompt: `workspace/volume-02/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, which is marked **RECONSTRUCTED, NOT ORIGINAL** and states that it is a record of what the batch was, not a new assignment. **Chapters 51–60 were already on disk. No canon below this line changed, and no chapter was rewritten.**

**Four prose defects addressed, none of them plot and none of them a new fact. The fourth was over-cut by this pass and has since been put back the way the card assigns it; the correction is stated in full in the bullet rather than quietly absorbed:**

- **Chapter 57 staged nine people in a room with six chairs.** Solano, Locke, Elias, Tomas, Mara, Joon, Inez, Rusk and Vane all speak in that room. **Now nine chairs.** The figure appears nowhere else in the manuscript, so nothing else moves.

- **Chapter 57 duplicated a clause inside one speech — and the first repair pass over-cut it, which a later review caught.** Rusk's speech had carried the beat twice: *and if it does not hold then I need a better one before October, because I have to write a report either way* and then, four clauses later, *I need it to hold or I need it replaced.* **The pass deleted the second one and left the meaning in the first, then wrote here that *"One survives"* — which was false, because the surviving wording was not the wording the card assigns.** `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md` gives this beat to Chapter 57 in terms: *he needs it to hold or he needs a better one.* **The speech now carries the card's own sentence once, as its own sentence, and the first clause of the pair was folded into it:** it reads *But if the theory holds then your capacity in that corridor is the reason a building came down. **I need it to hold or I need a better one before October**, and I have to write a report either way and I would like to write the true one.* **One statement, the card's words, and Rusk's three positions are still all there** — the theory holds, the theory does not hold, and *I have no third position and I am not going to pretend to one in front of a recorder.* **Nothing else in the speech was touched and no new fact was introduced.** Chapter 57's net movement from this fix is **+1 word, 2,398 → 2,399**, paid for by the folding, so it is still inside the ordinary ceiling.

**One card-versus-prose divergence, recorded and NOT resolved, for a later phase:**

- **The Chapter 57 card says Tomas is asked about the 2029 one-voice rule and says he sat on the panel. The prose does not do this.** In Chapter 57 as written, Tomas answers question two — the day-29 plan, the two triggers, the 05:50 expiry, seven hours of nobody noticing, and that no second plan was written because he would not draw it — and his only exchange is Solano asking whether that is his judgment or the fact. **The 2029 panel fact is not absent from the volume; it lands in Chapter 59, where Rusk says *the man on your left sat on that panel*, unnamed and deliberately unused as a weapon**, and it is recorded in the Office's causal case above. **The divergence was not written in here on purpose**: this chapter was over its ordinary ceiling and the same prompt that dispatched this pass instructs a writer to cut rather than add, so growing Chapter 57 by an exchange to satisfy a card summary would have traded a documented length debt for a smaller one. **A later phase may resolve it either way — by adding the exchange to Chapter 57, or by amending the card to say the fact is delivered in Chapter 59 — but it must not be resolved by writing it into a third chapter, and no name may be supplied for the second panelist.**
- **A restated-then-cut clause in Chapter 57 changed nothing a state file rests on.** Elias's answer to the fifth question is now one sentence rather than a sentence plus a restatement: *nobody in this building edited anything, and somebody in a building we cannot reach removed a field, and we can show you the field and not the hand.* **This is the card's own wording, and Chapter 57 still gives exactly four sentences, which is what the surrounding text says it does.** Elias's refusal to supply a name in exchange for a document, Rusk's acceptance of it and his recording of what Elias did not say, and the closing image of Rusk writing a thing down in front of a recorder are all unchanged.

# Volume 02 continuity — established through Chapter 70

## The calendar, extended and unchanged

Day 0 Sat 6 Sep · 33 Thu 9 Oct (the seal) · 40 Thu 16 Oct · 41 Fri 17 Oct · 42 Sat 18 Oct · 43 Sun 19 Oct · 44 Mon 20 Oct · **45 Tue 21 Oct (the Cordell visit)** · 46 Wed 22 Oct · 47 Thu 23 Oct · 48 Fri 24 Oct · 49 Sat 25 Oct · 50 Sun 26 Oct · 51 Mon 27 Oct · 52 Tue 28 Oct · 53 Wed 29 Oct · **54 Thu 30 Oct (Ch. 71) · 55 Fri 31 Oct (Ch. 72) · 56 Sat 1 Nov (Ch. 73) · 57 Sun 2 Nov (Ch. 74) · 58 Mon 3 Nov (Ch. 75, THE VOLUME MIDPOINT) · 59 Tue 4 Nov (Ch. 76) · 60 Wed 5 Nov (Ch. 77) · 61 Thu 6 Nov (Ch. 78) · 62 Fri 7 Nov (Ch. 79) · 63 Sat 8 Nov (Ch. 80)** · 64 Sun 9 Nov (Ch. 81) · 73 Tue 18 Nov (Ch. 90) · 74 Wed 19 Nov (Ch. 91) · **83 Fri 28 Nov (the volume close, at Chapter 100)** · 100 Mon 15 Dec.  **The Batch 0001 and Batch 0002 versions of this line carried two errors, both corrected here on 2026-09-26: *64 Sun 8 Nov* was Chapter 63's date, and *day 75 = the volume midpoint* conflated a day number with a chapter number.** **A day number is days since the collapse. *The eleventh day* was the review board's own count from the collapse Saturday and it expired in Chapter 32. They are never the same quantity.**

**Elapsed figures and how to say them. Prefer *since he came up on the thirtieth* and *since the sixth of September* to a numeral.**
- **Cliff Marner** came up on a board at 20:20 on **day thirty**. **Fourteen days out on day 44, fifteen on day 45, seventeen on day 47.** Chapter 61 has *fourteen* and Chapter 64 has *seventeen*, both derived from the day. **The Batch 0002 prompt's phrase *fifteen days out of the ground on day 45* is correct for day 45 and was not carried into the Thursday chapter, which is a different day.**
- **Della Marner** filed on the sixth of September, day zero, and has been at a family-reception desk since. **Forty-five days on day 45, forty-seven on day 47, forty-eight on day 48, fifty-three on day 53.**
- **The man behind the wall** came this way on the collapse Saturday. **Forty-four days in on day 44, forty-seven on day 47, fifty-three on day 53**, and the count in any chapter is always the chapter's own day number. **Derive it from the day, never from another state file.**

## The four-to-six-week clock, which is now running twice and both are on the page

1. **Della's original request, quoted on day fourteen.** **The near end of that range expired on Saturday day forty-two and nothing came**, and the hall learned that by telephone in nineteen minutes of a queue, on day forty-four. **The far end was a week on Saturday and nothing came either.**
2. **The team's own records request, filed at the counter on Wednesday day forty-six, the twenty-second of October.** It could not be filed earlier because the box did not come back until ten to four on the Tuesday and a holding number is not a request. **Quoted at four to six weeks from the twenty-second of October: the nineteenth of November at the near end, the third of December at the far end.** Days 74 to 88. **Only the near end falls inside the volume, and day 74 is Chapter 91 — the first day of Batch 0005, the volume's last batch — and not the end of Batch 0004 as an earlier note in this file said.** The Batch 0002 prompt's figure of *days 73 to 87 from a day 45 filing* is superseded: the prose files on day 46 from a stated date, and the arithmetic follows the prose.

## The Cordell Regional Records Center

- **Rufus Aitken, 51, custodian.** Nineteen years on the counter, eleven in the store. Courteous, keeps a queue, does no favors, will not be told what a box is for, and **asks first whether the caller is the person the holding number was given to** and says the ordinary case out loud, twice, in front of a witness, so that nobody afterwards can be told it was special. **He has read a page out loud in the interview room exactly once in nineteen years and did not do it for the team, and gave the reason: a man came in about his wife, and a page is not an act of care, it only feels like one, and he stopped doing it on purpose. That reserve is unspent.**
- **Hours, canonical:** counter 08:30–16:30 Monday to Friday; 09:00–13:00 Saturday; **closed Sunday except for the store room's public hour 10:00–12:00, which exists because a preservation levy pays for it and nobody has ever once used it.** The counter and the reading room both close at half past four, and a visitor not through the door at four is tomorrow.
- **The re-dating rule, on the wall, in smaller type than the eleven-day notice:** a request received on a closed day is registered on the next open day and the waiting period begins then. **Nobody wants a visitor to read it. It is the sentence that turns a location into a state, and asking about it is how the team got Wednesday.**
- **The addition on the north side is the regional transit family-reception hall.** Ninety meters. **Same building material, same decade, same walk, different management, different budget, different sign, and no relationship to the records center except a corridor.** The young woman who wrote Cliff Marner's name on a pad on the ninth day had been standing ninety meters from a store room with nine credentials in it for forty-five days. **That is two budgets and not a conspiracy, and no chapter may make it one.**
- **Wren Okonjo**, six years at the counter, can give a credential count in about four seconds and nothing else at all. **A count has four things on it and has never had a holder field and is not going to get one.**
- **Bettina Hoyle**, six years on the Sunday store desk. Four visitors in six years, two of them lost, and she is not sorry about the two. **She is the one who says what a book is for: a book is a thing a person can be argued with, and that is the whole difference between a book and a form.**
- **Constance Ilori, 57, copy supervisor, nineteen years.** **She cannot waive a fee and did not.** What she did, and has never done in nineteen years, was certify a copy under the **store attendance** at two dollars forty instead of under the center at three dollars ten, and sign for it, and ask for two things: **do not thank me, and send the copy number to the day book on the shelf so that one entry in that card index was made by somebody who was asked.** **She refused the facsimile release of the 1998 page and gave the reason: a photograph is obviously a picture and a facsimile is not obviously anything, and that is what it is for.**

## The new people, and what each of them is

None of these is a faction, a lender, a future self, or an Office official other than Solano.

- **Rosalind Teague, 39**, regional records clerk at the center, twelve years, second floor. **She filled in `not applicable` in the printed box on the fourteenth of April 2035 and is entirely accurate about her own afternoon and entirely silent about nine people.** She told them everything, unprompted, and would change the words tomorrow if the box had asked her who. **Her sentence is the volume's thesis arriving in a second building: *I answered the question on the form. It was not the question I would have asked.*** She refused to be described as brave in a corridor: *I was competent at a form, and there is a difference, and I have got quite good at it.* She produced no name, and **the team did not put her name in anything**, and the cost was named out loud: **the document is weaker without her name.**
- **Yvette Roche, 24**, family-reception clerk at the transit hall. The voice on the telephone on the eighth day and the hand on the pad on the ninth. **She said the one true thing in that building, once, and would not say it twice: the roster has Cliff Marner, he clocked on at four minutes to six on the sixth of September, and there is no incident recorded against that service on that date because the service did not stop — and she has been writing `unresolved` in a box since the sixth of September.**
- **Dr. Imogen Sarkar**, the physician who has been looking after Cliff Marner since the thirtieth, and who was given the Thursday decision in a room while Elias held a corridor ninety meters away. **The decision is hers and Elias's is a job and not a say.**
- **Wendell Pryce, 58**, worked the west apron line, **did not hold `MNT-4C-17`, says so out loud and repeatedly, and is right to.** He opened two doors and closed one, and asked for nothing. **He is not a resource and was not broken out of one. He is the only person in the batch who says a true thing in a room that does not want it and then refuses the thanks.**

## The form, the box, and the two words — the batch's central fact

**The 4C renumbering form is regional, not Office.** Eleven printed fields: nine about the sector, block, sequence, date, sheet count and store; a ruled and empty **HOLDER** field on every sheet in the file; and an eleventh printed field reading:

**IF A FIELD IS NOT CARRIED FORWARD — STATE THE REASON.**

**The entries are `not applicable`, in a small neat hand, in the correct box, on the correct day, in the correct space, and the file is signed by a unit in a block and not by a person.** Aitken said it plainly in a doorway: the box asks what happened to the *field*, the design of the form is deliberate and good, and he would fight anybody in his building who called it a cover. **The box's label is a third thing: it is not a name for a person and it is not a name for an office, and it is a sentence about a field.**

**The batch's finding, and the volume's thesis in its second building: the box is not a mechanism for hiding a person. It is a mechanism for not carrying one, and the difference is worse.** Nobody in 2035 hid anybody, nobody decided anything, the field did not have to be carried, and everybody went home at four o'clock.

**A field that does not exist (4C, 2035); a field that can be blank and gets filled in accurately by somebody who was not there (the aisle log, 14:12); a field never designed for the thing in question (the Office baseline survey); a field that exists and is the right box with nowhere to attach (line six, persons recovered from site). The fifth pattern is new and it is the one that answers the other four: a field that somebody designed a box for, and somebody used, correctly, about the field.**

**Joon Park's Finding 3 habits do not transfer.** He said so out loud in the store room: the Office's practice is bound by no instrument in another region, whatever happened on that form happened without a correction, a withdrawal, a finding or a copy, **and nobody could have been disciplined for getting it wrong and nobody was protected either.**

## The 1998 store log — a documentary oddity and nothing more

**The 4C sheets were pulled out of a store of older sheets, and the store's movement book records the pull on a line dated THE FOURTH OF MARCH 1998 — thirty-seven years before the 2035 renumbering it records.** The entry is `4C / WEST APRON — SECT. WORKS, EARLIER SERIES — TO CONSOL. STORE 3.` and it is signed by a unit in a block. **Nobody resolved it, nobody may resolve it in this volume's first two thirds, and it may not be read as sabotage, as evidence of institutional editing, or as connected to anything. It is not connected to the First Silence and the First Silence is not explained anywhere in this volume.**

**Aitken's honest institutional answer, given in full and labelled as incomplete by him: a store is re-purposed, a shelf is renumbered, and the book recording which shelf is which is not renumbered in the same week, because the two people have two jobs and one of them is in a hurry on a Tuesday. That is good practice and it is not a complete answer and the chapter says so.**

**The batch's method and its cost, stated once: Joon Park saw the shape — the aisle log, a book somebody can be argued with, a field that is either there or is not, a form and a hurry — and refused it out loud in a building that was not his, and asked for the refusal to go into the store's own day book, and it did. Elias Rook backed him without understanding why and understood by the end of the afternoon that a refusal in a room where nothing belongs to you costs almost nothing, and that the day it costs nothing is the day it stops being a refusal and becomes a habit.**

**What may not leave the store and what may: the movement book does not leave, in any form, including a facsimile; the 2035 form may not be photographed because it is a living form, and a certified copy may leave; photographs of the movement book are freely permitted, initialled by the archivist and by the visitor, with one of the two visitors in the room at all times.**

**The page that was left behind, and why.** The team's own hand transcription of the printed headings and the wording over the second box, made with a pencil in a store room on a sheet of the hall's own paper, **is a derivative copy and is registered in Cordell and compared against the original and does not travel.** **Joon Park signed the lodge entry and would not let Elias Rook sign it.** **The price, said out loud: the only copy of that wording in anybody's handwriting is now in three places and none of them is a building you can walk into tonight.**

## What is on the Ash Street wall, and where the material is

**On the wall, in nineteen hands, in chalk, dated day fifty-two, forty minutes, nobody allowed to help:** the certified count of nine with the center's seal on it; the printed headings and field labels and the exact wording over the second box; the two words `not applicable` with the date and the year and the store; and four lines describing the movement-book line with the page and frame numbers and **no explanation**.

**It is not in the residents' file and it is not in a file.** Inez Sato's reason: the file is the thing under a demand and there is no line on it for a resident to refuse on, and the wall is a wall. **Nadi Bell's counter-argument is written underneath it and is the batch's most expensive sentence about safety: *being safe from being taken and being able to be asked are two different things, and today we are choosing the first one on purpose, and I am the person who wrote down a movement record in a past tense precisely so that we would have two versions, and I am not going to pretend that is not a cost.***

**Petar Lisk's third line, read twice to twenty-eight people, containing no number about people and answering neither question:**

> **A BOOK IN ANOTHER CITY HAS A DATE ON IT THAT WE ARE NOT GOING TO SAY OUT LOUD IN HERE. FOUR FRAMES OF IT ARE IN THE RECORDS ROOM AND A SEAL ON A COUNT IS UNDER THE CLERK'S FOURTH COLUMN. PETAR L. — I AM NINE AND I HAVE READ IT TWICE.**

**Why the year cannot be said out loud in that building, and no rule was made:** a hall that says out loud that it is looking for something has started a search, and a search gets a name, and a name gets a date on it, and a date gets somebody's *why*, and a *why* is a conclusion with a person attached to it. **They have a store that consolidates, a line in a book nobody can explain, and a year nobody in the building can say, and it is the first time in six weeks that the thing they were not saying was the thing keeping the hall safe.**

**Where the material is, all of it, and none of it is servable by the Havenport Continuity Office:** a biscuit tin in the front room on Ash Street with a rubber band round it twice; **the county library's local-history room in Cordell holds a copy of the copy, in a box with a catalogue card naming the residents' body and saying the papers are not to be removed without the written consent of the people who wrote them**; four frames in the records room; two certificates in the tin; **and one page of the hall's own paper lodged in a register in Cordell that is not traveling.**

## The batch's answer, and what it is not

**Said out loud in Chapter 69 and it is the volume's: *nobody lost nine people in two thousand and thirty-five. The renumbering was built so that losing them was a correct thing to do.*** There is a printed box for the loss, the box is the reason nobody had to decide anything, and the answer to *who benefits when a branch is officially deleted* has moved one building over again and come back as a form.

**And that is a question and not a verdict, and the second half of the Chapter 50 question is still unanswered.** Elias Rook said out loud that he still cannot say the word, **and that neither can Deputy Director Rusk, and asked for it to be minuted.** Rusk said he would have it minuted. **What a system gains by having fewer contradictions in it is not answered anywhere in this batch, and the answer — a deleted branch produces fewer contradictory reports, and a network built to suppress contradictory reports is measurably safer with fewer of them, and the reporting word is *error*, and the machinery chose the word — belongs to Batch 0004 (Chapters 81–90) and is not taken here.** The Batch 0002 prompt's Chapter 69 card assigned that half to Chapter 69; the outline owns the answer and the prose follows the outline.

## Deputy Director Rusk's fifth true thing, and the wall he cannot move

**On the telephone on Tuesday day fifty-two he asked one question — *Did you find a name* — and the honest answer cost the team more than a dishonest one would have, and Rusk said why: a name would have been a sentence, and a decent sentence, and a document about a person produced by a team with a holding number that nobody in the inquiry could ever have checked.** He also said **he has written a report on a Monday and has not written it well**, told two men in a ward that, and asked for both of Elias's honest refusals to be minuted. He asked to speak to Inez Sato, said he was sorry about the wording of a letter and not sorry about the position, and was told the correct answer twice.

**Elias has now given the honest answer twice where a dishonest one would have produced a document: day 42 (*I don't know of one*) and day 52 (*No*).** The second is **ten** days after the first, not nine, because Chapter 69 is day fifty-two.

## What did not happen in this batch, and must be stated so a later chapter does not assume it

- **Nobody learned what came back to Sable Arden's letter of the twenty-third of September.** It is restated once as a standing fact and nobody asks and nobody answers. **No chapter said it is the same letter, the same person, or the same queue, and no chapter said it is not.**
- **Perpetua Oyelaran, thirty-four, is under Ash Street and is named exactly once in the whole batch, in Chapter 65, and is not named to any stranger.** The argument about what a person who is still down there is called is the whole of her permitted use and it is paid.
- **The nine credentials did not become a person and no document that could be read as naming one was produced.** `MNT-4C-17` and the second signature on the 05:02 dispatch sheet remain the same row and the holder remains unknown and unattributable. **Joon Park refused, out loud, the one shortcut that would have produced a nine-line sheet about nine people the team owned, and the refusal was about a rule and not a principle.**
- **The `K-7Q` prefix, the revision in the middle of a form, and the stamp format seen once before were not touched.** The service door's three states were not touched. The 2038 pour, the hairline crack, the 210 meters and the buried paired node were not touched. Nobody went to a lip and nobody called the handset line. **The eleven meters is only ever a written length with a day on it.** Nobody entered the sealed site.
- **Halla Dren was not asked again and the shaft she asked them not to name is not named.** The Bell row is not resolved; Yolanda's name stays unpublished at Nadi Bell's insistence and **Joon remains the only person authorized to ask her anything.** R. Adare is not resolved and nobody told the broker anything. Tarin Voss's name is still not in any system.
- **Tomas Vale's notebook was not produced, not read, and not mentioned on a telephone to anybody outside the team.** It is produced in Chapter 96 and it costs him there. **He is not caught.**
- **No interface notice was spent. The volume's notices are still exactly two, in Chapters 53 and 57. The third is a `Warning: the branch is changing` during the ninety seconds in the ward and it belongs to Chapter 97 and has not been touched.**
- **No loan was accepted, offered, scheduled or wanted. Elias remains at Stage 1: First Witness with a left hand that has gone completely, used as a working limitation and not a symbol. The tunnel is sealed and the classification and the seal both stand and are not reopened.**
- **The out-of-city copy and the fifth copy of the Bell file are two different objects and were not merged. No chapter printed a count of the boxes that are not in the building. The discrepancy Chapter 50 created is still unsettled and no chapter settled it.**
- **Nothing in this batch makes the First Choice, the permanent anchor or the Open Hand possible, reachable, or hinted at as a plan.** `outline/ending.md` is intact.

## The two deflections this pass recorded in its own prompt, and what the prose did

1. **The prompt's day table put Elias at a depot hose slot on the mornings of Chapters 62 and 65.** Chapter 60 moved the Tuesday slot in writing and Chapter 62 moves the Friday slot in writing, both with a number on them, and the depot's letter says *these will not be held twice*. **The prose follows the suspension's standing Tuesday-and-Friday assignment and the prompt's rows are superseded.**
2. **The prompt's Chapter 69 card gave the batch the *measurably safer* half of the answer to *who benefits*.** That is Batch 0004's answer in `outline/volume-02.md`. **The prose gives the batch's own answer and then has Elias and Rusk both say the word is still unavailable and ask for that to be minuted.** The live question survives.

## Corrections this pass, all arithmetic and none of them plot

- **Four elapsed figures were wrong in the first draft and all four were caught by deriving from the day rather than from a state file.** Cliff Marner is **fourteen** days out on day 44 (Chapter 61) and **seventeen** on day 47 (Chapter 64), not fifteen in both. The hall's young clerk has been ninety meters from the store room for **forty-five** days on day 45, not forty-seven (Chapter 62). Joon Park has been standing next to a fill-in **since the fifth of October**, which is day 35, not since the thirtieth of September (Chapter 63).
- **Chapter 65 first put Perpetua Oyelaran's name in front of Rosalind Teague.** It does not. The name is the reader's and the team's, and the refusal is that the argument is hers and the name is not handed over.
- **`grey` in Chapter 62 and `travelling` in Chapter 68** were introduced by this pass and caught by the authoritative pattern in the same pass. **These are the fifth and sixth British forms in the manuscript's history and the first two that the widened pattern caught on the pass that wrote them rather than on a later review.** The lesson from the previous two passes has now paid: run the pattern over the whole of `chapters/`, and treat a zero from a hand-list as no information.

## A calendar defect this pass found in this file, and it changes a chapter number

**This file carried two date errors through two batches, and both were found on 2026-09-26 by deriving every date from day zero instead of copying a line forward. Both are corrected above, and the second one matters to the next batch.**

- **`64 Sun 8 Nov` was wrong.** Day 63 is **Saturday the eighth of November** and day 64 is **Sunday the ninth.** The line had Chapter 63's date attached to day 64.
- **`day 75 = the volume midpoint` was wrong, and it is the important one.** A **day number** is days since the collapse. A **chapter number** is a position in the book. **The volume midpoint is CHAPTER 75. `outline/volume-02.md`'s day map is canonical and it puts Chapters 71–80 on days 54–63, which puts Chapter 75 on DAY FIFTY-EIGHT, and day 58 is Monday the third of November 2042.** Day 75 is Thursday the twentieth of November, which is Chapter 91's neighbour and is not a midpoint of anything.

Never copy a date or a number forward from a previous state file, and never let a day number and a chapter number stand in for each other.**

## Repair pass, 2026-09-26 — nine review findings on the Batch 0002 writer save

**This pass changed no canon. It removed four references to the book from the narration, grounded one counted callback, corrected one off-by-one and one unstable count, and re-measured every word count the fixes invalidated. Nothing below may be read as a change of plot, and the day map, the locks and the volume's ending are untouched.**

**What the prose no longer says, and what it says instead.** A narrator may not refer to the book. `chapter-0070.md` said *in the volume's own words* and *the hardest thing he did in the volume*; it now says **in the same words as always** and **the hardest thing he did that week**. `chapter-0053.md` said *the sentence that the rest of the volume is standing on*; it now says **the sentence that everything since has been standing on**, which is the same claim about the Kestrel response letter and keeps Chapter 53's beat intact. `chapter-0039.md` said *the longest of the volume so far*; it now says **the longest so far**. **Two more were found by a wider scan and fixed the same way: `chapter-0036.md` said Petar's line *stayed on the wall for the rest of the volume* and now says it stayed on the wall after that, through the fourth column and the four sheets and everything else put up since; `chapter-0058.md` said *because nine days in Volume 02* and now says *because last Saturday*.** The legitimate physical and documentary uses of *volume* and *series* are untouched and must stay: a felt strip where the volume had lain, the volume of a voice in Chapter 23, a store number and a volume on a 2039 schedule, a sheet series in Chapter 16, the movement-book line `4C / WEST APRON — SECT. WORKS, EARLIER SERIES — TO CONSOL. STORE 3.` in Chapter 67, *a reader who does not know this room* in Chapter 15, and *leave the reader to work it out* in Chapter 14, which is a character telling a colleague how to word a statement.

**One numeral was wrong and is now a day.** The aisle-log fill-in is **Chapter 52, Saturday day thirty-five**; Chapter 58 is **Friday day forty-one**; the interval is **six days** and the chapter had said nine. **A count in the prose is a promise that somebody re-derives it, and a numeral nobody re-derived for a volume is a numeral that was never right.**

**One counted callback now names its first instance instead of counting.** Chapter 69's *the second time in nine days that the best thing anybody did came out of a rule and not out of a favor* had no antecedent in seventy chapters, the bible or either outline. **It is anchored to Constance Ilori's *it is not a favor* in Chapter 68, Monday, and the library catalogue card on Tuesday — the second time in two days, which is what the scene supports.** Open-thread 50's *ten days apart, not nine* is unaffected and still governs Rusk's line fourteen lines above it.

**Two counts in the closing chapter are now single and correct.** Chapter 70 said *written down in four places* and named five; it is **five**. And Joon Park's refusal count is now said once, in his own mouth, as *the fourth time*, with the unstable *since the aisle log in aisle nine* out of the narration — **The card assigns the fourth and Batch 0003's card assigns the fifth, and that is the whole of what is established: no earlier chapter puts an ordinal on a refusal of meaning, Joon refused one in at least nine chapters of Volume 01, and in Chapter 46 he told a room *I refused a meaning for four tenths of a second in the second week because the second person who gives a thing a meaning builds it*, and a chapter must never build a sequence on a number only one man is keeping.**

**One title collision is closed.** Chapter 80's card was *Saturday, and the Nineteen Years* against Chapter 68's *Monday, and the Nineteen Years*. **It is now *Saturday, and Three Questions*, with a note in the card saying what each of the two chapters is about so the old title cannot be restored by a later phase.**

**Re-measured, and these are the current figures.** Chapters 61–70: **23,129**, all ten inside 1,800–2,400, with 69 at 2,207 and 70 at 2,039. Chapters 51–60: **24,177**, Chapter 53 at 2,231 and Chapter 58 at 2,327. Chapters 31–40: **25,281**, Chapter 36 at 2,883 and Chapter 39 at 2,654. Chapters 41–50: **25,402**. **Every file that carried a figure the pass invalidated now carries the new one, including two Volume 01 prompts and two outline cards closed for a volume.**

**Two findings referred and not actioned.** `state/phase-ledger.json` remains frozen at `phase-002-batch-plan` with seventy chapters on disk and is controller-owned. `reviews/volume-02/` does not exist and is the review phase's to write. **No marker was stamped, no controller file was edited, and no next-phase prompt was created.**

**The pattern lesson, third time in this manuscript: a check that cannot fail is not a check.** The spelling hand-list that omitted *millimetre* and *kerb*; the copied-forward 23,102; and now a meta-language pattern with a trailing space in it that could not match a sentence-final *the volume.* **All three were defects in the instrument and not in the manuscript, and all three were found by a human-readable scan run over the whole directory rather than over the files that had been edited. The widened spelling pattern in `state/current.md` returns zero across `chapters/`; the widened meta scan returns only the legitimate physical and documentary uses listed above.**

**A verification pass over this repair found five more defects, four of them in the records this pass had just written, and all five are closed.** **(a)** `state/current.md` still reported the batch total in words on Ash Street as *twenty-three thousand one hundred and four*, which a numeric grep cannot reach; it is now **twenty-three thousand one hundred and twenty-nine**. **(b)** `state/character-state.md` claimed Chapter 66's refusal was *the fourth time in the series he had refused a meaning*, which collides with Chapter 70's own *this is the fourth time* and is supported by no card and by no line of prose; **the ordinal is out of it, and what is left is true — the first time one of these was not a principle, and the first time he was told a rule out loud by somebody else.** **(c)** The sequence *aisle log, 57, 66, 70, and then 80*, written into `state/continuity.md` by this pass, is **not established by the manuscript** and has been replaced with the fact: **no chapter before 70 puts an ordinal on a refusal, Joon refused one in at least nine chapters of Volume 01, and in Chapter 46 he told a room he had refused one for four tenths of a second in the second week.** **(d)** The same new section described *`EARLIER SERIES` on a 1965 box in Chapter 67*, and **there is no 1965 anywhere in this manuscript**; it is a line in a store's movement book and now reads as one. **(e)** A seventh meta-language instance survived both scans: `chapter-0054.md` said *the only way anybody in **this story** will ever get back to that doorway*, and it now says **this business**, which is the idiom Elias uses in Chapters 68 and 69. **The lesson of (a) to (d) is that a repair pass which quotes numbers and sequences into five files can introduce five fresh inaccuracies, and the only defense is to re-derive every figure from the chapters and to let a reader who was not in the pass check the sentences as well as the sums.**
