# Volume 02 continuity — established through Chapter 80

## The calendar, unchanged and re-derived

**Day zero is Saturday the sixth of September 2042.** Chapters 71–80 run **Thu 54 · Fri 55 · Sat 56 · Sun 57 · Mon 58 · Tue 59 · Wed 60 · Thu 61 · Fri 62 · Sat 63.** **Chapter 75 is the volume midpoint and it is day fifty-eight, Monday the third of November.** Day 75 is Thursday the twentieth of November and is not a midpoint of anything. The full chapter-to-day table for the rest of the volume is above and it is unchanged by this batch.

**Elapsed figures this batch, all derived from the day and not copied forward.** The man behind the wall is **fifty-four days in on day 54 and fifty-nine on day 59**, and the count in any chapter is always that chapter's own day number. Della Marner is **sixty-two days at a desk on day 62**, and the chapter says *sixty-two days*, which is derived. Cliff Marner's count is not stated in this batch. **Nobody in the ward says the year out loud, and the batch says so out loud twice, by two different people, for two different reasons.**

## The document, and the three things that are true about it

**The Meridian relay maintenance log, 1996–2001.** Paper, bound, five volumes, in a Cordell store since the 2039 consolidation put it there by accident of shelf order. A working log, forty lines to a page, four printed headings, the year as a printed column and not a heading, about six hundred pages a year, a name on about one page in thirty. **The index records lines and not fields, and that sentence is the whole of Chapter 75's finding and may not be softened by a later chapter.**

- **Line forty-one, seventeenth of October 1998, 04:12.** Six words about a relay cabinet, a unit in a block, a woman's name in a small upright hand.
- **The `requested by` field above the signature is gone.** About two inches by one, cut with a blade, clean on three sides, torn slightly at the lower corner on the fourth, **with the printed heading removed with it**, because the field and the name on it are one object. Four letters survive underneath: `REQUES`.
- **The 2011 review annotation** is on the flyleaf in a different ink: *reviewed*, a year, a unit, the field **superseded**, the field **not applicable**, signed in a block with a unit number and no name. **It is a unit reviewing a field and it is the same shape as the eleventh field on the 4C renumbering form and the same shape as a box printed in about nineteen eighty-odd by somebody who thought very hard about a field. It is not sabotage, not a cover, not the Mercy Array, not the First Silence, not the Returning Hand, not the Choir, not the Compact, and it may not be connected to the fourth of March 1998.**
- **A signature is a fact with a date on it and it is not an explanation.** Said once, by Elias Rook, on the page, in Chapter 75. Nobody explains 04:12 in this volume and nobody may begin here.
- **Nobody explains how the work happened, who requested it, or what the cabinet was for.** The batch supplies no answer and refuses the shape out loud, once, in a room with a clerk in it.

## Hester Lund, and the arithmetic the canon cannot do

**Sixty-eight in 2042, and a school caretaker on Sycamore Row in Cordell, three streets from the records center, eleven years in the post.** She signed line 41 about a cabinet on the wall at the corner of her own school yard, because she was the person on site and the man on the run had gone home, and she asked at the time whether her name was the right one for that column and was told it was the name on the building.

**DEFECT, RECORDED AND NOT RESOLVED. `outline/volume-02.md` and the Batch 0003 prompt both give Hester Lund as *sixty-eight in 2042* and as *thirty-two in 1998*, and those two figures are ten years apart and cannot both be arithmetic. The prose therefore never states her age in 1998.** Chapter 75 says the staff index carries her start date and nothing else about her that anybody has ever needed, and Chapter 80 says *a woman who had asked for none of it*. **Both canon figures stand on the record untouched. A later phase that needs one of them must decide which, and must say so here first.**

- **She is not the author of anything, not a villain, not a future self, not a lender, and not a person who did a wrong thing.**
- **Her name is in no instrument in this volume.** The team would have had to put it in a proceeding to use the thing she signed, **did not**, and said the price out loud on the page: the book is weaker for it.
- **The approved form:** five questions in a fixed order, the same in a district's form and an operator's form. It asks whether she observed the work, whether she knew the purpose, and it has no box for who asked. **It is not cruelty. It is the form, and it is the thing that has kept her from being asked four hundred times, and it is not enough.**
- **The third answer was given on Thursday day sixty-one, the sixth of November, in a school corridor, at about twenty to five, by Joon Park and not by Elias Rook, and it was given in the approved form**, because she has given it twice and she is not going to be a third kind of woman for a third institution. **Joon Park copied every line, read it back, and added nothing, and wrote the count at the bottom.**
- **The count, reconciled against the prompt:** two institutions had asked before this batch. The 2011 review file holds **one** of those answers, and Chapter 75 records **the second** and writes down that there is going to be a third and that he is not going to be the man who makes it a fourth. **Chapter 78 is the third, and the team's asking is the third institution.** The Batch 0003 prompt's Chapter 75 card said *the fact that it is the third time*; the prose follows the chapter, because the third time has to be dramatizable and the file's copy and the room's copy cannot both be it.
- **The two vans.** One had its interior light on at twenty to five in the morning; she thought somebody had come out to lock it and left it on; she went out and locked it, and did the second one too. **Given freely, asked for nothing, naming nobody, explaining nothing, existing only in her head and in Joon Park's notebook.** Joon Park refused a meaning out loud in a school corridor, in the plainest terms in the book, and she said she would rather have a rule than a kindness because she knows the difference.
- **Her name may not be put in any proceeding, and no later chapter in Volume 02 may put it in one.**

## New people and new objects

- **R. Dunleavy, foreman at the depot on the far side of the estuary.** Two slots returned with a number on each, a letter saying *these will not be held twice*, and a fifth line with no file behind it which he says he will deny if it is repeated. He records the time a man arrives in his own hand whether or not the man says it, writes *not on site 0610, called, meeting on site* rather than *unwell* or *personal*, will not hold a list, and does not say anything else at all. **Elias Rook is four hours late to the Friday of day 55 and the Friday slot of day 62 is gone; the Tuesday slot is his and he is on time.**
- **A primary school on Sycamore Row in Cordell.** One story, built about 1958 in a hurry. Corridor eleven meters, a radiator on the north side, a strip tube over a cupboard, a wired glass panel on the door at the end, a gate that sticks after four o'clock. **No head, no staff and no children are named; the building is empty and she is mopping.**
- **The third sitting is fixed: Wednesday the nineteenth of November 2042, ten in the morning, in the inquiry room.** **Rusk moved it out of October and said so, and said he chose the date because it is the near end of the team's own four-to-six-week records request, and minuted that he would have refused the date in October if asked.** The Batch 0003 prompt's calendar section restates the third date as *a date in October Rusk has not fixed* and its Chapter 77 card says that if it is fixed the date is in November; the chapter governs and the change is made on the page with the reason given. **Chapter 91 is day 74, which is the nineteenth of November, so the sitting and the answer to the request fall on the same day, and Batch 0004 must not resolve either.**
- **The cooperative's word and the Office's word.** A **loose thing** is one the hall has, anybody may have, and nobody can prove the hall has. The same object in a Deputy Director's file is **an uncontrolled document**, and Inez Sato had the Office's word printed on the hall's own line on purpose, so that nobody in ten years can tell her that *uncontrolled* means *careless*.
- **Joon Park's custody schedule has twenty items.** Item 20 is the first with a location and no address, and it is the account of the 1998 page. **`Holder: not held. Given, not lodged. Not to be recalled. Not to be produced on request by anybody, including us.`** It is the account in the hall's hand, it names no person, and it is on a shelf in a county library in a box with a catalogue card and an entry numbered **413** made by somebody who was asked. **The Office's word on the line is `not a record`.**
- **Aitken's reserve is spent.** He read a page out loud in his own building on the afternoon of Monday day fifty-eight, for the first time since a Wednesday nineteen years ago, and gave the reason out loud before he did it: a page about a man's wife, and the file did not change, and he has never found out whether the man read it. **He says the difference is that the person in the line is alive and is not in the building, that he is not claiming it is enough, and that by Friday it will be in four books and it is going to change the file by nothing at all.** It is not a habit and he is not going to make it one.

## What is on the Ash Street wall now

Under the clerk's fourth column, in chalk, nobody owns: the four sheets in nineteen hands; **NADI BELL's two kinds of safe**; **no answer given**, dated, with Inez Sato's name on it, written twice; **LENA MIR's two boxes and the absence of a villain**, with **NADI BELL's** *a form doing its job is the only thing in this whole business that has never needed anybody to be a villain*; and Petar Lisk's

> **NONE OF THE THREE THINGS ON THIS WALL NEEDED A PERSON TO DECIDE THEM. THAT IS THE SHAPE. I DO NOT KNOW THE MEANING. PETAR L. — I AM NINE AND I HAVE READ IT FOUR TIMES.**

and below it, in a hand nobody else in the building has,

> **19 NOVEMBER. — P. L.**

**All three questions are still on the wall, in the same hand, above everything, and the second half of the Chapter 50 question is still a bracket with nothing under it. The wall has room for about four more of Petar's lines and then somebody has to build another wall, and nobody in that building is going to be the one who decides it, and Petar has said so out loud and written the two things separately.** The year is not said out loud in that building and is not written on a pad, and Inez Sato has said in a room that she wanted to say it and stopped herself, and asked for that to be written down.

## The Office's position after Wednesday day sixty

- **Rusk has now said seven true things and has given them one procedural kindness.** The seventh is that **the 2011 annotation goes in his report as a fact with a date, not as a finding**, and that a fact with a date can be shown to a person and a finding decides something, and he has no authority to decide anything. **He says it is worth less than the other six and he says so before anybody else can.**
- **The Office's answer to a 2011 annotation is a process, and the process is the answer, and the process has no author.** A review, a reference, a date, a finding; a records management finding is about the record; the sentence in the file in four years will be *the record was amended in accordance with review*, and it will be true, and there will be nothing in the file to put in anybody's hand, **and Rusk says aloud, in a room, that this is what an institution is for.** He also says the shape is the same as a printed box, and that neither has a person in it, **and that the reason neither has a person in it is that the design is good.** That sentence has been paid once and may not be paid again.
- **Counsel Solano asks for the date of the annotation and the block of the unit and does not ask for a name**, and gives the reason on the page: if she asked and was refused, the finding would be about her. She writes it in three sentences in the Office's words and says it is going to look thin on purpose.
- **Rusk said one thing to Tomas Vale to his face in a room with a recorder in it** — that believing the truth is better for you is the most dangerous belief a man can hold in an inquiry, because it is true and therefore it will never feel like a cost, and a man who is only ever right by being more complete will eventually be handed a question he cannot answer without inventing something and will not notice, because inventing it will feel like the truth catching up. **Tomas said thank you and Rusk told him not to, and both of those are typed. The notebook is not mentioned in that room and may not be.**
- **Della Marner declined a machine and asked for a table,** and Rusk said he would take the call himself at five, and said that she was the second person that week asked for something in a form who had said no, and that he was going to stop using the word coincidence in that building.

## Elias, and the two errors this batch

- **He was wrong about the second trip, twice,** and gave the reason out loud in a room with four people in it: **he has spent nine weeks being the man everybody decides is the right one, and he has enjoyed it, and a man who enjoys being the right one is a man who cannot say no, and there is going to be a day when that costs somebody a card.**
- **He was wrong about the page,** in the mirror of the holding number: **a document that cannot be used without a name is not thereby a safe document, because a document that cannot be used is a document that will be described by the people who read it, and the description gets written by strangers, and it will be accurate, and it will not carry the one fact that would matter, which is that nobody decided anything.** He had it in the notebook by the Wednesday.
- **He refused a bus fare twice** — on Friday day fifty-five when it was offered to him, and on Saturday day fifty-six when it was confirmed — and the second refusal was easier, and he noticed that it was easier and wrote nothing about it.
- **He did the most useful thing he has done in nine weeks and it was writing down somebody else's line:** item 20 of Joon Park's custody schedule, in his right hand, on his knee, because Joon was holding the book open and could not write and hold it at the same time, and his left hand is gone. **Joon said out loud that he was not being romantic about it, it is logistics, and Elias said that was a true thing to say out loud and would be written down in a book that is not that book.**
- **The most useful sentence he learned this month is not his and must not become his:** *a life with nothing in it is a different life and not a better one*, and a tick in the margin of a hose form that means the hose passed and nothing else.

## Rusk's true-thing count, and the count nobody else may keep

**Seven, in this order, all of them his and none of them to be renumbered:** the classification is not the whole cause; a private record is not an alteration of a public one; an inquiry that rules on contested evidence becomes a trial; the part he can give is small and he chose it; a search that produces nothing is the cheapest thing an institution ever does and he is the institution; **the sixth, on a telephone on Monday day fifty-eight, was *Tuesday morning. Not today. Today the woman exists as a woman and not as a block***; and the seventh is the fact-with-a-date in Chapter 77. **The Batch 0003 prompt's Chapter 77 card called the offer the sixth, and the sixth was spent in Chapter 75. The count is continuous and both are in the record.**

## What this batch did not do

- **Nothing about who filled the fourteenth field, and nothing about who cut the rectangle, and nothing about who requested the work.** A unit reviewed a field. A blade took a rectangle. Neither is a person and neither may be made into one.
- **The fourth of March 1998 is not connected to the seventeenth of October 1998, to the relay log, to the First Silence, or to anything, and the refusal to connect them is on the page.**
- **The nine credentials are not mentioned in this batch at all. `MNT-4C-17` does not appear. Nine stays nine and Joon Park's refusal of the nine-line sheet stands.**
- **Perpetua Oyelaran is not named once. Halla Dren is not asked again. The Bell row is unresolved. R. Adare is unresolved. The 2038 pour, the hairline crack, the 210 meters and the buried paired node are untouched. The service door's three states are untouched. Nobody went to a lip and nobody called the handset line.**
- **No interface notice was spent. The volume's notices are still exactly two, in Chapters 53 and 57, and the third is reserved for Chapter 97.**
- **No loan was accepted, offered, scheduled or wanted, and no future person was contacted.** Elias is at Stage 1: First Witness throughout.
- **The box count is still unsettled. No chapter printed a number for the boxes that are not in the building, and the out-of-city copy and the fifth copy of the Bell file were not merged.**
- **Tomas Vale's notebook was not produced, not read, and not mentioned to anybody outside the team.**
- **The answer to *who benefits* is not given.** The batch's answer is *a form doing its job*, and the second half of the Chapter 50 question is still a bracket with nothing under it, and neither Elias nor Rusk can say the word. **That answer belongs to Chapters 81–90.**
- **Nothing in this batch touches the planned ending.**

## Review repair, 2026-09-26 — Batch 0003, twelve findings, and what each one turned out to be

A review of the Batch 0003 writer save returned **twelve items: four blocking, eight continuity and repetition.** All twelve are closed. **No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut, no card-assigned beat was dropped, no day moved, and the volume's ending shape is untouched.** Nine chapters were touched and **all ten are inside their ceilings, Chapter 75 the only one over the ordinary 2,400 and card-permitted to 2,600.**

**The four blocking ones, and the fourth is the one this file's own history predicts.**

1. **Chapter 79 had an orphaned attribution and a missing line.** `Lena Mir said, ` sat on its own line before a scene break, and the question after the break — *then what is actually different about it* — carried no speaker, so a reader would pin it on Lena. **The line is now Lena's and the scene break stands where it was**, and the hinge into Inez's *loose thing* / *an uncontrolled document* distinction is intact. `state/chapter-summaries.md` had been reporting that question as Lena's all along, so the record was right and the page was wrong, which is the direction this repository has found the more dangerous one.
2. **Chapter 71 had two paragraphs fused mid-sentence** at *a thing you can talk somebody out of.Elias Rook wrote the two hundred and ten.* They are two paragraphs now.
3. **The nineteenth of November 2042 is a Wednesday and not a Thursday, and it was wrong in eight places.** Day zero is real — Saturday the sixth of September 2042 — and days 54 to 63 all check out, but Chapter 77 had Rusk fixing the third sitting for *Thursday* the nineteenth, and that had been propagated into `state/open-threads.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/current.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, the Batch 0003 summary and four places in the Batch 0004 prompt. **`state/continuity.md` was contradicting itself: its day table said `91 | 74 | Wed 19 Nov` and its prose said Thursday.** All are **Wednesday** now, and the Batch 0004 prompt — which instructs the next writer to derive every date from day zero while inheriting this one — no longer hands out a contradiction on its first page. **The same prompt also labelled Chapter 89, day 72, the seventeenth of November, *the day before the nineteenth*, and inverted its own pair: day 73, the eighteenth, is the day before. Chapter 90 is the day before it and Chapter 89 is two days before that.**
4. **Chapter 80's counted beat was wrong and two state files certified the error.** Petar Lisk said Elias had been in Cordell *two days out of the last ten*. **He had been there three: day 57 for the store room's public hour, day 58 for the request and the line, and day 61 for the school corridor, which is three streets from the records center.** The previous pass had counted only the two days Chapter 74 and Chapter 75 name in their own text, then written the two into `state/current.md` and the Batch 0003 summary as though a certified figure were a derived one. Petar now names all three days, and both records carry the correction. **This is the same lesson as the fourteen-word spelling hand-list, the copied-forward batch total and the two more counted figures in Batch 0002: the check was the defect, and a figure that has been promoted into a state file stops looking like a claim and starts looking like a fact.**

**The eight continuity and repetition ones.**

- **Chapter 74 said *Six weeks ago Elias Rook had spent nine weeks learning***, and a nine-week span cannot complete six weeks ago. The incoherent quantifier is out and the nine weeks stand.
- **Joon Park claimed the same refusal ordinal twice**, three days apart: the fifth in the school corridor on Chapter 78's day and the fifth again in Chapter 80. **Chapter 80 is now the sixth and names the school corridor as the fifth.**
- **Rusk's *I am not running a trial* was near-verbatim two days apart.** Chapter 75's version is the original and is untouched. **Chapter 77's is now a deliberate, self-aware variation** — he says he said it on a telephone on Monday, and that a room with a recorder in it is a different promise than a telephone — so the echo is a man being consistent in two rooms instead of a line being copied.
- **Chapter 76 asserted a maxim, denied it, and re-asserted the denial as revelation with nothing marking the turn**, so a reader could not tell whether Elias had been contradicted or had changed his mind. **The second paragraph now ends on the clause he objects to — *and not one line of any of it required anybody to decide anything* — and the third opens by naming it: *which is the sentence he heard come out of his own mouth and did not like*, and then gives the turn's actual subject, which is that he had believed on Monday that because a name was not in anything the page was safe.
- **Chapter 77 broke its own assignment forty lines before establishing it**: Counsel Solano's opening sentence was in the present tense and put the sitting *in October* *in a records center forty minutes away*, when the sitting is in the inquiry room in Havenport and Chapter 77 itself says the date is not in October. **It now says she had to account in a document for what she did in October in a records center forty minutes away, and this is not that room.**
- **Constance Ilori's *that is the most dangerous belief a man can hold in an inquiry* reappeared almost intact in Rusk's mouth to Tomas Vale, with nothing on the page.** **It is now the point of the scene rather than an echo nobody marks:** Elias writes nothing in the gap because he heard the same sentence nine days earlier on a telephone in a station in this city from Ilori, who is fifty-seven and was not on his side either, and neither of them has ever heard of the other one, and there is no form in this business with a box for two people arriving at the same sentence about the same man nine days apart. **Two competent women and a Deputy Director, three institutions, one belief, and nobody in a position to say it is a coincidence — which is the volume's subject arriving as a texture rather than as a thesis.**
- **Chapter 80 said Lena Mir had never said *that is well done* to anybody in that building, four days after Chapter 76 had her say *that is good work, Emil*.** It is now **the second time, and the first was to a functionary on Tuesday who went red to the ears, and she is not a woman who says a thing twice to the same man.**
- **Chapter 80's *six words in Joon Park's handwriting* was the wrong hand.** The six words are Elias's — *THEY HAVE NEVER HID ANYTHING FROM US*, written on a bus on the Sunday in Chapter 74, in a notebook that is the one in the tin — and the ending of Chapter 78 now says so. **The same two chapters also ran consecutive seven o'clock meetings deciding a question Chapter 79 had already decided and minuted as item 20**, so **Chapter 80's pad line is now about what is written down and in whose hand, with Inez's addition in a different pen that they decided on Friday where it goes and tonight is what it says.**

**Two more were found by this pass and are recorded because they are the same class of defect as the ones above.**

- **Chapter 76 ended *in four days a Deputy Director is going to decide what to do with the best thing any of them has found*, on a day when Rusk had already telephoned and Chapter 77, the next chapter, is him deciding.** It is **tomorrow a Deputy Director is going to answer that question, and he is going to answer it correctly.** A forward reference to the next chapter's event, four days out, with nothing on the page to carry it.
- **Chapter 73's last line restated the shelf discovery the chapter had already delivered**, which is the batch's own rule — state a rule once, pay it once — broken by a closing paragraph. It is now a forward pull: **the thumb that found them came off the line, and the next bus out of this city is on Sunday morning, and he had looked that up twice and told nobody either time.** Chapter 74 opens on that bus.

**Re-measured after the pass.** Chapters 71–80 are **23,318 words** (23,248 as first written): 71 (2,378) · 72 (2,265) · 73 (2,103) · 74 (2,304) · **75 (2,594, card-permitted to 2,600)** · 76 (2,400) · 77 (2,396) · 78 (2,256) · 79 (2,400) · 80 (2,222). **The added words went into Chapter 77 and Chapter 76 and both chapters were then cut back to the ceiling, because the repository's rule is *cut, not add* and a repair pass may not buy prose with a new length debt.** Nothing was cut that a card assigns: what came out of 77 was a third *worth what it is worth* in one chapter, a doubled *I am going to say it out loud*, a restatement of Monday having changed nothing, and two small qualifiers; what came out of 76 was a relative clause, an adverb and three connectives. **Six files were carrying a word-count figure this pass invalidated — this one, `state/current.md` twice, the Batch 0003 summary, the Batch 0003 handoff count, and the Batch 0004 prompt's copied-forward number — and every one now carries the re-measured 23,318.** The Batch 0004 prompt's 23,129 for Batch 0002 was checked and is still correct.

# Volume 02 continuity — established through Chapter 90

## The calendar, unchanged and re-derived

**Day zero is Saturday the sixth of September 2042.** Chapters 81–90 run **Sun 64 · Mon 65 · Tue 66 · Wed 67 · Thu 68 · Fri 69 · Sat 70 · Sun 71 · Mon 72 · Tue 73.** Every weekday is derived from the collapse Saturday and every one matches `outline/volume-02.md` exactly. **Chapter 86 is the batch midpoint and it is day sixty-nine, Friday the fourteenth of November.** The remaining table is unchanged: **91–100 run Wed 74 (19 Nov, THE THIRD SITTING) · Thu 75 · Fri 76 · Sat 77 · Sun 78 · Mon 79 · Tue 80 · Wed 81 · Thu 82 · Fri 83.** **The volume closes on Friday, day eighty-three.**

**Elapsed figures this batch, all derived from the day and not copied forward.** Cliff Marner is **thirty-four days out on day 64 and forty on day 70, forty-one on day 71, forty-three on day 73**, and **thirty-six on day 66, the day he comes home from Cordell**, which the prompt printed as day sixty-five and the day map does not support. Della Marner is **sixty-four days at a desk on day 64 and seventy-three on day 73.** The man behind the wall is **sixty-four days in on day 64 and seventy-three on day 73.** **Nobody in the ward says the year out loud and nobody writes it on a pad.**

## The 2003 line, and the night it was invented

- **The Office's Designated-Site Incident Return, form number DS-4**, carries a printed last row: *consent entered on behalf of a subject unable to give it — signature of the person entering it, printed name, and the capacity in which it is entered.* **It was added in Revision C, issued 14 January 2003, superseding Revision B. No field was altered and no field was removed.**
- **The printed reason on the revision letter is one sentence: *no field was available on this return for consent entered on behalf of a subject unable to give it.* Requested by: District 9. Signed: Forms and Returns, Continuity Office — a block, no name.**
- **The line is still printed and the form is on the current set, not a superseded one.** Rusk cannot produce the form and says why in one sentence: a form store is a room with forty drawers in it and a drawer is not a document, and there is no instrument that compels anybody to open a drawer for a man without a case number, and the team has a case number and it is the wrong one for a form.
- **LOCK: this is a different revision, on a different form, in a different year, for a different reason from the 4C renumbering form's mid-form revision, and Chapter 83 puts the two apart on the page and refuses to let anybody put them in the same sentence.**
- **The first civic use of that line: Monday 13 January 2003, 02:41, Site 4, Gate Street.** Injured male, unconscious on arrival, ambulance 22:10. **No subject signature obtained. A witness was present and could not find a line, because the form had none. A municipal duty officer wrote in the remarks space on the reverse that he was entering consent on behalf of the subject, signed, timed it, and sent the return to the district office at 06:50. Relief on site 04:15.** He wrote *the officer was entering consent* and not *I consent*, chose that in about four seconds, and has never been sure it was the right difference. **The reason it was the right thing in a hurry: a hole in that form said the district was on the site and nobody present could say what happened. The reason it was not enough, in his own words: *a person can be written in by anybody.***
- **Nobody decided anything. There is no villain, no cover, and no policy. A man in a hurry did a defensible small thing and went to the next hour, which is on the same page in a different column.**
- **The line was invented in the winter of 2003 and the officer is alive. He has not been told that the line came from him and no chapter in this batch tells him.**

## New people and new objects

- **Mavis Toomey**, about fifty-five, twenty-two years at the counter of the **District 9 Service Office, 118 Ninth Street**, a municipal building in a neighborhood the Havenport Continuity Office has no building in and never has. A duty officer's night book is not a public record. **She will not show the book; she opens a cupboard, reads one line and the signature line out loud, and shuts it, and says that if anybody in her office asks she will say she did not know what they were doing and will not be lying.** She gave Joon Park, unasked, the sentence the batch does not use: *he would not have needed to write that if the form had a line on it.* **It names nobody, explains nothing, went into a notebook and not onto a wall and not into a file, and nobody on Ash Street has ever heard it.** The night book is a bound volume, one per year, about forty on a shelf, none off the shelf since the year after it was filled.
- **Duane Prewitt, seventy-one, of the Ninth Ward, part-time at Kessler Hardware, 112 Ninth Street**, four doors down from the district office; the sign is original and dated about 1971; he is in on Tuesdays, Thursdays and Sunday mornings. **He is not a villain, not a resource, not a future self, not a lender, and not a person who did a wrong thing. He has never been asked about it by an institution. His name is in no instrument, no proceeding and no file in this volume, and it is not to be put in one.** **He was not told that the line came from him.** He said, unasked, that he does not remember and has been telling people that for twenty years, and that he remembers the floor, and that he went down on it twice, and that he thought at the time that if he had to leave a man to go and look for a box on a piece of paper then whatever he wrote afterwards would be about him. **The team has no standing over him: no form, no card, no line in anybody's column, no process anybody can start.** Joon Park wrote on the page that he is a person the team went to see and nothing else, and that anybody who writes him down again is going to be doing the thing this whole volume is about.
- **The free fact, which the batch does not use.** A woman came in on the Monday of that week at about four in the afternoon, asked whether the man was all right, was told he was, said thank you and went out. **It is the only time in thirty-nine years anybody has come back to that counter about that night. She gave her name and he has not got it and did not write it down.** It is in Joon Park's notebook with three stated reasons and is not on a wall and not in a file.
- **Three nines in two hundred yards of street** — District 9's office, the Ninth Ward, Ninth Street. **Joon Park noticed it, wrote down that he noticed it, and refused the meaning in writing: nine and nine is not a reason for anything, it is an address.**
- **A primary care clinic, unnamed, in a building with a ramp that is not at Ash Street.** Mr. Duarte's examination is on **the twenty-fifth of November at ten past two**; the driver is Ru Sabar on a swap against a night; Lena Mir signs a form at the clinic. The date had not been said out loud in the room on the evening of the eighteenth.
- **A finding word and a reporting word.** Counsel Solano's distinction, stated in a ground-floor records room on the Meridian on Wednesday day sixty-seven. **A finding word decides something and therefore needs an author who can be spoken to. A reporting word does not decide anything and reports only that a thing happened which is a thing that happened and is going to be put right in the ordinary course. *Error* is a reporting word and carries no author, and it is not a euphemism but a grammar, and it puts the event in the subject position and leaves the space where the person would go empty, and it has been empty since about nineteen eighty-odd when somebody in a forms office chose it while doing a job.** The form is not lying about the error. **No ordinal is put on it anywhere and none may be.**

## THE ANSWER, in the words it was said in, once

**Spoken by Tomas Vale, in a records room on Ash Street, on the evening of Friday the fourteenth of November 2042, to Joon Park, Elias Rook and — after ten past six — Inez Sato. Said once. Not paraphrased since. Not to be said again in that building.**

*A branch is a place where what happened and what somebody said happened do not match. A deleted branch produces fewer contradictory reports. That is a count and not a moral sentence. The job of the network is to suppress contradictory reports, and a network built for that is measurably safer with fewer of them in it, and that is the design target. Nobody has to decide anything. The machine is pointed at a number, the deletion takes the number up, the number is the safety, and the machine is right about itself. It reports all of it with the word *error*, because a person is not a thing that gets put right. And I have spent nine weeks telling this hall that the number of decisions in a corridor is the number of people who get hurt, which is the most correct sentence I have said in ten years, and it is the sentence the machine is built to act on, and I have never once asked who was counting.*

- **It is the answer to the second half of the Chapter 50 question and to the Chapter 60 board line, and it is an answer about a design and not about a person.** It does not make the Office wrong about anything it has actually said. It does not explain the First Silence, the Mercy Array, the Returning Hand, the Choir or the Compact. It is not connected to the fourth of March 1998 or to the 1998 relay log. It cannot be put in a proceeding, it cannot be put on a wall with a year on it, and it cannot be given to the hall, which Inez Sato refuses on Friday evening and then partly reverses on Saturday morning.
- **Tomas Vale has asked in writing that his name not be put on it, and says he is not going to say it again, and the reason he gives is that the moment a true thing about a design has a speaker it stops being a fact about the design and becomes an opinion somebody holds.** It does not touch the 2029 review panel and no chapter in this batch refers to it.
- **Joon Park did not refuse a meaning, because there was none to refuse, and that is the first time in the series.** He says he went to look for the meaning and there is nothing there to decline, and that he has never in his life had nothing to refuse, and that he does not know what he is supposed to do with his hands. **His wanting-to-stop is not a plot thread and Chapter 80's date-and-name entry is not a sequence: in Chapter 82 he says that on Saturday he found out that he did not stop and did not want to, and what he did instead was make up a fifth question on the spot, which he has never done in nine weeks.**
- **Elias Rook got what he asked for since Chapter 57 and it is not a person and it cannot be spent, and nobody in that room could say the next sentence because there was nowhere to put the question.**

## An answer with no author in it

- **It is the lightest object in the world and it is going to be carried by the people the team was trying to protect.** Elias Rook was wrong about the cost of holding it, said on the page in Chapter 86 that he had been pleased, and worked out the error on the Saturday. **An answer everybody can carry is an answer that will be carried by the people the team was trying to protect.**
- **It cannot be given to the county library, because it is not a document and it is nobody's. It cannot be given to a ward in another city. It cannot be handed to a Deputy Director, because it is not evidence about what he is doing and it will be accepted into a file and ruled on none of it.**
- **The hall refused to convene itself about the form on Thursday day sixty-eight and had no meeting on Tuesday day seventy-three, and neither refusal stops it. Inez Sato told the hall in eleven sentences on Saturday morning with no meeting, no form, no pad line and no vote, having changed her mind in about thirteen hours and said the change out loud and asked for it minuted.**
- **Emil Baros refused a column, and it was his function that made him the man who had to say it: a column with a heading is a receipt, a receipt says a thing came into this building on a date, and it did not.** His function is **the hall's copy, check it against the Office's when theirs turns up, know the difference by heart, and not the keeper's line.** The keeper's line remains refused by Lena Mir on the standing ground and Lena is not asked to explain it a second time.

## Joon Park's custody schedule has twenty-one items

**Item 20 is untouched and is not recalled. Nothing has been renumbered. A later chapter may add and may not renumber.**

**Item 21 is the first with no holder, no location and no address, and the first that is not attributed to a person at all.** A statement about the design of a network and about the word an institution uses to report a discrepancy between two records; said once, in a room, on the evening of Friday the fourteenth of November; **it is true, it is not evidence of anything anybody did, there is no column on the wall for it and no file in any building for it, and nobody to whom it can be given, because an answer with no author in it has nobody to give it to**; not to be recalled; not to be produced on request by anybody, including us; **not a record.**

## What is on the Ash Street wall now

Under the clerk's fourth column, in chalk, nobody owns: the four sheets in nineteen hands; **no answer given**, dated, with Inez Sato's name on it, twice; **LENA MIR's two boxes and the absence of a villain**, with **NADI BELL's** sentence; and Petar Lisk's

> **NONE OF THE THREE THINGS ON THIS WALL NEEDED A PERSON TO DECIDE THEM. THAT IS THE SHAPE. I DO NOT KNOW THE MEANING. PETAR L. — I AM NINE AND I HAVE READ IT FOUR TIMES.**

> **A HALL THAT AGREES IS SAFER. THAT IS WHAT WAS SAID. I AM NINE AND I DO NOT AGREE AND I AM WRITING IT DOWN ANYWAY. PETAR L.**

> **NOBODY OWNS THE ANSWER SO NOBODY CAN GIVE IT AWAY. PETAR L. — I AM NINE AND I HAVE READ IT SIX TIMES.**

and under the first of Petar's lines, in his own hand, **19 NOVEMBER. — P. L.**, which is still the only date on the wall. **On Tuesday the eighteenth he took the pencil to it, stood there with it for about eleven seconds, wrote nothing, and said — nobody having asked him — that a hall that puts two dates on a wall in one week is a hall that is starting to expect something and will be disappointed on Wednesday, so there is one date, with a dash and his name on it, and tomorrow is not on there. He asked for it to be written down that he decided it on purpose, and it was.** **The wall has room for about two more lines. All three questions are still up in the same hand and the second half of the oldest is still a bracket with nothing under it. Neither of Petar's new lines answers any of the three and neither contains a number about anybody.** The year is not said out loud in that building and is not written on a pad.

## The Office, after the batch

- **Rusk has still said seven true things and given one procedural kindness, and the count is his and is not renumbered, added to, or kept by anybody.** In this batch he speaks three times: the Sunday telephone call that gives the form's location, the revision date and the absence of a name, and says the sentence he said in the store room with the nouns changed; one line about questions and queues, which is a rule of his and is written down nowhere; and nothing at all about the answer. **He is not in the room on the fourteenth of November.**
- **The third sitting is fixed: Wednesday the nineteenth of November 2042, ten in the morning, in the inquiry room, and this batch does not have it.** Chapter 89 has the room prepared — the table out, the microphone at the end, eight chairs, a jug of water — and four people standing in the corridor outside it for eleven minutes and not going in, and says on the page that it is not tactics.
- **Counsel Solano took the four lines and put them in as facts with dates and ruled on none of them, and says the paragraph will end in a door with no room behind it and that she has to stop there.** She also says that **the covering letter of day thirty-seven promised that the omission in the equipment list would be corrected as an administrative error and it has not been corrected**, that she has checked the file twice in November, that she cannot make it be corrected, and that the form does not do that and will in a year. **The designation that would put Cliff Marner into a system is Chapter 99's and has not been created.**
- **The team's records request names two documents** — the 4C designation history with the intermediate sheets, and the holding-register page for the eleventh of May 2039 and the page either side of it. **Neither is in hand and neither can be in hand by ten o'clock on the nineteenth.** The far end is **the third of December, day 88.**

## Elias, and the three errors this batch

- **He was wrong about the cost of holding a true thing with nobody in it**, and the reason he did not know he was wrong was that he had been pleased. He had built nine weeks of weight on a frame with a man at one end of it, and the answer came in with no man at the end of it and got lighter, and lighter turned out to be the frightening part.
- **He was wrong about using a rule on a thing it was not written for**, and he said so out loud in a room of nine people: the rule says any offer, any boundary, any refusal and any condition, and a document is not any of those four things.
- **He was wrong about being right**, and said it on the steps of an Office building on the seventeenth: being right about the Office's paperwork is not a defense of anything he has done, and he had been quietly hoping it was. **It does not make the corridor right, the seal right, the classification right, the eleven meters right, or the twenty-seven hours and thirty-nine minutes right.**
- **He broke his own rule about Duane Prewitt in a room of nine people and was stopped.** Disclosing the finding on Thursday day sixty-eight, he said a street, a trade and an age out loud — Ninth Street, a hardware shop, seventy-one — which is the locating detail Joon Park's Chapter 83 rule exists to keep out of a room, and Inez Sato stopped him in the middle of the sentence and said why: a street and a trade and an age is an address, and this building does not get to be the thing that writes the man down again. **He gave the finding again without him, said he had said the other half before anybody in the room had decided whether he was allowed to, and asked for the breach to be written down. It went in the pad and not on the wall. No name, no street, no trade and no age about Prewitt is in that building, and a later chapter may not put one there.**
- **He lied to Duane Prewitt, in a shop, about whether the signature in the night book was his, and told him on the spot that he had lied and why.** It is the first time in the volume he has lied to a person rather than to an institution, it is marked on the page, and it is used nowhere.
- **He was on the wrong side of a wall for the third time in a month, on Thursday, and the wall was a refusal and there was no door in it and it was the right wall and there is no form on which he can complain about it.**

## What this batch did not do

- **Nothing about who filled the fourteenth field, who cut the rectangle, who requested the 1998 work, or what 04:12 is.** The 1998 relay log is not reopened. The fourth of March 1998 is not connected to anything.
- **The nine credentials are not mentioned once. `MNT-4C-17` does not appear. Nine stays nine and Joon Park's refusal of the nine-line sheet stands.**
- **Perpetua Oyelaran is not named once.** Nadi Bell's movement record and its past tense are referred to once, obliquely, by Ansel Duarte, and are not read out.
- **Hester Lund is not named once. Her name is in no instrument and no list.** The two vans are not mentioned.
- **Halla Dren is not asked again. The Bell row, R. Adare, the Calder Street room, the 2038 pour, the hairline crack, the 210 meters, the buried paired node, the service door's three states, the `K-7Q` prefix and the Chapter 48 voice are all untouched.**
- **The man behind the wall is unwritten, unresolved and not added to anything.** Nobody went to a lip, nobody called the handset line, and the eleven meters is only ever a written length with a day on it.
- **No interface notice was spent. The volume's notices are still exactly two, in Chapters 53 and 57, and the third is reserved for Chapter 97.** No loan was accepted, offered, scheduled or wanted, and no future person was contacted, asked, addressed or heard. Elias is at **Stage 1: First Witness** throughout.
- **The year is not said out loud in the ward.** It is said on Ninth Street, in a shop, in an Office building on the Meridian and in a corridor, and nowhere on Ash Street and not on a pad.
- **Tomas Vale's notebook was not produced, not read, and not mentioned to anybody outside the team. The 2029 review panel is not referred to in any of these ten chapters.**
- **The answer to the four-to-six-week request is not given, in any form.** The box count is still unsettled and no chapter printed a number for the boxes that are not in the building. The out-of-city copy and the fifth copy of the Bell file were not merged.
- **Nothing in this batch touches the planned ending.**

## The Batch 0004 prompt's own figures, and the three that were not derivable

The prompt instructs the writer to derive every date from day zero and every day number from the outline's day map, and to prefer *since he came up on the thirtieth* and *since the sixth of September* to a numeral. **It also restated the third sitting wrongly, once, as *a date in October Rusk has not fixed*, and its Chapter 89 card then labelled the seventeenth of November *the day before the nineteenth*, inverting its own pair.** The chapter governs and the outline's day table agrees with it: the third sitting is **Wednesday the nineteenth**, day 74, Chapter 91.

1. **Cliff Marner's homecoming is printed as *Tuesday day sixty-five*, and day sixty-five is a Monday.** Tuesday is **day sixty-six**, Chapter 83's day. The prose follows the day, and states thirty-six days out, which the prompt does not list and which is derived.
2. **Chapter 84's card title, *What The Word Is For*, is already Chapter 59's title.** The chapter is **The Reporting Word.**
3. **Chapter 83's card title says *Sixty-Two Years Old*, and the same card, `outline/volume-02.md` and the prompt's own new-canon section all say seventy-one.** The chapter is **Seventy-One, And A Counter.**
4. **Chapter 89's card title, *The Day Before*, is a day and a half out.** The chapter is **Two Days**, and the prose says forty-one hours and *the day after tomorrow*.
5. **Chapter 84's card calls the covering letter's *administrative error* 'the fifth use of the same idea'.** In Chapter 59 it is **the fourth** of five honest words. **No ordinal is used anywhere in the prose.**
6. **Four British spellings were introduced by this pass and caught by the authoritative pattern on the pass that wrote it: `kerb` three times in Chapters 83 and 84, and `defence` once in Chapter 89.** All four forms were already in the pattern. **The pattern was re-run over the whole of `chapters/` afterwards and returns zero.** Both forms are covered, so the pattern is unchanged, and the run of catches-on-the-writing-pass is now four batches long.
7. **Chapter 90 shipped 159 words over the ordinary 2,400 ceiling and is now 50 over.** The 2026-09-26 review-repair pass took 109 words out of it and stopped there, because the residue is inside card-assigned dialogue and two verbatim artifacts. **Four chapters were over the ceiling as written and this file's Length line recorded three of them as inside it: the per-chapter figures were hand-entered and wrong and the batch total was right.** Every per-chapter figure for Chapters 81–90 is now re-measured.

**Re-measured after the 2026-09-26 Batch 0004 review-repair pass. Chapters 81–90 are **23,483 words** (23,571 as first written): 81 (2,397) · 82 (2,400) · 83 (2,350) · 84 (2,193) · 85 (2,399) · **86 (2,598, the midpoint, card-permitted to 2,600)** · 87 (2,399) · 88 (2,086) · 89 (2,211) · 90 (2,450, a recorded 50-word overrun).


## Review repair, 2026-09-26 — Batch 0004, thirteen findings, and the one decision that fixed four of them

A review of the Batch 0004 writer save returned **thirteen items: four state-file errors that would propagate into Batch 0005, nine contradictions and internal errors inside Chapters 81–90, and four minors.** All thirteen are closed. **No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut, no card-assigned beat was dropped, no day moved, no lock touched, and the volume's ending shape is untouched.** Six chapters were touched: 73, 82, 85, 87, 88, 89, 90, plus the four state files, the Batch 0004 summary and the Batch 0005 prompt.

### The one decision: the team's records request went in on a Wednesday

The request that names the two outstanding documents had **four different weekdays on the record.** `state/continuity.md` said Wednesday, day forty-six, the twenty-second of October. `chapter-0063.md` opens on Wednesday morning, the forty-sixth day, and narrates the filing at the counter at four minutes past twelve. `chapter-0073.md` put a filing on a Saturday. **Chapters 81–90 added a fourth claim, a Monday**, in three places in Chapter 89 and in two state files.

**The decision is the twenty-second of October and the prose governs.** It is the only one of the four that produces the arithmetic the volume depends on: four to six weeks from the twenty-second is **the nineteenth of November at the near end, day 74, which is the third sitting, and the third of December at the far end, day 88.** The outline's own day table reads `61–70 | 44–53 | Mon 44 · Tue 45 · Wed 46`, so day forty-six is a Wednesday, and a Monday or Saturday filing cannot be reconciled with the date Rusk minuted the sitting against. **Chapters 73 and 89, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, the Batch 0004 summary and the Batch 0005 prompt now all say Wednesday, day forty-six, the twenty-second of October.**

**Chapter 73 needed more than a weekday.** Its Saturday trip is a *different* request — the shelf-range series request, a subject and a shelf range and a span, which is what Chapter 73 is about and what came back with a shelf list — and the two were filed at the same counter by the same man on the same piece of paper three weeks apart, which is exactly the confusion the review caught. **The chapter now says so on the page, in Joon Park's voice, and records that nobody at the counter put the two together and that he wrote down that they were not put together.** One adjacent weekday in the same scene, Rufus Aitken's *"in my hand at ten past nine on Monday"*, is now **Saturday**, which is the day the counter was open till one and the day the request went in.

### The state-file errors, and the rule they repeat

1. **Petar's Chapter 87 wall line was quoted wrongly in four files.** The prose (`chapter-0087.md`) reads *A HALL THAT AGREES IS SAFER. THAT IS WHAT WAS SAID. I AM NINE AND I DO NOT AGREE AND I AM WRITING IT DOWN ANYWAY. PETAR L.* `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, the Batch 0004 summary and the Batch 0005 prompt all carried a *different* sentence — *I DO NOT WANT THAT. I AM WRITING IT DOWN BECAUSE IT IS TRUE … I AM NINE AND I HAVE READ IT TWICE* — **which appears nowhere in the manuscript.** The prose governs and all four files now carry it. **The second line, *NOBODY OWNS THE ANSWER…*, was quoted correctly and is unchanged.**
2. **"Changed her mind in about nine hours" was wrong in five files; the prose says thirteen**, and 6:10 p.m. Friday to 7:40 a.m. Saturday checks out. Corrected in `continuity.md`, `open-threads.md`, `chapter-summaries.md`, `character-state.md` and the Batch 0004 summary.
3. **`state/chapter-summaries.md`'s Chapter 87 entry had drifted from the prose in five places** and is the file the next writer reads first. It attributed **Nadi Bell's Chapter 76 line** — *this hall does not find things, this hall keeps things other people have decided are finished* — to Tarin Voss in a scene he is not in; it gave Cliff Marner **eleven weeks** where the prose says ten; Comfort Osei **home five days** where the prose says eight; **"the most argumentative eleven people inside four miles"** where the prose says *the most argumentative building on this street*; and it **invented a clause, "nobody in that room could hear themselves over it,"** which belongs to Friday's room of four and not to Saturday's room of twenty-three. **The same *home five days* error was also in `state/character-state.md` and is fixed there.** All five corrected against the prose, and the entry now carries the seven-person corridor round and the fact that Petar was not told.
4. **The wall-space count was off by one and then off again.** Chapter 86 leaves **four** lines; Chapter 87 spends one (**three** left, which the chapter states correctly); Chapter 90 spends a second, so **two** remain. Chapters 90, `continuity.md`, `open-threads.md`, the Batch 0004 summary and three places in the Batch 0005 prompt all said three. **All now say two.** The three questions and the bracket with nothing under it are untouched.

### The two errors that were logic holes, not arithmetic

5. **Chapter 85 disclosed Duane Prewitt to a room of nine people.** Elias said **a street, a trade and an age** — Ninth Street, a hardware shop, seventy-one — while disclosing the finding, which is the locating detail **Joon Park's Chapter 83 rule exists to keep out of a room**, and the batch summary did not record the disclosure at all. **The disclosure is kept and the rule is enforced instead:** Elias gets a street and a trade and an age out loud, **Inez Sato stops him in the middle of the sentence** and gives the reason — *a street and a trade and an age is an address, and this building does not get to be the thing that writes him down again* — and he gives the finding again without the man, says he said the first half before anybody in the room had decided whether he was allowed to, and asks for the breach to be written down. **It goes in the pad and not on the wall.** No name, no street, no trade and no age about Prewitt is in that building, and the fact is now in `continuity.md`, `open-threads.md` and the Batch 0005 prompt as a standing prohibition. **This is the finding's fourth appearance in a room and the chapter still pays its card beat.**
6. **Chapter 87 leaked the thing it is built to contain.** Petar is deliberately not told — Elias refuses the fifth and Inez had already decided not to ask him — and yet he writes *THAT IS WHAT WAS SAID* about the finding and reads it aloud to twenty-three people. **Both halves are now on the page.** The corridor round **accounts for seven people and not four** — Elias's four are named (Miss Bell, Mr. Kwarteng, Mrs. Osei, Mr. Voss) and Inez's three are named (Lena Mir, Mr. Duarte, Mr. Marner), which is exactly the set that discusses the finding in the front room, and **it was not written down anywhere which seven.** And Petar's access is stated: **nobody told him what was found, and he wrote it because a man of nineteen said the first half of that sentence out loud to twenty-three people, and a nine-year-old can hear a sentence.** The line is his answer to the front room, not to the finding.

### The smaller ones, all local

7. **Chapter 88 said Prewitt had been asked *"You have both been here twice now"* when Joon Park never went inside** — Chapter 83 has him on the pavement at ten past three. He was on the door twice, and Elias behind the counter twice.
8. **Chapter 88 said Elias had *"not bought anything either time"* when he bought a roll of tape and a pound ten of bad ties on the Tuesday.** Prewitt now says the whole of what he has bought out of it.
9. **Chapter 90 said a woman walked into *"a room with nineteen people in it"* on the Saturday morning when Chapter 87 says twenty-three, three times.** Nineteen is the hall's standing size and is used that way correctly in Chapters 80, 82, 85 and 86.
10. **Chapter 90 said *"three answers in ten weeks"* and then *"the first of those two things"* four lines later.** Two.
11. **Chapter 87 had Ansel Duarte driven to his clinic on the twenty-fifth by *Ms. Osei* with *a nurse* signing.** Chapters 85 and 90 and the Batch 0005 prompt all say **Ru Sabar, on a swap against a night, and Lena Mir signing the form**, and the chapter now agrees with them.
12. **Mavis Toomey's arithmetic was off on the same figure twice.** *"I have had that page in my head for nineteen years"* is **thirty-nine** — the night is January 2003 and the present is November 2042, which is the figure the batch uses everywhere else — and *"a thing a woman said at a counter in nineteen ninety-nine"* is **two thousand and twenty**, which is her twenty-two years at that counter and cannot predate the event.
13. **The minors.** Chapter 90's *"the four dark hours were four hours away"* at five in the evening is **five hours** on the batch's own clock, and its *"at nine o'clock on a Tuesday"* is **half past seven**, inside the fifty minutes the chapter says the discussion ran. The Batch 0004 summary and `continuity.md` both said Petar **was asked** to put tomorrow on the date and refused; **Chapter 90 has him decide it himself, unasked, and ask for that to be written down**, and the prose is better, so the state files follow it.

### What this pass cost in length, and what it did not

Two of the repairs add words, and the batch was **already over its own ceiling on four chapters** — which the batch summary and `state/current.md` did not say, because their per-chapter figures were hand-entered and wrong. **`wc -w` as written: 82 at 2,481, 85 at 2,412, 87 at 2,424, 90 at 2,559.** The two repairs that add prose were made as tight as the beats allow and then the chapters were **cut, not padded**, following the rule the Batch 0003 pass wrote down: second statements of consequence go, scenes and card-assigned beats stay.

- **Chapters 82, 85 and 87 are now inside the ordinary ceiling** at 2,400, 2,399 and 2,399, having started this pass at 2,481, 2,629 and 2,644. What came out: two previous walls recited one after another in Chapter 85, a *pad is a file* argument stated twice, and a closing beat where she would have given it to him said twice; in Chapter 87, the board described a second time, the round sheet described twice, Petar's sentence-not-on-the-wall asserted three times, and **a narrator flash-forward past the chapter's own day**; in Chapter 82, *a file set does not have a custodian* twice, a refusal-of-meaning argued on both sides of the same door, and a closing reflection that restated the scene it was reflecting on.
- **Chapter 90 is 50 over, down from 159.** The pass took 109 out of it — from the closing reflection, from Lena Mir's account of the recurring shape, and from a clause of Emil Baros's that restated what the room had already established. **The remaining 50 words are inside card-assigned dialogue and inside two verbatim artifacts, Joon Park's twenty-first line of the custody schedule and Tomas Vale's written refusal.** The batch summary's earlier claim that about 160 words could come out of the closing reflection alone was wrong: the reflection is 410 words and cannot lose half of itself without losing the chapter. **The residue is recorded as an overrun and not hidden, and it is the only ceiling in the batch that is not met.**
- **The batch is 23,483 words**, inside the 20,000–24,000 range, down from 23,571.
- **Chapters 51–60 (24,177), 61–70 (23,129) and 71–80 (23,318) are untouched.** The only chapter outside this batch to be edited is **Chapter 73**, and only for the two weekdays the decision above required.
- **This is the third time in this volume that a hand-entered per-chapter figure has been wrong while the batch total has been right**, after Chapter 60 at 2,398 and Chapter 57 at 2,398. **The rule does not change: re-derive every figure from the chapters, and never type a per-chapter number.**

### Still true after this pass

Every lock holds. **Zero interface notices spent** and the volume's two remain in Chapters 53 and 57. **Stage 1: First Witness** throughout. **No loan, no future person, no new enemy.** `MNT-4C-17` and Perpetua Oyelaran absent. **The man behind the wall stays "a length with a day on it"** and the count is the chapter's own day. **The 2003 line is still printed, still nameless, and still first used by a municipal duty officer in about nine minutes of shift.** **The answer is still said once, in a records room on Ash Street, on Friday the fourteenth, by Tomas Vale, and Chapter 87 still refuses to say it a second time.** **The third sitting is still Wednesday the nineteenth at ten in the morning, and neither it nor the answer to the request is in this batch.** The Box 4C designation, Cliff Marner's designation, and Tomas Vale's notebook are all still unspent and still belong to Chapters 96, 97 and 99.
