## Batch 0005 — new canon recorded before the prose, 2026-09-26

**Volume 02, Chapters 91–100, *The Release*.** The prompt's new-canon section authorizes the volume's ending material; this section records the *specific* documents, dates, wording and people that material needs and that the prompt does not carry, so that no prose in this batch has to invent them later. **None of it names anybody who already exists, and none of it is a faction, a lender or a future self.** Everything here is used in the chapters named and is not available to any other.

### Chapter 91 — what the two documents are

- **The near end arrives by telephone at seven minutes to ten on the morning of the nineteenth, from Rufus Aitken at the Cordell counter, and the two documents reach the inquiry room on the Meridian at about ten past twelve, in the middle of the sitting.** He reads the receiving officer's own words off his own file, says both documents are produced and stamped, says the envelope has gone with the regional service, says nobody has to come and collect anything, and says the two of them together take about eleven minutes to look at and about four hours to mean anything. He is the custodian doing the last part of his job correctly, which is not a favor, and it costs him nothing, and he is thanked by nobody.
- **The designation history of sector 4C, from the 2039 consolidation, with the intermediate sheets and not the summary:** 4C was created in that consolidation **as a storage sector designation and not a field designation**, and no intermediate sheet ever converts it. It has been carried forward as a storage sector at every renumbering since, including the 2035 one. The consequence is a category and not a person: the nine rows the Office indexed in 2035 are, on the designation history's own classification, **shelf movements, and a storage sector sheet has a column for a place and not a column for a person.** **Nine stays nine. No holder is named, implied or made findable, nobody learns who signed the second line of the 05:02 dispatch sheet, and this is not a resolution of open thread 9 — it is a document saying the question was asked of the wrong kind of sheet.** It does not exonerate the Office, does not refute the Office's theory, and is not evidence about anybody's crew.
- **The holding register for the same store, the page for the eleventh of May 2039 and the page either side:** a store's register. Columns, on all three pages: **date, box, volume, from, to, and a unit number in a block. There is no column for a person on that page or on either side of it.** The 4C sheets appear as a movement in a date row with a unit number and no name. **The holding register is a different book from the store movement book of Chapter 73 and in a different room of the same building; the two are not merged and no chapter says they are.** The fourth of March 1998, the 4C renumbering form's mid-form revision, `MNT-4C-17`, the `K-7Q` prefix and the stamp format are all untouched by this and appear nowhere in it.
- **Rusk's procedure, which is Chapter 91's chapter:** every document produced is **entered as an exhibit and referred to nobody**, the irregularity named in the four lines is **recorded as a fact with a date and not as a finding**, and the inquiry will make **no finding on any of it**. He minuted that he chose the nineteenth because it is the near end of the team's own request, and that he would have refused the date in October if asked. **He accepts everything put in front of him, including a document he has wanted since September, and rules on none of it, and the room finds out that a hearing can end with nobody having won anything and still be the correct outcome.**

### Chapter 92 — the charge, and what it would have to explain

- **The charge, as particularized on the face of the notice:** that the Kestrel crew **entered a consent on behalf of a person who could not give it, on a resident's own card, at a designated site, without a designation and without a physician's order.** The particular act is a nurse's signature in the capacity box on a resident's consent card in October. The second particular is the carded-draw system itself.
- **The only printed authority for the act is DS-4 Revision C, issued the fourteenth of January 2003, requested by District 9, signed in a block with no name, and still on the current form set.** The inquiry cannot produce it, because a form in a drawer in a form store is not a document and no instrument compels anybody to open a drawer.
- **Rusk's statement, in a room, with a recorder in it:** he says the Office cannot bring the charge as particularized, he says why in one sentence, **he says the defect is in his own office's form set and not in any person, and he says he cannot produce the form and is telling the room that he cannot.** He does not withdraw the charge in that room; the disposition is reserved to the findings sitting, which is ordinary procedure and not mercy. **His count of true things is not numbered, added to, or kept by anybody.**
- **Mara Okafor's disclosure, on the Thursday, to the people who pay for it, in the order her rule requires, and she refuses the vote:** **her own handwriting is going to become a public exhibit of a process that worked, read by strangers, and the wrong stranger, and she wants the hall to have that before it happens and not in a corridor afterwards.** She is not softened, nobody inherits her, and she does not re-explain the rule.

### Chapters 93 and 96 — the box and the eight pages

- **The 2034 write-off box is a cardboard records box on the floor of Tomas Vale's own hallway, under a chair, in a rented first-floor flat, labelled in September by Joon Park with the write-off's holding number and nothing else.** It has not been opened since the third week of September by anybody, including the man who packed it. It contains the 2034 report and, under it, eight pages in one man's hand.
- **The eight pages contain a method and not an origin, and the method is stated in the first person.** It is a way of putting a held plan in front of a room so that somebody who is not the plan's author can stop it. Its steps, in his order: **write the plan down, and write the date, and write the reason you have not said it out loud, and the reason is the thing you are protecting; do not send the plan; take it to a room with at least one person in it who is not you and who was in the room where you were going to do it; say out loud, before you say anything about the plan, that you have not told this room what you are about to do and that you are telling them that before and not after; then stop talking and let them stop you; if nobody stops you, you have not done that step and you go back two; write down who stopped you and the date, and that writing is not a confession, it is the only part of the thing that is a record.**
- **His own name is on the page four times** — at the head of the first page, in the body where he says the method is his and not the industry's and not the office's, in the body where he says he wrote it down because a man ought to be able to write down how to be stopped before he needs to be, and at the end.
- **It contains no origin of anything.** It does not explain the First Silence, the array, the design of the network, the word *error*, the 2003 line, the 1998 page, the 2011 annotation or the collapse. It is a record of a man and a procedure, and it is the only reason he is not the villain of Chapter 92, because a man who has written down how to be stopped cannot afterwards be cited as a man who holds plans, and nobody may cite him that way.
- **He tells them himself, in a room, to the two people he names, which are Mara Okafor and Joon Park. He is not caught. The disclosure the Office cross-indexed to the 2034 write-off in week three does not mention this document, because he told them about the write-off and not about the notebook, and the Office never learns of it from him.** No chapter before 96 mentions it to anybody outside the team.
- **Joon Park's custody schedule gains a twenty-second line in Chapter 96 and is not renumbered.** Item 20 and item 21 are untouched and are not recalled.

### Chapter 94 — the sheet of quoted words, and the letter

- **One page of hall paper, not a form, with two columns: the words as spoken, and where, when and by whom.** Headed **THIS IS NOT A FINDING. IT IS A QUOTATION.** **No signature block, because a signature would make it a document about the signers**, and a note at the foot: every line on it was said in a room with a recorder in it, or typed by a clerk who types what is said.
- **Emil Baros's finding, and it is the same letter it has always been:** the hall's copy of the four lines says *items, 2*; the typed copy in the inquiry file says *item, 2*. One letter, and it came off the wall, because the hall copied the clerk's chalk. **The consequence, and it decides something: the hall's copy of every impounded-items figure in this business is downstream of a clerk's chalk on a wall, there is no original anywhere, and the hall therefore may not say it is quoting the Office — it may only say which copy it is quoting.** His function paid once more and the keeper's line is still not his and Lena Mir's standing ground is not explained a second time.

### Chapters 95 and 98 — the vote and the instrument

- **The release vote is taken on Wednesday morning, the twenty-sixth, at seven, in the front room, before the release, one name at a time, with refusals read aloud.** Petar Lisk is a resident of the building and his consent card is on the card rail, and he reads the board at seven. **Nineteen of the twenty-two agreed in the second week of October what the hall would do if a seam opened inside the building and nobody had to be asked; the other three were asleep, away, or new, and they did it anyway.** **Cliff Marner is not on the sheet and is not offered a place on it, and the two no's on the release vote are not his to cast and he does not get to vote.**
- **The two no's are Nadi Bell and Ru Sabar, and the two yes's are Ansel Duarte and Osei Kwarteng, and those four are the only names the instrument carries.** Nadi Bell's no is aimed at the disclosure rule and is about herself: she has been quiet for ten weeks about a document the hall puts out, because nobody asked her, and she would have said it on the ninth day. Ru Sabar's no is that a condition is not a safe place, because the day there is a copy of a condition is the day somebody can be asked whether they agreed to it. **Neither no is softened, neither is asked twice, and the instrument records both in the same ink and at the same size as the yeses.**
- **What goes in front of the room and into the release is documents only:** the four institutional fields, the blank field, the two words, the 2003 form and its provenance, the 2011 annotation with its unit number, and the eight pages. **No name of a person goes in except the four above.** Hester Lund, Duane Prewitt, Perpetua Oyelaran and the man behind the wall are in none of it.
- **Nadi Bell's movement record enters the record as testimony and is refused as a finding, in the same instrument and in the same session, and nobody in the room treats the refusal as a defeat.** The record is referred to and is not named; **Perpetua Oyelaran's name is used once in this batch, in Chapter 100, on the wall, in chalk, and nowhere else.**

### Chapter 97 — the ninety seconds

- **The doorway is in the records room, where the custody schedule and the tin are, and it is there for ninety seconds.** It opens onto a street that is not there, and the smell is rain on a road surface the team has not got. It is on no drawing, in four buildings, and there is no plan of the ward that carries a door in that wall.
- **Nobody goes through it, and nobody goes toward it, and the volume's third and last interface notice is spent inside the ninety seconds and refused by the fact.**
- **The hall's own procedure runs and it was agreed in October by nineteen of the twenty-two without anybody being asked to agree to it:** nearest person to the door counts the people in the room and says the number out loud; cards of anybody not in the room come off the rail and their names go into the round sheet as out; the round sheet is not a record of where anybody is; nobody runs for a door; and after, the thing that happened is written down in as many hands as will write it.
- **Petar Lisk's card comes off the rail because he was in the dry end, and the hall does that in about eleven seconds without a conversation, and Elias Rook does not help and is not asked.** Ansel Duarte, Lena Mir and Ru Sabar are at the clinic that day and are not in the building. Cliff Marner is in the chair by the window and is in the room.
- **The interface notice, in the approved format, is the third and last in Volume 02, and it is not an offer and it is not a loan and it is not refused in words — it is refused by nobody going through the door.**

### Chapter 99 — the designation, created in public

- **Cliff Marner's designation is created in a hearing room, out loud, with a recorder running, by the Deputy Director who convened the inquiry, and the words are his own and are the volume's:** *not a passenger, a person recovered from a designated site under an inquiry reference.* **It is a field that did not exist in 2042.** Rusk says out loud, before he does it, that **a form does not do this and will in a year, and a Deputy Director can, in a room with a recorder in it**, and that if it is done wrongly it is done wrongly in a document that exists, which is the whole of the argument of the last ten weeks in one sentence and is not an argument against anything.
- **Della Marner is telephoned in Cordell and is given a piece of paper.** She is in Cordell and is not moved for the convenience of a scene.
- **Elias Rook is told, in a room, by the man who is being fair, that he is not a field technician this month**, and the Rescue Service's hose tests and inventory at the depot on the far bank are the whole of what is left to him, and it is the best thing that has happened to him in nine weeks and it is a loss, and both of those are on the page. **The hose work was already three weeks old when the notice was read (Chapters 79, 93 and 97), so the notice removes nothing and no later chapter may describe it as removing something; what is new is the endorsement revocation, the extension of the suspension, and a department's own sentence about him read aloud in a room, and Elias named the loss first so that the room could not hear the sentence as a promotion.**
- **Tarin Voss cannot be found by name in any system, because Finding 3 removed his name from the page that names him as the patient, and a search returns nothing, and no chapter fixes it.** Correct behavior, and the volume's argument in one man.

### Chapter 100 — the last image

- **The board is read at seven on Friday morning, the twenty-eighth, by a nine-year-old, out loud, twice.** On it: the clerk's fourth column in a stranger's chalk saying *items* where it should say *people*, **nineteen hands underneath it, which is a different count from the twenty-eight lines and the two numbers are not required to agree**; the three questions, all still up, with the second half of the oldest still a bracket with nothing under it; Petar's lines; the one date; and **two names in chalk that have to stay in chalk**, which are **CLIFF MARNER**, whose name is now also in a system and whose chalk is going to stay on that wall because chalk is what a person wrote about a person and a printer's face is what an institution wrote, and **PERPETUA OYELARAN**, who is under Ash Street and is not a rescue, not a deadline, and not a row in anything.
- **Underneath Petar's line, in his own capitals, in two halves, exactly like the last one, and not answered:**
  **WHO BENEFITS WHEN A BRANCH IS OFFICIALLY DELETED — AND WHY DOES THE FORM CALL IT AN ERROR?**
- **Then one ordinary beat.** Elias Rook, who is not on the lip and will not be, is asked to carry a box, and carries it, with one hand, up a step, while Mara Okafor tells him to use the ramp and he says he will use the ramp. **The envelope to Nell is unsent, the reason is four words, the road is sealed, he does not ask for a promise and he is not asked for one.**

## Volume 02 is complete — post-Chapter-100 canon, 2026-09-26

**The state the series travels from is the state below. Everything in the section above it is the record of how it got here and is not to be replayed.**

- **The volume closed on Friday, day eighty-three, the twenty-eighth of November 2042.** Day zero is Saturday the sixth of September 2042 and is fixed. **Volume 02 opened on Friday, day thirty-four.**
- **The team's immediate prosecution is prevented.** The charge fell on the twenty-seventh of November **on the ground that the Office cannot bring it without explaining its own form set**, and the finding that replaced it is **a records management finding: an irregularity in a record is answered by a process, and the form cannot be produced, and that is a fact with a date and not a finding about anybody.** Counsel Solano's dissent of about four hundred words was promised in the room and is not written.
- **Three Office endorsements are revoked, effective the twenty-seventh of November:** Mara Okafor's medical advisership for designated sites, and the designated-site advisor's endorsements of Elias Rook and Tomas Vale. **Rusk said in public that he knew since the fourth of October that he could do it, held it for seven weeks, and did it because it costs him nothing, and that it is neither cruel nor generous.** His last statement in the volume is that every instrument he used was available to him in October and holding it was worth more to his office than spending it, and that this is the finding too, and his name goes on it.
- **Tomas Vale is not available as a structural authority on a designated site** — not suspended, not under review, **not available, in the past tense, in a document, forever**, in a box on a form that already existed. His condition: he is not consulted about any designated site by anybody, in writing or out of it. **Rusk said in the room that it is the only identity he has left and that he knows what it is.**
- **The Rescue Service suspension of the forty-first day is extended, not imposed.** The department's sentence is that **Elias Rook is not a field technician this month**: hose certification and inventory, depot, far bank, Tuesdays and Fridays, 06:30, and **these slots will not be held twice.** The hearing record carries Rusk's statement that a Deputy Director wrote one paragraph and a man in a depot wrote the rest.
- **Cliff Marner's designation exists, created in a hearing room with a recorder running, by the Deputy Director, in a box that is not on a form.** ***Not a passenger. A person recovered from a designated site under an inquiry reference.*** Case reference HC-42-0417, dated the twenty-seventh of November 2042. Rusk said out loud first that **a form does not do this and will in a year, and a Deputy Director can, in a room, and if it is done wrongly it is done wrongly in a document that exists.** The name is on it, **spelled from the chalk wall in four hands and from a transit operator's case extract in another city, and the provenance of the spelling is on the record**, and the word *error* is kept out of it. **Della Marner was telephoned in Cordell and has a piece of paper.** He is fifty-three days out of the ground.
- **Tarin Voss cannot be found by name in any system, a search said so out loud on the record, in a search box and not an instrument, and nobody in the room treated it as a problem.** Hollis Vane's sentence is canon: **in about four years somebody will ask why the patient on line six is not a name, and the answer will be a form this office adopted and this inquiry's own findings section carried, and that is the correct answer and the worst answer anybody in that building has ever given. This is correct behavior and no chapter may fix it.**
- **Sector 4C was created in the 2039 consolidation as a storage sector designation and no intermediate sheet converts it.** It has been carried forward as a storage sector at every renumbering since, including 2035. **A storage sector sheet has a column for a place, a date, a volume and a unit number and no column for a person, and there is no printed space on it for a name and there never has been.** The holding register for the same store, the page for the eleventh of May 2039 and the page either side, has six columns on three pages and eighteen rows and not one that a person goes in. **The consequence, and Rusk put it in the record himself: the team's question in October was who holds nine credentials; that question is not answerable and never was; you did not lose an answer, you asked a store a question about a person.** **Nine stays nine. No holder is named or made findable, `MNT-4C-17` is not mentioned, nobody learns who signed the second line of the 05:02 dispatch sheet, and the holding register is a different book from Chapter 73's store movement book and is in a different room of the same building.**
- **Tomas Vale's eight pages are on the record and their contents are now fixed.** Six steps, a method, his own name four times, no origin of anything. **Step four is `say out loud, before you say anything at all about the plan, that you have not told this room what you are about to do, and that you are telling them that now and not afterwards. Then stop talking.` Step five is `stop talking and let them stop you; if nobody stops you, you have not done step five, and you go back to step two and take it to somebody else.`** **It was used once, in public, in the front room on the twenty-sixth of November, by Inez Sato, who said out loud that Mr. Vale told her on Monday not to ask anybody to use it and that she had not asked and was not going to.** He is not caught, the Office never learns of it from him, and the 1998 disclosure names only the write-off. **He refused to have it used as evidence, in any form, in any proceeding, and said a method is only worth anything if somebody else uses it, and that asking them to use it would be him using it on them.** The notebook page about a night carries **no month** and Joon Park refused the connection out loud, in the room, and asked for that to be written down with the rest of it.
- **Joon Park's custody schedule has twenty-two items.** **Item 22 is the only line in the schedule that is a person keeping a thing for himself.** Items 20 and 21 are untouched and are not recalled, and nothing has been renumbered.
- **Ninety seconds happened in the records room on the twenty-fifth of November at about twenty past two.** A doorway in the north wall, about seven feet high, onto a street with buildings on both sides, no people, rain falling on it, a smell of grit and hot brakes and no engine making that smell, and a far end that does not get smaller. **It was there for ninety seconds and nobody went through it and nobody went toward it.** It is on no drawing in four places. **The hall's October procedure ran in about eleven seconds: the number in the room said out loud, cards of people not in the room off the rail, names into the round sheet with the word *out*, the card in a pocket and not back on the rail, and the round sheet is not a record of where anybody is.** The account is in **nineteen hands in chalk under the clerk's fourth column** and Joon Park has put four copies into four buildings and says **none of the four is the record and the record does not exist.**
- **The volume's third and last interface notice is spent, in Chapter 97, inside the ninety seconds:** a `Warning: the branch is changing`, with **nothing under the heading**, which is new. **It was not an offer, it was not a loan, nobody was asked anything, Elias Rook did not answer it and did not say *I hear you*, and it is refused by the fact that nobody went through the door.** There is no fourth notice and none is scheduled.
- **The cooperative voted and the release went forward.** The Cinder Quarter Electrical Cooperative appears, for the first time in this volume, as a body with a sentence in a civic file that it wrote itself. **Twenty in favor, two against, nobody abstaining; four reasons recorded and the eighteen silent votes recorded as a count; the two no's in the same ink and at the same size as the yeses.** **Nadi Bell and Ru Sabar voted no and are named. Ansel Duarte and Osei Kwarteng voted yes and are named. Those four are the only names in the instrument.** **Cliff Marner was in the room, is not on the sheet, and did not get to vote.** **All twenty-two people on the sheet were in the room, so Inez Sato's own rule — a card is not counted if its holder is not in the room — was stated and never invoked. Petar Lisk is nine, is not one of the twenty-two, is not on the sheet and did not vote, and she said she was not making an exception for a boy of nine at seven in the morning in either direction: not protecting him by keeping him off the page and not putting him on it.** The instrument is on the shelf in the tin's place with a band round it twice and **is not in the residents' file and is not in Elias Rook's handwriting.**
- **Nadi Bell's movement record is in the record as testimony and refused as a finding**, at her own request, on her own stated ground, and the refusal is not treated as a defeat. **Perpetua Oyelaran's name is used once in the whole batch, in Chapter 100, in chalk, on a wall, as one of the two names that have to stay in chalk. She is not rescued, not counted as recovered, not a deadline and not a row in a system, and there is no designation for her and there is not going to be one.**
- **A sheet of quoted words exists:** nineteen lines, two columns, headed `THIS IS NOT A FINDING. IT IS A QUOTATION.`, no signature block, every line said in a room with a recorder in it or typed by a clerk who types what is said, and a fourth page carrying the sentence that the hall's figures and the Office's figures are not the same document, that it is quoting a chalk wall and not the Office, and that neither copy is an original because there is no original. **Emil Baros found that provenance in Chapter 94 and did not re-perform the one-letter difference in Chapter 98: the difference was announced in Chapter 76 and read out on the round in Chapter 90, and on the Saturday he was the one who followed it back to the wall.**
- **The hall's two standing refusals are current and were honored in the room that took the vote:** the hall does not convene itself about a form, and there is no meeting, form, pad line or vote about the finding. **Neither is treated as a solution and neither stops the answer being carried.**
- **The wall, as it stands at the end of Volume 02.** At the top, in a stranger's chalk, the clerk's fourth column: twenty-eight lines, a date and a figure against each, **headed ITEMS where it should say *people*.** **Its twenty-eight lines hold two rooms, a laundry, eleven boxes of somebody else's evidence, a terminal, two anchor lamps, a corridor repeater, a corridor light, six access codes and an oxygen cylinder that line fourteen says is two, and the numbers do not agree and were never going to, because the clerk numbered the lines she wrote and not the things on the cart; line fourteen is a child's name written out in full in a ward nurse's hand with the figure struck through it, and that is the line the wall is actually for.** Under it, **nineteen hands** in nineteen different sizes. Under those, **two names that have to stay in chalk: CLIFF MARNER, whose name is now also in a system, and PERPETUA OYELARAN, who is under Ash Street.** Then the three questions in a nine-year-old's capitals with **the second half of the oldest still a bracket with nothing under it**, and three of his lines, and one date, **nine** days old, written on the nineteenth of November and decided on the eighteenth. And then the closing line, which is the Volume 03 question, in two halves, **and it is not answered.** **The wall is full after this line, somebody has to build another wall, and it is not going to be anybody in that building.**
- **The volume's closing question, on the wall, in a nine-year-old's capitals:** **WHO BENEFITS WHEN A BRANCH IS OFFICIALLY DELETED — AND WHY DOES THE FORM CALL IT AN ERROR?** **The first half has an answer, given once on Friday the fourteenth of November by a man who asked in writing not to have his name on it, and the answer belongs to nobody, so it could not be given to the hall and could not be given to a room, and a nine-year-old knows there is one and has not been given it and is not going to ask.** **The second half has no addressee, and the reason is on the wall in Petar Lisk's own reasoning: you cannot ask a form a question and the form does not answer, and that is not a fault in the form, that is what a form is.** **No chapter may restate the answer, improve it, summarize it as a discovery, or use it in a room, and no chapter explains why a form calls it an error.**
- **Elias Rook ends Volume 02 at Stage 1: First Witness**, having been told by a Rescue Service form that he is not a field technician this month, having carried a shoebox up a ramp in one hand, having not asked anybody for a promise and not been asked for one, and having had a thing arrive that is not a loss and finding out that there is no procedure for that either. **The envelope to Nell is unsent. The road is sealed. He is not on the lip and never will be.**
- **Named Thread begins in Volume 03.** The series table assigns it to Volumes 02 and 03; **Volume 02 deliberately did not take it and that decision is recorded in `outline/volume-02.md` and must not be retrofitted into a Volume 02 chapter.**
- **Volume 03 is fixed by `outline/series.md` and `outline/ending.md` and is not the writer's to draft.** A city pilot uses a future engineer's load-reading gift to save a district from a planned demolition, the prediction is accurate, and the pilot chooses which residents stay on the official map. **A future Mara refuses to lend her hands and the refusal is consistent with her boundaries and is not a random outage.** The volume closes on Elias exposing the pilot through patient testimony, recovered logs and a failed evacuation the central plan had hidden, and on the Choir's first public leader arguing that a single chosen future is kinder than the uncertainty.
