# Volume 03 continuity — PROPOSED CANON, recorded 2026-09-26 by the Volume 03 outline phase

**Everything below is a proposal made by `outline/volume-03.md` and recorded here BEFORE any Volume 03 prose exists, as the assignment requires. A writer may use a fact from this section freely. A writer may not use a fact that is not here, and may not change one that is. Anything Volume 03 needs that is not in this section goes into this section first and then into the batch cards, in that order.**

## The calendar, fixed and derived

**Day zero — the collapse — is Saturday the sixth of September 2042. Chapter 100 is Friday, day eighty-three, the twenty-eighth of November 2042. Chapter 101 is Saturday, day eighty-four, the twenty-ninth of November 2042.** Volume 03 runs **day 84 to day 133, Saturday the twenty-ninth of November 2042 to Saturday the seventeenth of January 2043.** **The conversion is: a day number is a chapter number less seventeen. Chapter 101 is day 84; Chapter 125 is day 108; Chapter 150 is day 133.** Every weekday below was derived from the collapse Saturday and not chosen, and the table was machine-checked against a real 2042 calendar when it was written.

| Chapters | Days | Weekdays |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 101–110 | 84–93 | Sat 29 Nov · Sun 30 Nov · Mon 1 Dec · Tue 2 Dec · Wed 3 Dec · Thu 4 Dec · Fri 5 Dec · Sat 6 Dec · Sun 7 Dec · Mon 8 Dec |
| 111–120 | 94–103 | Tue 9 Dec · Wed 10 Dec · Thu 11 Dec · Fri 12 Dec · Sat 13 Dec · Sun 14 Dec · Mon 15 Dec · Tue 16 Dec · Wed 17 Dec · Thu 18 Dec |
| 121–130 | 104–113 | Fri 19 Dec · Sat 20 Dec · Sun 21 Dec · Mon 22 Dec · **Tue 23 Dec (125, the volume midpoint)** · Wed 24 Dec · Thu 25 Dec · Fri 26 Dec · Sat 27 Dec · Sun 28 Dec |
| 131–140 | 114–123 | Mon 29 Dec · Tue 30 Dec · Wed 31 Dec · Thu 1 Jan · Fri 2 Jan · Sat 3 Jan · Sun 4 Jan · Mon 5 Jan · Tue 6 Jan · Wed 7 Jan |
| 141–150 | 124–133 | Thu 8 Jan · Fri 9 Jan · Sat 10 Jan · Sun 11 Jan · Mon 12 Jan · Tue 13 Jan · Wed 14 Jan · Thu 15 Jan · **Fri 16 Jan (149)** · Sat 17 Jan |

**The volume's midpoint is CHAPTER 125, which is day 108, Tuesday the twenty-third of December.** Volume 02's midpoint was Chapter 75 at day 58; the two must never be confused.

**The working week is load-bearing.** The Havenport Tidal Board and the Bellwether cell's public counter are **closed on Saturdays and Sundays.** The works yard runs six days and the Saturday shift is when the program's own people are most visible. **The depot on the far bank runs Tuesdays and Fridays at 06:30** and the next Tuesday slot after day 84 is **day 87**. **The Friday is an inventory half and not a hose certification, and no Volume 02 document removes it — see the depot rota note below.** **No chapter may put a hearing, a counter, a determination or an office on a day it does not open.**

**The depot rota, stated once, because this volume's close prompt and this volume's first batch card both got it wrong and a writer must not have to rediscover it.** R. Dunleavy's letter came back on **Friday the thirty-first of October 2042, day 55**, and it is four lines long and it returned **both** slots in the words *Tuesdays and Fridays, hose certification and inventory, effective from the fourth. Your Tuesday slot is returned to you, 06:30, and your Friday slot is returned to you, 06:30. These will not be held twice* (`chapter-0071.md`). **The Friday hose certification was never his** — `chapter-0086.md`, day 69: *a hose is not his and the inventory half is* — **and the Friday inventory half was his and ran at least to the seventy-sixth day, Friday the twenty-first of November** (`chapter-0093.md`), which is the last Friday depot entry in the manuscript. One Friday was lost on the way, for lateness: **Dunleavy tells him on the Saturday of day 56 that he is four hours late and *your Friday was this morning and you were four hours late for it*** (`chapter-0072.md`), **and Chapter 79, day 62, opens *He had no slot at the depot that Friday*, because a slot is not held twice and he said out loud that he would not be the man who argued with that** (`chapter-0079.md`). **The Friday inventory half then runs again on day 69 and day 76** (`chapter-0086.md`, `chapter-0093.md`), so what was lost was one Friday and not the arrangement. **The notice read into the record on Friday the twenty-seventh of November still lists the depot as running *Hose certification and inventory, depot, far bank, Tuesdays and Fridays, 06:30*** (`chapter-0099.md`), so **the Friday is on the depot's notice at the end of Volume 02 and no document takes it away.** Volume 02 closes with **the Tuesday slot live** — `chapter-0100.md`: *The slot is mine. It is on the rota and it is mine and the depot says they will not hold it twice, and that is a fact about a Tuesday and not about me* — **and a Friday that is on the rota and empty in his week** (`chapter-0099.md`: *it was Friday tomorrow, and Elias Rook had nothing in his week for the first time in eleven weeks*), which is a fact about his week and not about a cancelled slot. **The claim *the Friday slot has been gone since October* is false in every variant of it and no Volume 03 file may repeat it.**

## The counters, and the rule that is standing whether anybody likes it or not

**A counter is always the chapter's own day number minus its own start day, and no figure may be copied forward from a state file. This volume caught five copied-forward counters in one pass in Chapters 93 to 97 and that is the standing argument for the rule.**

- **Cliff Marner came up a corridor on a board at 20:20 on day 30**, Monday the sixth of October 2042. **His counter is the chapter's day number less thirty.** 54 in Chapter 101 · 63 in Chapter 110 · 73 in Chapter 120 · 78 in Chapter 125 · 83 in Chapter 130 · 93 in Chapter 140 · 103 in Chapter 150.
- **Della Marner filed on day 0** and has been at a regional family-reception desk in Cordell ever since. **Her counter is the chapter's own day number.** 84 in Chapter 101 · 108 in Chapter 125 · 133 in Chapter 150.
- **The man behind the wall came this way on day 0. His counter is the chapter's own day number.** 84 in Chapter 101 · 108 in Chapter 125 · 133 in Chapter 150.
- **Prefer naming the day over printing a numeral:** *since he came up on the thirtieth*, *since the sixth of September*. A drift-prone figure is worse than a sentence.
- **Four figures that are not counters and may not drift:** the hall's headcount does not move — **twenty-two resident cards and twenty-eight people, four dark hours a night** — and nothing in Volume 03 adds a household to Ash Street; **Joon Park's custody schedule has twenty-two items and nothing may be renumbered**; the works board's model says **1,140 addresses and 2,900 residents**; the Retention Schedule has **697 rows, 412 retained and 285 transitional, and forty-four addresses inside the September band and outside the December one.**

## Tidegate, the works, and the program

- **The district: Tidegate**, on the west bank of the Tannery Cut, about two miles from the Cinder Quarter and **eleven minutes on a bus.** Four walk-up blocks, a strip of low workshops along the cut, a boatyard, a community health center, a school on Marden Row, Cutbank Row, Sallow Court, a works yard on the north side with a canteen and a survey office, and **Postridge and Co., newsagents and post office, 2 Cutbank Row**, which has a counter, a queue and a nail. **1,140 addresses inside the alignment area and 2,900 residents on the works board's own 2039 model.** The model has been adjusted twice and is a model and the board knows it.
- **The demolition: the Fourth Stage Tidal Defense**, a sea-wall alignment. The board's flood model puts 1.4 m of water in Tidegate on a once-in-sixty-year surge and the ground along the chosen line is soft and cheap to build on. **The Havenport Tidal Board, three commissioners, statutory, frightened and competent, did not choose Tidegate out of cruelty. It chose Tidegate because the ground is soft there and a flood model is a flood model.**
- **The program: the Bellwether Pilot Program**, regional, running **a Bellwether pilot cell in Tidegate** in two rooms above a shop, under a **pilot authorization signed by Director Sable Arden.** `Bellwether` is canon from `bible/terminology.md`: the early name for the One-Future Choir's public pilot cells, each of which selected one safer district outcome. **It is a political and bureaucratic label and not a faction, and Volume 03 does not explain the Choir.** **The paperwork shortens it: `bible/terminology.md` gives the cells' name as the Bellwether Choir, and the authorization, the schedule's program reference, the verification log and every leaflet in this volume carry `BELLWETHER` in capitals or `Bellwether` in title case, with no second word. Nobody remarks on it, Ferrand says the full name aloud in Chapter 148 in front of two hundred people, and the program does not correct him.**
- **Tidegate is designated on day 87, Tuesday the second of December 2042.** The designation is signed by the Director, it is correct, it is what makes the pilot lawful, **and it activates a line in a form that says Tomas Vale is not available as a structural authority on a designated site.** Nobody barred him. He barred himself in November, in a room, in his own words, and asked for it to go in a box with a form in it. **He is not consulted about Tidegate by anybody, in writing or out of it, and does not ask to be.**
- **The securing band** is a measurement: the margin inside which the ground carries, given by a load reading. **The September reading is dated the eleventh of September 2042. The December reading is later. The band has moved. Forty-four addresses were inside the September band and outside the December one, and every one of the forty-four is classified `TRANSITIONAL` on a schedule dated the second of December 2042.**
- **The Retention Schedule.** A program reference, **a date — the second of December 2042 — and 697 rows.** Columns: **address, structure, occupancy class, status.** `OCCUPANCY CLASS` is a **building attribute.** A two-room flat is `PARTIAL` whether one person or five live in it. **There is no column a person goes in, there never has been one, and nobody had to decide anything for a person to be absent from it.** **412 `RETAINED`, 285 `TRANSITIONAL`.** A `TRANSITIONAL` address is not counted in the retained district, and a district that is not counted carries no obligation to rehouse anybody. **The schedule's own footnote, in italics, four lines long, says the band is indicative and the schedule is the operative document.**
- **The clearance of the ninth of November 2042, at 3–27 Sallow Court.** The block is the site and not a destination: **the clearance was run at 3 to 27 Sallow Court and nobody was rehoused there and nobody left it alive to be rehoused anywhere.** The clearance record is **one line and reads `clearance complete, no outstanding`.** **Four people died.** The block was inside the September band and the December band says it is outside it, and the clearance was run correctly on the reading available at the time. **The four are recoverable only from a margin in a pencil in a kitchen, and there is no second copy.**
- **The two address lists for the block do not agree, and the gap is two and is never closed.** The Board's list for 3 to 27 Sallow Court is **41 households**; Winifred Alagoa's sheet has **39 addresses** for the same block; **and the four names in her margin are in neither list, because neither list is a list of people.** Forty-one less thirty-nine is two. **The subtraction is the batch's central exhibit and a reader will do it; the two are a smaller version of the volume's argument and the volume does not resolve them in this batch, or in the volume, because nobody alive has both lists in front of them.** Do not renumber 41, 39 or four, and do not close the gap by inventing a fifth name.
- **The cell's verification log** is held regionally, not by the district. **Requested by Joon Park on day 90, Friday the fifth of December 2042. Near end day 118, Friday the second of January 2043. Far end day 132, Friday the sixteenth of January 2043, which is Chapter 149.** The request is a public records request: **four to six weeks, anonymous by design, and it is for the verification log and not for the clearance record and not for the works' shift book, and the team does not yet know there are three different books.** The far end is the last business day of the volume and the log that arrives is the document the Tidal Board's determination rests on.
- **The volume's central distinction is said once, and the chapter is fixed: Chapter 106, on Ash Street, in Tomas Vale's mouth, with Joon Park writing it down.** It is a measurement and a list are not the same quantity; a load reading tells you where force goes, a retention schedule tells you who is a household, somebody converts one into the other, and the conversion is where people disappear. **It is not in a Tidegate room, it is not about Tidegate, and Elias does not ask for it and may not: he may not put a question about a designated site to a man who is not available on designated sites, and the batch's restraint is that he does not try.** **Nobody else states it in any form in any register — not Anum, not Hobbs, not Ferrand, not a narrator, and not Elias — and nobody paraphrases it.** Anum gives the conversion procedurally and gives the operational reason once; Hobbs declines to discuss the works in a corridor; Ferrand makes his own case in his own words in Chapter 110 and it is a different case. **The one permitted resemblance between the pilot's method and Tomas Vale's eight pages is named once, in one sentence, in Chapter 103, and stopped.**
- **The sixth institutional pattern of the series, and the first of Volume 03, is a field that is not for *when*.** A schedule that records a state has no version history, because no field on it is for when it changed, and a form with no field for when it changed cannot be asked whether it was right on the day it was written. **The canonical count is five and all five come from Volumes 01 and 02** — a field that does not exist (4C, 2035); a field left blank (the aisle log, 14:12); a field never designed for the thing in question (the Office baseline survey); a field that exists and is the right box with nowhere to attach (line six, persons recovered from site); a middle line added overnight with a revision letter. **Anything Volume 03 adds is the sixth, and the Retention Schedule's missing person-column is the third of the five and not a new one.** **The sixth is Hollis Vane's observation, in his own words, in Chapter 134, and it is the volume's technical hinge. It may not be stated by a narrator, by Elias, or by anybody else.**
- **The program's headings for a gift the panel still names `Still Hand` are `SECURED SUBJECT` and `SUBJECT HELD`.** A printer's face is what an institution wrote about one: Chapter 100's sentence, arriving in a form.

## The three interface notices in Volume 03, and no others

**None of Volume 02's three notices carries over. At most one notice in any chapter. The volume carries exactly three.**

| Chapter | Day and date | Notice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 113 | day 96, Thursday 11 December | `Loan offered: Tomas Vale, nineteen years ahead`. `Gift name: Load Reader`. Accepted in the same chapter. |
| 125 | day 108, Tuesday 23 December | `Loan offered: Mara Okafor, twenty-one years ahead`. `Gift name: Still Hand`. **Refused**, with a reason, in a room, before it was acted on. The volume midpoint. |
| 134 | day 117, Thursday 1 January | **A boundary only.** Elias asks the thread to repeat it and the panel returns the boundary and nothing else. |

**The first is not in the first chapter and not in Batch 0001. Batch 0001 carries zero notices of any kind.**

## The gifts, the panels, and the boundaries

- **Load Reader — a future Tomas Vale.** *Reads the path force will take through a damaged structure. It reveals direction and stress, not a magical repair.* Already on the record in `bible/power-system.md` and `bible/terminology.md`. **It cannot repair, cannot predict a collapse, cannot be carried out of a room, and cannot tell you how many people are on a street.** The panel's own fields, fixed:
  - **Want, in his words:** the works are moving and the reading is the only thing standing between a district and a hole in the ground, and he will not have it be said that the method failed because it did not fail. *Give it the season. I am not asking for the district.*
  - **Boundary, stated once:** **the reading is mine to give; the list of who is on it is not.** He will say where force goes. He will not draw a line with people behind it, and he will not know that he has, and the program asks its questions carefully enough that the answer never shows him what he is drawing.
  - **Return, which is a request for a fact and not a favor:** *tell me, out loud, in a room, whether the line moved after I gave it to you.*
  - **His cost to him:** in his branch the reading arrives eleven days later than it does here and four people are in the November clearance who are not in it here, and he has been carrying that for twenty years and does not say so. **The volume finds it out from a schedule and not from him.**
- **Still Hand — a future Mara Okafor, twenty-one years ahead.** Already on the record from Volume 01's first loan. The Chapter 125 panel, in the approved shape:
```text
Loan offered: Mara Okafor, twenty-one years ahead
Gift name: Still Hand
Gift: Keep one living body from failing while another person carries or repairs it.
Boundary: Do not use my hand on anybody who is on a list. Do not use my hand to make a schedule come out right.
Return: When the last one is out of the building, and you say out loud that it is the last one.
```
  - **The refusal is a refusal.** It is consistent with three of her four standing refusals already on the page: *no predicted outcome, no number and no category, anywhere in any document*; *she will not write a rule, only a decision*; and the *estimated survival window* box she filled with a sentence because a technical term in a box reads as a finding. **The Retention Schedule is a document with a category in it, about people, prepared before anybody was asked.**
  - **Her answer to the one question Elias is entitled to ask, in her own words, before the answer:** *You have written down what happens to them before you have asked me, and I am not going to put my hands into a plan that already knows. I have spent twenty-one years refusing to write a rule and only ever writing a decision, and a decision is about one person at a time, and there are two hundred and eighty-five addresses on that page and I have not met one of them.*
  - **It costs eleven people in the January clearance, and the refusal is not the villain of that, and Elias is.** The present Mara does not consent for her and does not defend her, and says out loud that she is not entitled to be angry at a future version of herself and is not going to be grateful to her either. *Nobody inherits me.*
- **No third lender. No second thread. No new mechanic and no new species. Stage 2, Named Thread, is the only stage change in the volume and it is earned across Chapters 113–134.** Its cost is paid in full: the thread is a **proximity**, so Elias knows where a future man is offering; and in **Chapter 134 the most innocent possible use of the new ability — asking a lender to repeat a boundary — moves the band two streets, and forty-four addresses change class, and the schedule does not, because a schedule records a state.** Nobody caused it and the damage is recorded by a form that is right about itself.

## New people

**None is a faction, a lender, or a future self except where stated.**

- **Tobias Ferrand, 52**, Regional Director of the Bellwether Pilot Program. A duty flood officer in 2027, 2031 and 2036. **The volume's antagonist. Competent, warm in a room, not a villain, and he says the number out loud: two hundred and eighty-five addresses, twice, and that he signed the schedule and will sign it again.** **He has never read Tomas Vale's eight pages and nobody connects the two by anything but resemblance.** **Every figure he says in Volume 03 is one of the recorded ones and no chapter may invent a new number in his mouth:** the two hundred and eighty-five, the four hundred and twelve `RETAINED` addresses, the one thousand one hundred and forty, the forty-four, the nine years he has been in the district. **The Chapter 110 card's *four hundred and six* was an unrecorded figure sitting six away from 412 and is now 412, so the room hears the form's own number out of the man who signed the form.**
- **Selwyn Anum, 41**, the cell's method officer. **He drafted the footnote.** He believes a measurement should be reduced to an operational document, he did it correctly, and he cannot sleep. **He gives the conversion and the operational reason in his own professional mouth and stops there: he does not deliver the volume's central distinction and no chapter may put it in his mouth.**
- **Ezinne Abioye, 71**, of 19 Cutbank Row, Tidegate, forty-four years. Classified `PARTIAL` in a two-room flat. **Not a household, not angry, and not wanting anything.** Her sentence: *they are not putting me anywhere. They are putting my address somewhere, and I am at the address.*
- **Winifred Alagoa, 63**, Tidegate, the address keeper. **Has kept a sheet of addresses in her own hand since 2031 because the works board's list has been wrong twice. 1,163 addresses against the board's 1,140. Four dead people are written in the margin in a different pencil, in names, and none of the four is on either address list for the block — see the Sallow Court arithmetic above.** She does not know that what she has is a movement record and says so when she is told, and she does not use the word. **She says no to the word `record` and yes to the fact of existing, at her kitchen table with the door shut for the draught — and she refuses nobody in a doorway anywhere in Volume 03, because the volume's one permitted carrying-beat is Chapter 142 and it is hers and it is staged in a doorway to two program officers, and this batch may not pre-empt it.**
- **Nerys Hobbs, 47**, resident engineer for the works. **Will not say a thing in a corridor that she would say in a room, and will not sign a structure she cannot account for.** **Her refusal in Chapter 106 is about the corridor, not about the band, and she does not deliver the volume's central distinction: she is on her way out, she says she will discuss the works in a room, and she leaves.**
- **Nev Peart, 51**, works foreman. Nineteen years of a shift book in his own hand. **The reason he keeps it is not that the terminal refuses corrections — the terminal takes corrections — it is that the terminal cannot show what it said before.**
- **Ivor Harkness, 68**, Harkness Yard, boat repair on the Tannery Cut.
- **Rhoda Mistry, 47**, clinic manager, Tidegate Community Health.
- **Marged Prosser, 55**, Tidegate. Lost her son in the 2039 outfall. Chairs a parents' group. **The constituency, and she is right about most of it.**
- **The Havenport Tidal Board**, three commissioners, **unnamed in Batch 0001 and not to be named in any batch without a scene that earns it.**
- **The three branch survivors**, who arrive on **Sunday the eleventh of January 2043, day 127, with no interface notice of any kind:** **Emrys Vaughan, 41**, who is legally married in this branch to **Ceri Vaughan** and who uses the name **Bronwen** for a woman in a register who exists and whom he has never met; **Marek Drzewicki, 19**, whose father **Piotr Drzewicki, 54** is alive in this branch, lives eleven minutes away, and cannot be made; and **Sabina Hough, 63**, whose other-branch son is, in this branch, her brother's son, **Declan Hough, 31**, who is in the room. **Volume 03 does not ask any of the three what they want. That is Volume 06's question and may not be taken here.** Volume 03's only question to them is *what is your name and what is your address*, and it is refused to be anything more.

## New facts about Elias's standing position

- **Rusk appears twice in Volume 03 and no more, and the two chapters are fixed: Chapter 120, day 103, Thursday the eighteenth of December, and Chapter 147.** `outline/volume-03.md` asserted the count without allocating it, and the escalation table then had him once. At 120 he is the man who tells the team, in a room, that the pilot authorization is a document with a date on it, that nobody in the Meridian is going to defend a conversion nobody asked for, and that he has now read the schedule and does not like it and cannot say so in his own office. At 147 he says the two things the escalation table gives him and no others. **He does not appear in Batch 0001, and no Volume 03 chapter outside those two may put him in a room.**
- **The Rescue Service notice read into the record on Friday the twenty-seventh of November 2042 said *this month* in four lines, and in the same four lines EXTENDED the suspension from seam assignment of the forty-first day and left case reference HC-42-0417 open** (`chapter-0099.md`). The month was November. **A Rescue Service letter dated the twenty-eighth of November 2042 says the extension expires at the end of that day and is not renewed, and restores him to ordinary field duty from Monday the first of December 2042, with the three endorsement revocations unchanged and irreversible; the case reference stays open, because the case is not his to close.** **This is the only account. The letter may not say the suspension *has run its stated term*, which would contradict the notice issued the day before it, and the volume-close prompt's own summary said exactly that and was wrong.** **No Volume 03 chapter may treat the restoration as a gift, may apologize for it, may restore an endorsement, and may not turn the sentence into a demotion he is fighting.**
- **The depot's Friday certification moves to Thursdays by a depot letter of the ninth of January 2043, and that letter says the slots will not be held twice and takes nothing away and gives nothing else.** The Friday was never gone: it is an inventory half, it is on the twenty-seventh of November notice, and it is in the rota at the end of Volume 02 — **the depot rota note above is the account.** **The Tuesday 06:30 slot costs him an hour of sleep a week for the rest of his life and the volume does not fix it.**
- **The volume's relationship milestone is a practice and not a feeling, and it is Chapter 122:** before a gift, tell the other one — not the gift, *that you are going to ask* — with a four-minute rule that makes silence mean no. **Nothing romantic happens. No future version of either of them promises them anything. The word *faster* is not used anywhere in Volume 03.**
- **The volume's one permitted beat carrying the paid answer is conduct and not speech:** in **Chapter 142**, Winifred Alagoa refuses to hand her 2031 sheet to a program officer for "verification," in a doorway, to two people, and gives a reason that is about her street. **Joon Park is not present and does not record it, and nobody in the chapter says the word, and no chapter restates the Chapter 86 answer.**

## The volume's closing question

**WHO IS INSIDE A LINE THAT WAS DRAWN ON A DIFFERENT DAY? — AND WHO SIGNS THE SCHEDULE?**

**It is not on a wall.** It is written in the back of a school exercise book, bought for two dollars, on a page with a sum on the other side, in the hand of a woman of sixty-three who says she is not writing it for a record and is writing it because she cannot sleep and there is nowhere else to put it. **Nobody takes anything off a wall, and the three questions on Ash Street stay exactly where they are.**

**The Volume 04 question, and the series' first named political opponent:** the Choir's first public leader argues that a single chosen future is kinder than this uncertainty, and his argument attracts people who have already lost too much to wait. **The word `Choir` is spoken for the first time in this manuscript in Chapter 148, once, by Ferrand, and the movement's structure, membership, origin and history are not explained in this volume.**

# Volume 03 continuity — BATCH 0001 AS WRITTEN, recorded 2026-09-26 by the Volume 03 Batch 0001 prose phase

**This section is what the prose actually did, written down so that Batch 0002 drafts from fact and not from the cards. Where a card and this section disagree, this section wins and the card is defective. Every name, street, figure, date and form the ten chapters invented is here, because the assignment says it goes in before the next batch and not after.**

## What the prose settled that the cards left open

- **The second reading is dated the twenty-ninth of November 2042 and the program calls it the second reading.** The outline and the earlier continuity section say "the December reading" and deliberately did not date it; `continuity.md` above says only that it is later than the eleventh of September. **The prose's identification, and it is the one every later batch uses: the second reading is the one taken on 29 November 2042, and the schedule of 2 December 2042 was built on the 11 September 2042 reading, so the cell held a four-day-old measurement when it signed.** Ferrand says in Chapter 110 that the second reading came in on the twenty-ninth of November and the band moved two streets, and that the December reading and the November reading are the same object under two names in two mouths. **The word "December" may not be used for the second reading again.**
- **The Retention Schedule's own footnote says `Current reading: 11 September 2042`, on a document dated 2 December 2042.** This is the batch's find and it is a field, not a lie and not a mistake, and there is no box on that page the twenty-ninth of November could have gone in. **No chapter in this batch states the sixth institutional pattern, and no chapter in any batch other than 134, in Hollis Vane's words, may state it either.**
- **Winifred Alagoa lives at 22 Cutbank Row, not at 19.** Chapter 102 as first written put her at number nineteen and Chapter 105 puts Ezinne Abioye there; 19 is Abioye's and is fixed in `outline/volume-03.md`, so 102 was corrected. **The house numbers on Cutbank Row as they now stand: 2 is the newsagent and post office; 9 is Mrs. Ogundipe, who has a folder; 19 is Ezinne Abioye, two rooms, `PARTIAL`; 22 is Winifred Alagoa, upstairs, with stairs and a boiler; 31 is a woman of seventy-nine who has been on the row forty-six years and is called by her husband's name by everybody including the post office. Number 6 is a shop that sells door furniture with the cell's two rooms above it and a public counter at the back.**
- **The second reading's three dates, and they are three dates and not a conflict.** It was **taken on 29 November 2042**, it was **issued out of the cell on 1 December**, and the Retention Schedule that was built on the September reading is **dated 2 December 2042**. Anum says the pair in Chapter 106 as *"it went in on the twenty-ninth and it came out on the first,"* and that is **issue against date, in a method officer's own professional mouth, and it is not a third account of when the ground was read.** The date on the schedule is the day the conversion was done and not the day the ground moved, and Chapter 103 says so. **On 1 December 2042, the day Chapter 103 is set on, the second reading is two days old** — the chapter's closing line is *The second reading was two days old*, and **no chapter may make it eleven days old again; eleven belongs to the first reading of 11 September and nothing else.**
- **The person handing out leaflets at the corner of Cutbank Row and the yard approach is a woman, and she is never named.** Chapters 101, 102, 104, 108 and 110 all carry her: she has the tape, the clipboard and the box on the Saturday of 29 November, the pile at the newsagent queue on the Sunday, the folding table and the van on the Saturday of 6 December, and she is the *woman in the vest* Inez Sato names in Chapter 104 and the one who tells Elias Rook on a Saturday that the school leaflet is the cleverest piece of administration anybody has done in this city in ten years. **She says *most people do, love* and she is not a joke and not a device. She is not Marged Prosser and is not on the schedule and is not load-bearing; the one thing she is is the reason the reader learns that the program's public face is competent before anybody hostile has said a word about it.** **A different man in a high-visibility vest, from the yard and eating a sandwich, appears in Chapter 108 among Alagoa's Saturday listeners; he is a different person and may not be merged with her.**
- **Elias Rook's days in Tidegate are countable from the day map and the count is now the only count.** He is in the district on **Saturday 29, Sunday 30 November, and Monday 1, Tuesday 2, Wednesday 3, Thursday 4, Friday 5, Saturday 6 and Sunday 7 December — nine days — and the church hall on Monday 8 December is the tenth day and his first evening there.** Chapter 104 is a Tuesday and he is at the depot on the far bank at ten to six and at Ash Street at ten to nine, and he is also in Tidegate that day, at the public counter at number six in the early afternoon and the yard corridor at ten past three, so **a Tuesday in this batch is not a day off from the district and the depot is not the only thing that happens on one.** The prose figures are now **five in Chapter 105, six in Chapter 106, seven in Chapter 107, eight in Chapter 108, and Ferrand's nine in Chapter 110**, and **Ferrand's line is *You have been in this district nine days. I have been in it nine years* — two nines, both true, and that is the cruelty of the sentence and not a coincidence anyone has explained.** A chapter that wants the figure should count from this list, not from a state file, and **Inez Sato's *four days* in Chapter 104 is a forecast about a trip and is not a count and does not need to agree with any of this.**
- **The 33/39/41 arithmetic is unchanged: the works board's printed list for 3 to 27 Sallow Court says forty-one households; Alagoa's sheet says thirty-nine doors; the four names in the margin are in neither; the gap of two is not closed by anybody and may not be.** The reader does the subtraction in Chapter 109 and is not given the answer.
- **There are three books and the team has asked for the wrong one.** Chapter 107 has Joon Park name two of the three at a counter — *verification log, not the clearance record, not the shift book* — and the counterwoman says that if you want to know what happened on a site, that is the works, and they are not us, and you will get a correct wrong answer. **The clearance record is then found in Chapter 109 not in a file but as three printed lines on a works notice on the outside of a shut gate at the yard, where it has been since the ninth of November.** Nobody hid it, nobody covered it, and no program officer stood in front of it and said nothing. **The shift book is Nev Peart's, on the bench by the kettle, and nobody has asked about it and Elias's hand goes on it and comes off.**

## New people, places, objects and figures introduced in Chapters 101–110

**None of these is a faction, a lender, or a future self.**

- **The Rescue Service letter of Friday 28 November 2042**, one paragraph, case reference HC-42-0417, which is the only account of the restoration and is set out in full in the earlier continuity section. Elias carries it in the inside pocket of his coat from Friday the twenty-seventh to Chapter 110 and never puts it on a table.
- **The Bellwether leaflet.** Four sides, one color of ink, a photograph of a wall being built against weather, `BELLWETHER PILOT PROGRAM · Tidegate — works notice · Fourth Stage Tidal Defense, Havenport Tidal Board · Reading office: above 6, Cutbank Row. Open Monday to Friday, 09:00–16:30. Closed Saturday and Sunday. · Your address, your classification, and what happens next: inside. · Ref. BWP/TG/4SD/2042` and, in the same ink and the same size at the bottom, **Tobias Ferrand, Regional Director.** Forty-one thousand went out with the rates, one is on every door, and one is on the school on Marden Row at Marged Prosser's asking. **The word `Choir` is not on it.**
- **The program card.** One per address, handed out with the rates in September, four fields — address, structure, occupancy class, status — and **Ezinne Abioye's says `19 CUTBANK ROW · STRUCTURE: TWO ROOMS · OCCUPANCY CLASS: PARTIAL · STATUS: TRANSITIONAL`.** She dates the back of it in pencil, `18 SEP 42`, and says *it is not a record, it is so that I know how old it is.* **The card is not a record and the pencil date on the back of it is not a document, and that is the volume's thesis in one object and no chapter may say so.**
- **The works notice board at the yard gate.** Carries the alignment figures (`1,140 addresses. 2,900 residents (works board model, 2039, adjusted twice)`) and a printed slip saying the model was adjusted twice, that a model is a model, and that anybody who wants to know the adjustment can write to the board's surveyor and get an answer inside twenty working days — **the Volume 02 sentence in a different font, printed by a man with a surveyor's job and no idea what he has done.** It also carries three notices, of which the oldest is the Sallow Court clearance of 9 November 2042.
- **The clearingance notice**, verbatim:
> **WORKS NOTICE — 3 TO 27 SALLOW COURT · Clearance, 9 November 2042. Nights of 8 and 9. Residents relocated by arrangement. · Clearance complete, no outstanding.**
- **The second reading**, dated 29 November 2042, a soft line in three colors of ink across the west bank, laid over the 11 September roll on a trestle in the works yard survey office. **The two lines are four inches apart along about two streets and on top of each other everywhere else; the difference is forty-four addresses along two streets and not a blob; all forty-four are `TRANSITIONAL` on the schedule of 2 December and none is `RETAINED`.**
- **The Tidal Board's referral letter**, one side, typed, a clerk's hand, no name: enquiries to the board's surveyor at Civic buildings, third floor; requests to attend site, to observe works, or to be consulted on structural matters are not accepted and are referred; **the board has no procedure for a third category and does not intend to build one during an active alignment.**
- **The named observer clause**, which came in under the pilot authorization in August 2042, is regional, is in every cell running one of these programs, and is not a favor. It gives a visiting party an observer with a hand on the log. **Joon Park recognizes the shape of it — it is the same clause shape as a license the Office issued in October to a broker nobody told anything to — and says nothing, in daylight, in a voice four people could hear, and asks for the not-saying to be written down.**
- **The public records request form.** Four things: who is holding it, which series, the span, and a year. The series box holds one series and the back of the form says in eleven words that a request for more than one series is two requests. **The span box is free text and the year box is a menu, and filling in the year overrides the span.** The regional office is not in Tidegate and is on the far side of the city; the form has to be handed in at a counter, because a posted form does not get a date. Four to six weeks, set by nobody at that counter, and a favor does not come back in the same form.
- **The two documents Elias now carries**, and they are the only two in the batch and both are copies: the Retention Schedule of 2 December 2042, nineteen pages, stapled at the corner, given to him out of a drawer unasked on Monday afternoon; and the two rolls, which live in a cardboard tube because a man of forty-one told him on a Monday that a man on a bus is the reason things get damaged. **Joon Park holds neither and so Tomas Vale's sentence does not become a twenty-third custody item.**
- **Elias's marginal note of Thursday 4 December**, on a page he decided weeks ago not to tear out, with two crossed-out attempts on the same page: *two pieces of paper about the same street, made three months apart, and neither of them can find out what the other one said. I have the right question and I do not have the sentence. Somebody in this city writes sentences about forms for a living and it is not going to be me.*
- **The inside back cover of the notebook** now carries the two documentary dates in his own hand: **2 JANUARY / 16 JANUARY.** The back of Thursday-through-Monday has one line on it and it is the near end.
- **The church hall off Marden Row**, booked for the hour by a group with no name on the booking sheet, with a kettle in the corner and a folding table and a microphone that works. About forty people on Friday 5 December, about two hundred on Monday 8 December, and twelve came to the first one in November.
- **The chemist on Marden Row**, unnamed, fifty-eight, doing a weekly delivery round to about forty older residents out of his own van since March 2042, told on Thursday 25 November that a transitional address cannot be billed against the district, still going, and out of money by about the middle of January. **This is Mara's thread and it is about sixty people and it is a clinical and administrative fact and not a moral one, and it is live and unpaid.**
- **Mara's count:** ninety-one people have come to Tidegate Community Health with a program letter in eleven weeks, and every letter says the same four things in the same order. **It is a count of people and not a count of doors, and the difference between that number and Alagoa's 1,163 is the batch's argument and no chapter may state it.**
- **Ferrand's two previous mistakes**, both produced in writing: the 2039 eastern-bank classification error, where people were told the wrong thing for six weeks and he was right about the ground and wrong about the rows; and this one, which he owns in the order *I knew and I signed it anyway*. **Neither is a lie and neither is a crime and the 2039 one is never investigated in this batch.**
- **Ferrand's open promise**, made in a church hall on 8 December and unanswered: **the working folder, with the drafts in it, the September overlay, and the thing he sent back twice, on a Monday.** The next Monday after 8 December is **the fifteenth, day 100, which is Chapter 117**; the fourteenth is a Sunday. **The folder has not arrived. No chapter may say when it arrives without a scene, and it is Batch 0002's first errand.**
- **The margin of Alagoa's sheet.** Half an inch, inside the second fold, four names in a different pencil, written on 9 November at about eleven at night with a kitchen pencil while **a woman from the works** stood in the doorway with a torch she did not need and asked whether anybody at 3 to 27 was elderly and living alone. **She is from the works and not from the cell, and this is load-bearing: the works ran the clearance, the notice on the gate is a WORKS NOTICE, and no Bellwether officer was in that doorway. It was *the cell* in the first draft and it contradicted Ferrand's Chapter 110 claim, that the reading has not lost anybody in the field, which `outline/volume-03.md` holds to be true and which no Volume 03 chapter may refute.** **The four names are not printed in any chapter and the reader does not get them; they are named in Chapter 142.** The only copy in the world is that margin until Joon Park writes them on the back of his own hand in pencil, and he puts them on no schedule and gives them no number.
- **The courtyard of the Cinder Quarter hall:** a chair left in the corridor, a corridor light on the second landing out since Thursday the twenty-sixth, and nine people in the front room at nine o'clock on the fourth of December. **The headcount does not move.**

## What the batch fixed, and what it deliberately left alone

- **Zero interface notices, zero loans, zero offers, zero threads, zero afterimages.** The volume's three are Chapters 113, 125 and 134 and none is spent. **The word `faster` does not appear in Volume 03 in any sense, including the ordinary one** — the batch's two drafts of a records-service sentence were changed to *anything that came back sooner* rather than leave the word standing, and a writer must not reintroduce it.
- **Rusk does not appear and Sable does not appear.** The word `Choir` is not spoken, not written, and not on any reproduced leaflet. **The Havenport Tidal Board's three commissioners are still unnamed and no batch may name them without a scene that earns it.**
- **The answer to *who benefits* is not restated, put in a room, or used as a weapon anywhere in the ten chapters.** The volume's one permitted carrying-beat is Chapter 142 and it is Winifred Alagoa's, in a doorway, to a program officer, on a reason about her street, and it is not this batch's. **What this batch does instead is carry it as conduct: a man who is four seconds from opening a folder does not open it, a man who has a document somebody gave him on purpose does not use it in a yard, and a man who has four names does not go to four doors. That is the whole of the carrying and no chapter may narrate it as an answer.**
- **Tomas Vale was not asked about Tidegate by anybody, is not consulted, and does not ask to be.** He is in one room, Chapter 106, on Ash Street, and what he says there is about method. **He has never read the pilot's method and the pilot has never read his pages, and the one permitted resemblance is Chapter 103's single sentence in Elias's mouth.**
- **Mara's four standing refusals are intact and are not explained.** **The bilateral practice is Chapter 122 and is not started here.** Nothing romantic has happened and nothing has been promised by any future version of either of them.
- **Petar Lisk's wall is full and the three questions are untouched.** A chapter may not add to them, answer them, or take them down, and Petar's wall does not carry Volume 03's question.

## The state of the clock at the end of Batch 0001

- **Day 93, Monday 8 December 2042.** The batch ends on the batch climax.
- **The verification log was requested on day 90, Friday 5 December 2042, at a regional counter, for the Bellwether pilot cell's log for the district of Tidegate. Near end day 118, Friday 2 January 2043. Far end day 132, Friday 16 January 2043, which is Chapter 149 and the last business day of the volume.** **Nothing has come back and nobody has chased it and Joon Park has said the date out loud in public and refuses to say it sooner.**
- **The four names in the margin have existed in exactly one place in the world since 9 November and exist in two places since Sunday 7 December, and neither place is a system, a file, an instrument, a schedule or a form.**
- **The batch's arc is closed and it is a defeat that is not a loss: Elias came to Tidegate to find a hand in a document, found none, told a yard full of people so, asked a room of two hundred a question he knew was bad, was handed four minutes of silence and a folder he did not open, and went home on the 11 with a date on the back of his notebook and a woman with a sheet who is not on anybody's list.**

## Review-fix pass on Batch 0001, recorded 2026-09-26

**A review of Batch 0001 returned twelve findings — four high, four medium, four low — and all twelve are now resolved. No chapter was restarted, no scene was replaced, no beat was cut or added, no day in the volume moved, and the volume's ending, its locks, its counters and its three interface notices are unchanged.** The batch is 22,744 words and only Chapter 101's length changed, by seven words.

**The four high findings, all of them false certifications or false dates, and all of them the kind this file exists to stop:**

- **Chapter 103 said the second reading was *eleven days old* and it is two days old**, being the day before a reading dated 29 November on a Monday, the first of December. Eleven is the first reading's month and it bled into the second reading's age. **The error was repeated as fact in `state/chapter-summaries.md` and has been fixed there too**, which is the fourth time in this manuscript that a summary has been the file which carried a wrong figure forward.
- **Chapter 120 was given a wrong weekday and date in three places — `outline/volume-03.md`, this file, and `workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`** — all three saying *day 103, Wednesday the seventeenth of December*, when **day 103 is Thursday the eighteenth and the seventeenth is day 102, which is Chapter 119.** The prompt's own day table at its line 38 had it right, which is the only reason this was catchable. **The same prompt separately claimed the next Monday after 8 December is *the fourteenth, day 99, which is Chapter 116*; the fourteenth is a Sunday, and the next Monday is the fifteenth, day 100, Chapter 117**, where Ferrand's promised working folder is waiting. Both are now corrected in the prompt and the first is corrected at source in the outline. **The general rule this earns: a date inherited from a source file is a defect until somebody has re-derived it from day zero, and a prompt that contradicts its own table has told you the table is right.**
- **Chapter 105's line 98 carried three `**` markers and rendered as broken emphasis plus two stray asterisks**, on a line the previous pass had edited. It was the only odd-bold file in the volume. **It is now two markers and the whole speech is bold, which is the shape Chapter 105's other bolded speech already uses.** Volume 02 Chapter 85 still has the same defect and is still this repository's open item.
- **Three state files certified a figure the prose does not contain.** `workspace/volume-03/batch-0001/SUMMARY.md`, `state/current.md` and `state/open-threads.md` all said the woman at number thirty-one had been on Cutbank Row **forty-one years**, and **the prose, the chapter summary, this file and `state/character-state.md` all say forty-six.** The prose figure is the one the sentence needs: Abioye has been at 19 since 1998 and says she has known about the woman at 31 *since about 1998*, which is only possible if the woman at 31 arrived first. **All three files now say forty-six, and the defective card at `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0001.md` line 167, which still carried the original thirty-eight, is corrected to match the prose.**

**The four medium findings, all of them the same person or the same numeral failing to stay a person or a numeral:**

- **The leaflet-giver changed sex across the batch** — a man in Chapters 101, 102 and 110, a woman in Chapter 108, and *the woman in the vest* in Inez Sato's mouth in Chapter 104. **She is a woman throughout**, because Chapter 104 had already said so before anybody had to choose, and the person is now recorded above as a woman and left unnamed.
- **Chapter 101 credited the school leaflet to *Mr. Prosser* and Chapter 110 makes her Marged Prosser, she/her**, who went to the school and asked and chairs a parents' group. **The honorific is gone and Chapter 101 now says *Prosser's idea*,** which is how a man in a fluorescent vest with a clipboard would refer to the woman he is talking about anyway. `state/chapter-summaries.md` carried *Mr. Prosser* and is corrected.
- **Ferrand's *You have been in this district four days* reconciled with no count the batch used**, and the batch's own looser figures were themselves one or two behind the day map. **The whole cluster is now derived from the day map and recorded above: five, six, seven, eight, and Ferrand's nine, with the ninth day being Sunday 7 December.** The three other figures in the same cluster went with it: Chapter 105's Abioye *you have come four days running* is **five** and is now true of consecutive days, Chapter 107's *been in Tidegate for five days* is **seven**, Chapter 108's *a district he had been in for six days* is **eight**, its leaflet *carried around for five days* is **seven** (taken 29 November, carried on 6 December), and Chapter 110's closing *what he had learned in four days* and *a man from the Cinder Quarter who had come four times* are both **nine**. `state/current.md` and the batch summary said five and four and are corrected. **Every one of these was a numeral somebody would have re-derived, and none of them was re-derived, which is the standing rule of this repository stated as a fact about the prose and not as a preference.**
- **Chapter 108's canteen woman said *you're the one that got off the eleven on Tuesday*, and no Tuesday in this batch puts him on a bus in Tidegate** — Chapter 104's Tuesday is the far-bank depot and Ash Street. It is **Wednesday**, which is 19 Cutbank Row with the folder in his hand, and it does not collide with the depot-Tuesday lock the next prompt restates.

**The four low findings, all of them a record that had drifted from the prose:**

- **Chapter 106's Anum gives the second reading two dates — *it went in on the twenty-ninth and it came out on the first*** — against a schedule locked at *dated the second of December*. It is issue against date and it is defensible, and it is now recorded above so that a later phase reads it as one reading with three dates rather than as a contradiction.
- **The Chapter 105 card still carried the thirty-eight-year figure the prose pass had already fixed**, and the new prompt's own instruction is that a card disagreeing with continuity is defective and fixing it is part of the phase. **The card is now corrected**, and it is the only Volume 03 card that had to be.
- **`state/current.md` contradicted itself about its own corrections.** It said Chapter 105 was a one-word change and that the two corrections did not change either chapter's word count, and in the same list said Chapter 105 was cut from 2,424 to 2,380; and it said Chapter 101 now reads *the only person keeping it* when the clause had in fact been **cut altogether**. **All three are now the measured facts:** Chapter 101 went 2,325 → 2,314 in the writer's pass and 2,314 → 2,307 here, Chapter 102 is a one-word swap at an unchanged 2,278, Chapter 104 went 2,224 → 2,226 on a two-word expansion, and Chapter 105 is 2,380 after a forty-four-word cut and a restructure.
- **The Batch 0002 prompt's clause *no earlier chapter may stage Alagoa refusing anybody in a doorway at all* was over-broad and collided with Chapter 102**, in which she shuts a door on Elias Rook on a Sunday morning and then opens it four inches and lets him in out of the draught. **The clause is now scoped to the reserved beat — a refusal to hand over a document in a doorway, to two people, on a reason about a street — and Chapter 102 is named as the thing it is not a counterexample to**, which is what `state/continuity.md` has said all along.

**What the review confirmed and nothing here disturbs:** zero interface notices and zero loans in Chapters 101–110; Cliff 54 → 63 and Della and the man behind the wall 84 → 93, every chapter; 412 + 285 = 697; forty-four addresses, all `TRANSITIONAL`, none `RETAINED`; every chapter inside 1,800–2,400; the widened British-spelling pattern at zero over all of `chapters/`; the widened narrator-reference scan at zero for Volume 03; Perpetua Oyelaran unnamed; the three questions, the bracket and the 19 November date untouched; twenty-two cards and twenty-eight people unmoved; Joon Park's schedule still twenty-two items; no Tidegate address in the residents' file; no controller file touched. **The volume's sentence is still spoken once, in Chapter 106, in Tomas Vale's mouth, and is still not restated anywhere.**

## Second repair pass on Batch 0001, recorded 2026-09-26

**A repair pass was dispatched over a finished batch. Nothing was restarted, no scene was replaced, no beat was cut or added, no day in the volume moved, and the volume's ending, its locks, its counters and its three interface notices are unchanged. Eleven findings, all of them concrete, all of them now applied. The batch is 22,868 words and seven of the ten chapters changed.**

**The three that mattered, and the first of them is the one no file in this repository had noticed.**

- **Chapter 109 never said the four people died, and said the opposite instead.** The card requires it — *"The four who died on the ninth of November in a clearance at that block"* — and `outline/volume-03.md`, `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0001.md`, `state/open-threads.md` item 102 and this file's own Batch 0001 section all state it. **The prose said it nowhere**, and the temptation paragraph went the other way: *four doors in this district that did not know what had happened on that street; not one of the four had been told anything by anybody since the ninth of November* — which is only true of living people. Chapters 106 and 110 both lean on the deaths existing (*this district has already had it happen* / *People die in the moving*), so the batch was resting on a fact it had not given the reader. **Winifred Alagoa now says it, once, in her own mouth, with the second pot on and without looking at him**, and the paragraph is the chapter's find: *And they are dead, Mr. Rook, and that is the part nobody has said to anybody. I knew by the Wednesday which end it had gone. Nobody has come to this door… Their names are not on the board's list and they are not on my sheet, because neither one of those is a list of people. The only place their names are written is that margin, and I would know, because I have been checking that board since 2031 and it has been wrong twice.* **She does not say how they died, does not name them, and will not go and tell a family**, and the temptation paragraph is now *there were four people dead in this city for twenty-eight days and there was no piece of paper in the world that said so*. **`Doors do not die` in her mouth two pages later now lands on the fact instead of floating beside it.**
- **Chapter 109 put a Bellwether cell officer in a doorway on the night four people died, which contradicted Ferrand's Chapter 110 claim that the reading has not lost anybody in the field** — and `outline/volume-03.md` holds that claim to be *true and it stays true*, and no Volume 03 chapter may refute Ferrand. **The woman is from the works.** The works ran the clearance, the notice on the gate is a WORKS NOTICE, and no program officer was in that doorway, so the pilot's field record is clean and the horror is worse for being a works notice and not a program one. The word was **the cell**; it is **the works**.
- **Chapter 103's one permitted resemblance claimed three things that are false about Elias Rook.** The sentence read *that is step four of a method I have read once, in a records room on Ash Street … and I have never been in that room and I have not seen the pages and I do not know who wrote them.* **Chapter 96 is canon and has Elias in that room, on the record, asking about the pages, with a man of sixty-four holding them.** He was in it; he saw them; he has known who wrote them since the seventy-ninth day. **The sentence is now *that is step four of a method I have read once, in a front room on Ash Street, in eight pages that a man of sixty-four put on a table in November and said was a method and not a confession, and I have not shown them to anybody and nobody in this room has ever seen them*** — still one sentence, the resemblance still named once and stopped, and the paragraph that follows it, about a thing he brought two miles and eleven minutes and put on a stranger's desk, still works. **The room was also wrong: Chapter 96's eight pages are on a table in the *front* room, and Chapters 103 and 107 both said records room. Both now say front room.**

**The eight smaller ones.**

- **Chapter 101 performed the same beat twice** — *put the inventory sheet into the drawer in the records room where it belonged*, once at the end of the Tidegate walk and again two lines later. `state/chapter-summaries.md` had silently merged them into one sequence, which is how a duplicated paragraph survives a summary that reads like the prose. The second instance is cut and the telephone call about the corridor light stands on its own.
- **Chapter 104's double negative cancelled itself:** *could not find a sentence in it that was unkind and could not find a sentence in it that was anything other than unkind*. The second is now **blunt**, and the sentence says what it was trying to say about a correct letter.
- **Chapter 105 miscounted its own folder and denied its own street.** *The folder in the room was four hundred and twelve addresses of which this street was not a line* — the folder is 697 rows, 412 is the count of `RETAINED`, and 19 Cutbank Row **is** a line, `TRANSITIONAL`. Now *the folder in the room was four hundred and twelve retained addresses and this street was not one of them.*
- **Chapter 105's narrator contradicted its own character.** The narration said she *worked it out in September*; Abioye says *I worked it out before September and I have been living on it for forty-four years*, and what she plainly understood on the fourth reading of the card in September is what the sentence on the card was about. The narration no longer pins a date she refuses.
- **Chapter 106's opening retrospect was impossible.** It put the card scene *on her landing at half past eight* on the Thursday, on a day the chapter opens with him getting the 11 at ten past eight. The time is cut; the scene is on the landing with the door shut, which is what Chapter 105 established.
- **Chapter 106 said *forty-four streets' worth of new measurement* on a passage that has just said the two lines are four inches apart along about two streets.** It is **forty-four addresses' worth** now.
- **Chapter 106 ran two consecutive untagged dialogue paragraphs** in a room of nine, the second of them ending on a pronoun the reader has to carry across a paragraph break. Tomas Vale is now named as the speaker of the first.
- **Chapter 107 said *about thirty people*, and then *thirty people*, and then *a room of thirty people*, for a building the whole manuscript counts at twenty-eight.** All three are **twenty-eight**, and the headcount still does not move.
- **Chapter 107 attributed a line to Selwyn Anum that he never says.** *A man of forty-one had told him on a Monday that a man on a bus is the reason things get damaged* is a quotation attributed to a named character, and Anum says nothing of the kind in Chapter 103 or anywhere else. It is now a characterization and not a quote: *a man of forty-one had told him on a Monday that the reason things get damaged in this world is that somebody carried them onto a bus.*

**The three state-file findings, which are the class of defect this file exists to stop.**

- **`state/chapter-summaries.md` certified *Five days in Tidegate* for Chapter 107 where the prose says seven.** `state/current.md`'s own review-fix note states that this figure *is seven* and that the cluster was re-derived from the day map. **The prose was fixed and the summary was missed**, and a summary that disagrees with the chapter is the file the next writer actually reads. It is **seven**.
- **`state/open-threads.md` item 120 still said the next Monday after 8 December is *the fourteenth, which is day 99*.** The fourteenth is a Sunday. `state/continuity.md` and the Batch 0002 prompt both had it right — **the fifteenth, day 100, Chapter 117**, where Ferrand's folder is waiting — and the item was the one place it was still wrong. Corrected, with the error named in the item so it cannot be reintroduced.
- **`state/character-state.md` linked the woman at the back of the front room in Chapter 106 who said *that's just* to Ru Sabar, whom Chapter 101 puts in a doorway saying something else.** The prose does not name her. The file now says so and forbids a later chapter from making it Ru Sabar on the strength of a state file.

**What the repair did not touch, and why.** **Ferrand's *You have been in this district nine days* in Chapter 110 stands**, and so does the *nine* cluster, because `state/continuity.md` above already re-derived it from the day map and the ninth day is Sunday 7 December; Ferrand is speaking on the tenth and is a man who counts other people's days for a living. **Chapter 110 was not edited at all** — it is 2,395 words against a 2,400 ceiling, and its only defect was the one in Chapter 109, which is why nothing in it needed moving. **The eight pages are in the front room and not the records room, and Tomas Vale's *I have taught it to a man in a records room with nobody in it* refers to some other occasion in his own history and is left as it is.**

**Re-measured, not estimated. The authoritative pattern in `state/current.md` was re-run over the whole of `chapters/` and returns zero, and the widened narrator-reference scan — plural forms included — returns zero for Volume 03, with the only `series` uses being the records-request series.**

# Volume 03 continuity — BATCH 0002, NEW CANON RECORDED BEFORE THE PROSE, 2026-09-26

**Recorded before a single sentence of Chapters 111–120 is drafted, as the Batch 0002 prompt requires. A writer may use anything below. Anything not below may not be used. The section headed *BATCH 0002 AS WRITTEN* follows it after the prose exists, and that one governs.**

## The ten beats, in order, so the shape is auditable before the sentences are

111 **The Issue Slips** · Tuesday 9 Dec, day 94. The band's two occasions get a **date, a time, and a foreman's name** for the first time. 112 **A Room, As She Asked** · Wednesday 10 Dec, day 95. Hobbs in a room; the only man who could say the sentence is barred. 113 **Nineteen Years Ahead** · Thursday 11 Dec, day 96. **The volume's first interface notice, offered and accepted.** The four-day rule expires in this chapter. 114 **What Cannot Leave a Room** · Friday 12 Dec, day 97. The thread as a proximity, the gift's four limits, the first use, and the terrible use thought about and not done and not told. 115 **Three To Twenty-Seven** · Saturday 13 Dec, day 98. Offices shut. The ground on a block went quiet a month after four people died on it. 116 **What I Have Not Told This Room** · Sunday 14 Dec, day 99. Ash Street; the gift nobody can check; step four said out loud with nothing behind it. 117 **Sent Back Twice** · Monday 15 Dec, day 100. **Ferrand's folder arrives.** 118 **The Shutters On Cutbank Row** · Tuesday 16 Dec, day 101. The gift used for what it is, and **a lie told to a program officer in a corridor.** 119 **A Reading You Cannot Repeat** · Wednesday 17 Dec, day 102. The reading changes and nobody caused it. 120 **A Document With A Date On It** · Thursday 18 Dec, day 103. **Rusk's first and only other appearance in the volume.**

## New objects

- **The two issue slips.** One yellow slip per roll, rubber-banded to it, printed by the cell, ref `BWP/TG/4SD/2042`. One slip per occasion. Shape: `SECURING BAND · OCCASION n · TAKEN <date> <time> · WORKS CLEARANCE <time>–<time> · ISSUED <date> <time> · N. PEART, YARD`, and at the foot a ruled line and a foreman's signature. **Occasion 1: `TAKEN 11 SEP 42 06:40 · WORKS CLEARANCE 05:50–07:05 · ISSUED 11 SEP 42 08:10`. Occasion 2: `TAKEN 29 NOV 42 06:15 · WORKS CLEARANCE 05:50–07:30 · ISSUED 1 DEC 42 09:40`.** A reading is taken with a vehicle-mounted instrument and the alignment street has to be shut, so the works' permission is on the slip, and the person who gives it is the works' foreman. **This is the batch's second objective discharged: the band has a date, a time, and a man's name on it, all of them correct, and none of it a form that anybody has to sign twice.** These are the only two new physical slips in the manuscript and their text is fixed above.
- **The closure timetable on the yard gate**, ordinary works practice, a printed card: the alignment goes from open to closed, and after the closure the public side of the alignment cannot be stood on. **A season is a window and the window is the thing the future lender's want is about.**
- **The reclassification sheet.** One page, from Ferrand's regional office to the cell's method officer, **dated 1 December 2042**, listing **the forty-four** addresses with a proposed status change `TRANSITIONAL` → `RETAINED` and a box for the method officer's check. **Stamped and returned twice** — `RETURNED — CANNOT BE ACTIONED ON A LATER OCCASION`, once dated 1 December and once dated 2 December — and **the same annotation in Selwyn Anum's own hand in the check box both times: `A schedule is drawn once. Re-issue on the next occasion.`** At the foot, printed by the regional office, a tally line: **`IF ACTIONED: TRANSITIONAL 241 / RETAINED 456`** (241 + 456 = 697). The next occasion is the fourth and is **January 2043, date not yet fixed**, and the reason it is not fixed is that the date has to be inside the contractor's window.
- **The named observer, Wendy Tallow, 34, regional pilot observer.** Her appointment is the standing request Elias Rook filed at the cell's public counter on 1 December 2042, under the named observer clause that came in with the pilot authorization in August and is in every cell running one of these programs. **She has a hand on the cell's counter log, which is a book at the counter and is not the regional verification log and is not the clearance record and is not the works' shift book.** She is a program officer in the volume's ordinary sense and is correct, and asks fair questions, and is the person Elias lies to in Chapter 118. **She is available for Chapter 142's doorway and is not committed to it; that beat's program officer is not fixed by this batch.**
- **The Tidal Board's query of 16 December 2042**, from the board's surveyor, asking the cell for the date of the reading the Retention Schedule was built on. It could not be answered at the counter, so it went up to the regional office, **and a copy of the Retention Schedule went up with it — the first time that document had left the district.** It arrives in the Meridian on the sixteenth and reaches a Deputy Director's desk on the seventeenth, and Rusk has read it by the morning of the eighteenth. **This is the whole of the reason Chapter 120 can happen, and it is a pass-through, not a decision.**

## The interface notice, verbatim, and it is the only one in the batch

```text
Loan offered: Tomas Vale, nineteen years ahead
Gift name: Load Reader
Gift: Read the path force will take through a damaged structure. Direction and stress. It is not a repair.
Boundary: The reading is mine to give. The list of who is on it is not.
Return: Tell me, out loud, in a room, whether the line moved after I gave it to you.
```

And under the panel, not in a field, the want, once: **Give it the season. I am not asking for the district.**

**These five lines are fixed by `outline/volume-03.md` and by the proposed-canon section above and are not the writer's to improve. The boundary field is the lender stating a condition and not a narrator restating the volume's distinction, and no chapter may build a second pass at the mechanism on it.**

## Named Thread, exactly as the outline states it, with its four canonical limits

- **The thread is a proximity, not a voice and not a summons.** Elias can feel whether it is live, and it is live when he is standing on ground he is being asked about — which in this volume means the alignment in Tidegate — and it is off on the far side of the cut and off in the Cinder Quarter. **It does not tell him where anybody is, and it does not tell him that a man is near.**
- **The four limits of the Load Reader, all of them already on the record** (`bible/terminology.md`, `outline/volume-03.md`): **it reveals direction and stress; it is not a repair; it cannot be carried out of the room; and it cannot tell you how many people are on a street.** The third of those is the batch's engine: **a reading does not leave the building it was taken in**, so nothing he learns can be written down, carried to a counter, or handed to anybody.
- **Hand on the structure is required to read it.** The aftermath of a use is the new perception and it is the dangerous one: **for about an hour afterwards he can see the stress in a structure he is not touching, and it shows a load that is not this building's load today, and it cannot be refreshed and it cannot be willed.** This is Stage 2's cost as the bible states it — aftermath becomes easier to identify and not easier to undo.
- **The batch's terrible use, and it is a use of this batch and not a search for the man.** A man who can read force in a structure, who has no form, no date, no signature and no second copy, and who is the stranger a district has believed for three weeks, **can become the schedule.** He could put a reading into a program and nobody in that program could ever ask whether it was right on the day it was said, and every reason the Retention Schedule is defensible — an author, a method officer, a footnote, a date, a re-read occasion — would be absent. **He thinks about it, he does not do it, and no chapter lets him off, and nobody in this batch tells him he was right to stop.**
- **The immediate strain is bodily and is not a meter.** Two uses on consecutive days give him a locked right hand — the two knuckles of the right hand stay locked for an hour and a half, and he cannot hold a cup and cannot write — and one Saturday gives him a nosebleed and a lost hour.

## New figures, all of them derived and all of them fixed

- The reclassification sheet's tally: **241 and 456**, against the schedule's **285 and 412**, out of **697**. The difference is **forty-four**. **A printed document carries 241 and 456; no character says either number in a room in this batch, and the reader is left to do the subtraction, which is the volume's method.** **SUPERSEDED BY THE REPAIR: the writing pass did not print the two numbers anywhere, so the reader was told to do a subtraction nobody could perform while four state files certified the figures. The tally is now printed in Chapter 117 as `IF ACTIONED — TRANSITIONAL 241 · RETAINED 456`, in the same reproduced-document manner as the two issue slips in Chapter 111.**
- `N. PEART, YARD` on both issue slips, in ink, and it is a man who is fifty-one and has been the foreman of that yard for nineteen years and **does not know that Ferrand named him in a church hall on 8 December.**
- The band's first occasion was taken on **Thursday the eleventh of September 2042, which is day five, and the sixth of September is day zero.** Elias notices the date, knows what day the sixth was, does the subtraction in his head, does not write it down, and **the reason he does not write it down is on the page**: a man with four dead women's names in pencil on somebody else's hand has no way of knowing whether he has got one of them wrong. **No chapter connects the two events and no narrator may suggest a connection.**

## People added, and what each one is

- **Wendy Tallow, 34, regional pilot observer.** See above. Not a villain, not a resource, correct, and owed an ordinary professional reply in Chapter 118 that is a lie.
- **Nobody else is new.** No faction, no lender, no future self, no second thread, no new mechanic, no new species.

## What this batch must not do, restated from the prompt and binding

- **The sentence is not said again.** Chapter 106 is the only place the volume's distinction has ever been spoken, and Chapters 111–120 may not restate, improve, summarize, paraphrase, hand it to anybody, or put it in Elias's mouth. **The reader can hold it alone.** Chapter 112 has Hobbs say that the man who could say it exists and is not available, and that is a fact about a boundary and not a second pass at the mechanism.
- **The third batch objective fails.** *Get somebody who can say out loud that a band is not a list* cannot be achieved in this batch and its failure is the batch, and the reason it fails is that the only man who can say it set a boundary in writing in November and is not asked by anybody, in writing or out of it.
- **The answer to *who benefits* is not restated, improved, summarized, put in a room, or used as a weapon.** What the batch carries is **conduct**: Elias does not trade a Deputy Director's admission back to him, and Elias does not put four names from a margin in a pencil into a parents' group's card index in a church hall, and the reason is a woman on Cutbank Row's consent and not a moral argument.
- **The volume's one permitted carrying-beat is Chapter 142 and is not in this batch.** No chapter here stages a refusal to hand a document over in a doorway to two people on a reason about a street.
- **The word `Choir` is not spoken, written, or on any reproduced leaflet.** The word `faster` is not used. `SECURED SUBJECT` and `SUBJECT HELD` are not used. Perpetua Oyelaran is not named. The three questions on the Ash Street wall are read at seven in Chapter 116 and are not taken down, added to, or answered, the second half of the oldest is still a bracket with nothing under it, **the nineteenth of November is still the only date on that wall**, and Petar Lisk does not add a line because there is no room and he is not going to be the one who builds another wall.
- **The verification log is not chased and nobody in a room asks Joon Park whether it has come.** The near end is 2 January and the far end is 16 January and this batch receives neither.
- **A Tidegate address is not read out loud in the front room on Ash Street and none goes near the residents' file.**

## The batch's work-week, checked against the day map

Chapters 111 and 118 are Tuesdays and the depot's 06:30 slot is available and is used in both. **Chapter 114 is the Friday of the batch and the depot runs Tuesdays and Fridays at 06:30: the Friday half is an inventory half, it is on the depot's notice, and it is empty in his week. That is a fact about his week and not about a cancelled slot, and no file and no chapter may say the Friday is gone.** Chapters 115 and 116 are a Saturday and a Sunday and the Tidal Board and the cell's public counter are shut and the yard does not run on Sundays. Chapter 120 is after the counter shuts at 16:30, in the room above number six, with the counter locked.
