Zero restatement of Chapter 106 sentence. Sixth pattern unspoken. Three wall questions untouched, bracket empty, 19 November only date, wall full, nobody builds another. Who-benefits not restated; carried only as not opening folder, not using document in yard, not going to four doors, not trading Rusk admission, not putting four in card index. No doorway refusal beat. No bilateral practice. No Sable. No future Nell, Door Memory, branch travel; envelope unsent reason four words. Sector 4C, nine, 2011, K-7Q, 1998 page untouched. Tarin Voss not fixed. Verification log neither end received nor chased nor asked after in room. Depot Tuesday used 111 and 118; Friday inventory half on notice empty in his week.

## Review repair pass on Batch 0002, 2026-09-26 — the durable facts, for any later phase

Sixteen findings, all applied. No chapter restarted, no scene replaced, no beat cut or added, no day moved, the Chapter 113 panel untouched, the ending shape and the single next-phase prompt unchanged. Detail in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/SUMMARY.md`. **These are the facts a later phase would otherwise inherit wrong:**

- **The Tidegate day-run started Saturday 29 November 2042 and Chapter 120 is its nineteenth day.** Three canon chapters fix the start and agree: `chapter-0105.md` *five days running* (3 Dec), `chapter-0107.md` *seven days* (5 Dec), `chapter-0108.md` *eight days* (6 Dec). **Run day per chapter of Batch 0002 is 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, off, 16, 17, 18, 19 — Sunday 14 December is the day off.** Chapter 114 says *fourteen days running*; Chapter 116 says *fifteen days, and this is the day off*. **A figure for how long he has been going is derived from 29 November, never copied from the previous chapter's prose.** The writing pass had used 6 December and was wrong by five days at the start and one at the end.
- **Fares are pounds only in this volume: two pounds seventy-five each way, eleven minutes each way, five-fifty a day round trip.** The cumulative figures are derived and are **sixty-six pounds at twelve days (Ch. 112), eighty-two pounds fifty at fifteen (Ch. 115), about eighty-eight at sixteen (Ch. 117)**. **No chapter may put pounds and dollars in one breath.** Volume 01 Ch. 35 and Volume 02 Ch. 71 carry dollars and always will; the `twenty-two dollars` of Volume 03 Ch. 104 is not carried forward past that chapter.
- **The Load Reader's five limits are stated in full in Chapter 113 and nowhere else in the volume.** `direction and stress` survives only there. The boundary is in the Chapter 113 panel and once in Elias's mouth to Hobbs the same afternoon — twice in the whole batch, both load-bearing, and **no chapter may add a third.** A later use may show one limit failing and that is the whole permitted method.
- **`Stage 2` is a planning label and may never appear in the narration.** The volume's second power state is called **Named Thread** in `outline/volume-03.md`, and its cost is now stated in the prose in Elias's own terms: *nobody had stated that cost to him in so many words and there was no form anywhere in the city that stated it: from tonight the after part was the easy part to see and the hard part to put back.*
- **The closure timetable card is on the yard gate, behind glass.** Chapter 115 is the first time Elias stops to read it and Chapter 119 the second. **There is no second card on Sallow Court's gate** — the writing pass had one in Chapter 113 and it is gone; Elias knows the closure date there because the street knows it, not because he has read a card.
- **Petar Lisk reads the board at seven. Volume 03 Chapter 101 has it in the morning; Volume 01 Chapter 31 has it in the evening, and that cross-volume ambiguity is pre-existing and untouched.** In Batch 0002 he reads it in the morning on Sunday 14 December (Ch. 116) and in the evening on the weekdays (Chs. 114, 118, 119, 120). **No chapter asserts a twice-daily habit.**
- **`Before I say anything else` is now in four chapters of Batch 0002 only: 112 (introduced), 114 (Anum names it step four), 116 (Elias names it), 120 (Rusk performs it, Joon Park notices).** Cap for a later batch is one appearance unless a character names it.
- **Chapter 117 prints the reclassification tally** — `IF ACTIONED — TRANSITIONAL 241 · RETAINED 456` — against the schedule's 285 and 412 out of 697. **The figures are fixed and may not be renumbered: 456 − 412 = 44 and 285 − 241 = 44, and both differences are the forty-four.** No character says either number in a room.
- **Chapter 115's interval is twenty days** — 9 November to 29 November — and **Chapter 111's four-inch streets were known since Thursday 4 December**, the day the forty-four was counted with a finger.
- **Chapter 119's half-street softening is the licensed small instance and is NOT Chapter 134.** Chapter 134's event is larger and is brought on by an innocent use: asking the boundary to be repeated moving the band two streets, forty-four addresses changing class, and the schedule not changing because a schedule records a state. **The outline says of 134 that nobody caused it, and that stands: the asking is what set it off, where 119 has no action behind it at all.** **No chapter may repeat, extend or escalate the Chapter 119 softening, and nobody in a room may call it a cause.**
- **The wider narrator-reference scan must include the bare singulars.** The pattern that failed was plurals-only; add `pre-canon`, `Stage [0-9]` and `canon`. Run over the whole of `chapters/`, not over the files the pass edited.
- **Re-measured after the repair:** 111 (2,278) · 112 (2,316) · 113 (2,255) · 114 (2,024) · 115 (1,926) · 116 (1,833) · 117 (1,908) · 118 (1,817) · 119 (1,830) · 120 (1,841) = **20,028**. Nine chapters were cut and three took a few concrete in-world words back. No scene was added and no beat was bought with the length. **Nothing outside Chapters 111–120 was touched.**

# Volume 03 continuity — BATCH 0003 AS WRITTEN, recorded 2026-09-26 by the Volume 03 Batch 0003 prose phase

**This section is what Chapters 121–130 actually did. Where the pre-batch prompt restates canon and this section disagrees, this section wins. Every new name, street, form, date and figure the ten chapters invented is here, because the next batch will inherit it whether or not it is written down.**

## Measured result

**Chapters 121–130, *The List*, days 104–113, Friday 19 December to Sunday 28 December 2042. 20,919 words after the writing pass's own repair round (measured with `wc -w`, not estimated).** Per chapter: 121 (2,399) · 122 (1,915) · 123 (1,903) · 124 (1,982) · 125 (2,373) · 126 (2,022) · 127 (2,037) · 128 (1,988) · 129 (2,049) · 130 (2,251). **All ten inside 1,800–2,400 and the batch inside 20,000–24,000.**

**Exactly one interface notice in the batch, Chapter 125, the volume midpoint, refused. No others. Volume 03 has now spent two of its three (113, 125, 134). Rusk does not appear. Sable does not appear. Ferrand appears only as a signature on a letter and says no figure. `Choir` is not spoken, written, or on any reproduced document. The word `faster` does not appear in any sense.**

## The ten beats, in order

121 **Two Names On One Card** · Fri 19 Dec day 104 · depot Friday half on the notice and empty in his week, twenty days, £110. The works' sequence on the wall planner, eight nights, the January four in a different pen. The wallet of cards and the worked pile on the second nail, a card off the night of the sixteenth with a second name added at the door in the dark, put back upside down, Peart squares it the right way round and knows which card. The stair that still shakes. **The decision: a reading is not hands, and there is one person he could ask, and a question is a hand put out, and he does not telephone her.**

122 **The Rule Is Four Minutes And It Is Not A Courtesy** · Sat 20 Dec day 105 · offices shut, yard open, Alagoa at the bollard with about a dozen and he walks past her. The practice is worked out on the telephone, twice wrong, right the third time. **Four minutes, silence means no, a yes after four minutes is a no with a bad memory.** Petar puts it in the book and not on the wall. Inez: a rule with two people in it is worth two people and the day it goes into a room with a form on the table it becomes a procedure.

123 **How You Tell A Woman Of Eighty-Four** · Sun 21 Dec day 106 · no bus. The board at seven in the morning. Joon rewrites the four names in ballpoint; forty-two days. Joon's "we do not know that" applied to asking a person. **Rhoda Mistry telephones the hall at half past two** and asks how you tell a woman of eighty-four she is being carried out on Wednesday night. Ninety-one people in eleven weeks. She has eleven names on the back of a discharge list and has said no once to a man from another office.

124 **A Form In A Drawer Since August** · Mon 22 Dec day 107 · the clinic at ten past two, Mistry's conditions, the list goes to the works and nowhere else. Peart copies the eleven onto eleven works cards in his own hand and calls for a ladder out, not a carry. Hobbs in a room with the door shut, the eleven handed over, and she tells him what the form is going to be. **Selwyn Anum's form from a drawer since August: `SECURED SUBJECT` / `SUBJECT HELD`, no field for agreement, so the answer goes on the back.**

125 **Two Hundred And Eighty-Five Addresses** · Tue 23 Dec day 108 · **THE MIDPOINT.** Depot at ten to six. Room above six booked 09:30 to 13:00. He tells Mara, four minutes, she says yes at two minutes forty to being told. He invites Wendy Tallow because the people who pay are in the room, which is the flaw arriving. **He asks the question out loud in front of five people and it takes eleven seconds and the question is about the eleven and not about him or about her.** The panel arrives verbatim. The terms are read out (step four, named by Anum). The one question he is entitled to ask. **The refusal, in her words, with three of her four standing refusals in it, in the room, before anything was done.** Anum writes the answer on the back of his own form. Tallow's log entry. **Mara refuses Elias in front of the room: *nobody inherits me*, and being grateful is the same shape of theft as being angry, and the rule was kept and the question was a different one.**

126 **Nobody Was Hurt That Night** · Wed 24 Dec day 109 · last working day of the year, twenty-four days and £132. **Elin** from regional oversight, a one-sided form with eleven ruled lines, three answers in the program's own voice, and the third box has no word in it that is true. `Declined, with a reason, in the room, before any use` — five words, chosen and explained. The address goes in the margin in ink; **Joon refuses to copy it.** Yard: `YARD CLOSED 25, 26, 27, 28 DEC · REOPENS 29 DEC` typed along the bottom of the closure card. **Hobbs's four photographs of 9 November, twenty to seven, from the north corner.** The booking: twenty-eight working days, Monday 5 January to Thursday 5 February, four nights left in it, two of them Saturdays.

127 **The Whole Thing Was Still There** · Thu 25 Dec day 110 · **Christmas Day. Yard, counter, board, determination office, hearing room all shut, and it is shown on printed notices and not announced.** The run stops at twenty-four days. Marigold Truscott's turkey in a blue blanket. Petar's tin of biscuits, which is not a rule. **Mara is not in the building and the reason is Saturday night.** Two minutes forty on the telephone at ten past four. Joon says the two dates in a room on Christmas morning because nobody asked him to.

128 **The Two Dates And A Sheet With Four Rows On It** · Fri 26 Dec day 111 · **Boxing Day.** `CLOSED 25, 26, 27, 28 DEC · REOPENS MON 29 DEC 09:00` in a plastic holder on the inside of the cell's door. The depot notice's Friday column: a tick, no name, the same as every Friday. **The letter from the regional office**, one side, signed Tobias Ferrand: the authorization stands, the schedule is to be reduced to an operational list, `SECURED SUBJECT` and `SUBJECT HELD` are program headings and a gift name is a gift name, the request was registered 5 December, near end 2 January, far end 16 January, nothing sooner, and the log is the only document that can answer the board's surveyor's question. **No figure anywhere in the letter.** The file of working logs comes down off the shelf on Boxing Day and goes back up on Sunday; Elias lays two fingers on the box and nothing comes up; he does not look. Inez's arrangement: nothing leaves the building, no Tidegate address, and the sequence is read out on Sunday and not taken further.

129 **What She Reads Out Loud On A Saturday** · Sat 27 Dec day 112 · counter shut again. Alagoa at the bollard, fourteen people, Rennard and Murrow in a different voice, **Sallow Court last, thirty-nine doors, and the forty-one not given away by anybody.** A woman of forty has lived at 18 since 1988 and has never wanted a number and now does. He stands still this time, and puts the restraint before the number and not after it. **He has the four December dates in his coat and does not give them to her**, on Alagoa's consent and not on a moral argument. The 41/39 gap stated in a street for the first time, and unclosed. Joon has copied the four names out again; forty-eight days.

130 **What You Would Have Had To Do** · Sun 28 Dec day 113 · the log goes back on the top shelf, 1998 at the bottom. The sequence read out in the front room with thirteen people: four rows, four nights, four crews, no addresses, and **Elias says out loud what the document is and is not so that this building cannot later say it did not know.** Joon asks the one question: *what would you have had to do* — and Elias answers **eleven seconds**, and Joon makes him say the sentence he will not be able to un-say, and Inez closes the lock-box. **The hand lamp** — second-hand, out of a box of eight, a pound and something with a pound ten of batteries in it and a pound ten to run, bought Christmas Eve with nothing in it, tested on Boxing Day, not on the residents' file, not on a pad, not in a book, not near the power budget, in his coat. The notebook's two dates and one line under them.

## New names, streets, forms, figures and objects invented here and not before

- **Rhoda Mistry, 47**, clinic manager, Tidegate Community Health. Named in `outline/volume-03.md` and on the page for the first time. Eleven names on the back of a discharge list, three years old, since the 2039 second outfall. Her condition: the list goes to the works and nowhere else. She has said no once, to a man from another office in November who wanted to be complete. **Not a resource, not a moral point, and she says the moral point is not hers to make.**
- **Elin**, regional oversight for the pilot program, name card and role card, out on the 11:40 on Christmas Eve. Fills a one-sided form in the program's own voice, chooses five words, and explains why she chose them. **Not a villain and not a resource; she asks him whether he is going to do it again and is told the truth.**
- **Marigold Truscott**, brings a turkey to Ash Street on Christmas Day in a blue blanket she means to have back in January. Ordinary and named once.
- **The works' sequence**, a wall planner, reproduced once: **20 DEC 2–10 Marden Row crew 4 · 22 DEC 2–12 Rennard crew 4 · 23 DEC 4–14 Murrow crew 5 · 24 DEC 4–12 Marlow St crew 4 · 6 JAN 14–22 Marlow St crew 4 · 10 JAN 22–32 Rennard + 16–18 Murrow crew 5 · 17 JAN 14–22 Cutbank Row + 31 Cutbank Row crew 4 · 19 JAN 15–21 Rennard + 2–12 Cutbank Row crew 4.** Eight nights, four left in 1942, four in January. **Weekdays derived from day zero, and the yard is shut Sundays, so no night falls on a Sunday: 20 DEC Saturday, 22 DEC Monday, 23 DEC Tuesday, 24 DEC Wednesday, 6 JAN Tuesday, 10 JAN Saturday, 17 JAN Saturday, 19 JAN Monday — two Saturdays and a Monday in January, which is what Hobbs and Inez both say.** No block is cleared twice, and the claim now holds in the strong form: within every street the ranges are pairwise disjoint (Murrow 4–14 and 16–18; Marlow St 4–12 and 14–22; Rennard 2–12, 15–21 and 22–32; Cutbank Row 2–12, 14–22 and 31; Marden Row 2–10 once). **The twenty-first of December is not on the planner at all and never was, because the yard is shut on Sundays and Chapter 123 has him not crossing the cut that day.** Peart's figures: nine, eleven, eleven and the rest; **ninety and something across eight nights** (inclusive counting of the eight printed blocks gives 91, which is what the ninety-something has to mean); **the woman at 31 Cutbank Row wants to be last on the seventeenth.**
- **The works' clearance cards**: one per door, works-printed and works-filled, address, night, crew, foreman's initials in ink, in a wallet of plastic sleeves on a nail; **a second nail holds the worked cards**, one for every door for every night since the beginning of November. The card off the night of 16 December for **9 Cutbank Row** has a woman's name and, under it, **a man's name added at the door in the dark in a different hand and ink, with one small figure for a floor.** Elias reads the address and the first name, stops, puts the card back upside down, and Peart squares the pile the right way round and knows which card it is. **Nobody is told any of it and nobody ever will be.**
- **The eleven cards** made by Peart on Monday 22 December from Mistry's list: works' own card stock, a floor and a stair on each, his own initials on all eleven, in a second wallet. **A ladder out, not a carry, one at a time, a man at the bottom of it.** Left with Hobbs in her own drawer.
- **`SECURED SUBJECT` / `SUBJECT HELD: [ ... ]`** — Selwyn Anum's form, one side, program letterhead, **in a drawer since August**, never used until Chapter 125. Four sub-headings under *Acquisition and Return* in the pilot authorization, three addressed to an operator and one to a program. **No field for whether the subject agreed, so the answer is written on the back, in ink, with the date and the hour, and signed.**
- **The counter log entry** written by Wendy Tallow on the night of 23 December, ink, present tense, four lines, last line `no answer offered, no use made, one question asked`. **The counter log is a book at the counter and is still not the regional verification log, not the clearance record, and not the works' shift book.**
- **Elin's one-sided form**: eleven ruled lines, a box at the end of each, and the third box that wants a word in it. The words used: *Declined, with a reason, in the room, before any use.*
- **The regional letter of 26 December**, one side, typed, on program letterhead, signature block `Tobias Ferrand, Regional Director`. **No figure appears in it.** It says the authorization carries a date in August and stands, the method is not withdrawn, the schedule of 2 December is to be reduced to an operational list, the two program headings are program headings, a gift name is a gift name, the request was registered at a regional counter on 5 December with a near end of 2 January and a far end of 16 January and no expedite, and the log is the only document the office holds that can answer the question the board's surveyor asked on 16 December.
- **The closure line on the yard gate card**: `YARD CLOSED 25, 26, 27, 28 DEC · REOPENS 29 DEC`, typed along the bottom of the existing card behind glass. **The cell's door slip**: `CLOSED 25, 26, 27, 28 DEC · REOPENS MON 29 DEC 09:00`, in a plastic holder screwed to the inside of the door at number six.
- **The file of working logs** comes down off the top shelf of the records room on Boxing Day and goes back up on Sunday morning, 1998 at the bottom, spine label gone the color of tea, not opened. **The custody schedule stays at twenty-two items and a man not looking at a thing is not a custody item.**
- **The hand lamp**: second-hand, out of a box of eight, a pound and something; two batteries, a pound ten, **which is the running cost and the only figure the lamp is ever given**; a belt clip; tested in a corridor on Boxing Day at half past nine. **Not a machine, not on the residents' file, not on a pad, not in a book, not near the power budget, and in his coat.** Bought Christmas Eve with nothing in it, so on Christmas Day it is in his coat as an object that cannot yet be used.
- **The works' booking**: twenty-eight working days, **Monday 5 January 2043 to Thursday 5 February 2043**, four nights left in it, two of them Saturdays. **Derivation, so the numeral is never re-broken: 5 January to 5 February inclusive is thirty-two days containing four Sundays, and the yard is shut Sundays, which leaves twenty-eight working days on a six-day week. 5 February 2043 is a Thursday.** Hobbs signs a sheet for every night. **After 5 February the board has to come back for money, and in her experience the board does not come back for money in the same year it has already spent it.** That is the January pressure and it is hers, stated in December, in a room.
- **The four photographs** on the shelf above the terminal in the survey office: 9 November, twenty to seven in the morning, Sallow Court from the north corner, a doorway and a torch beam on a stair. Not a document, not evidence of anything, shown in a closed yard to one man who does not lean over them.
- **The four-minute rule**, as the practice and as the record: *before a gift, tell the other one — not the gift, that you are going to ask — and stop, and count four minutes; silence is a no; a no is a no; a yes after four minutes is a no with a bad memory.* **It is in Petar Lisk's book on the page after the 22nd of November, dated Saturday 20 December, and it is not on the wall and the wall is full and nobody builds another one.**

## Figures derived in this batch, all from the day map, none copied

- **Cliff Marner 74 → 83** (day less thirty). **Della Marner and the man behind the wall 104 → 113** (day). Both correct in every one of the ten chapters.
- **The Tidegate run**: started Saturday 29 November 2042. **Run day is 20 in Chapter 121, 21 in 122, 21 in 123 (he does not ride on the Sunday), 22 in 124, 23 in 125, 24 in 126, and it stops at twenty-four on the night of Wednesday 24 December because Christmas Day is Thursday the twenty-fifth and he does not go.** He rode on Sunday 30 November and Sunday 7 December and took Sunday 14 December off. **The figures in the prose are twenty days and £110 (121), twenty-one days and £115.50 (123), twenty-two fares and £121 (124), twenty-four days and £132 (126), and twenty-four days at the end (127–130).** Fares are two pounds seventy-five each way and five-fifty a day round trip. **The writing pass had every one of these one day too high from Chapter 122 onward and caught it in its own repair round; the arithmetic is now `days elapsed from 29 November, less the Sundays of 14 and 21 December`.**
- **The four names**: written by a kitchen pencil on 9 November, in half an inch of margin inside Alagoa's second fold, and by Joon Park first in pencil on Sunday 7 December, then in pencil again on 21 December, then in ballpoint on 27 December. **Ages given: forty-two days on 21 December, forty-eight days on 27 December.** Both correct.
- **What is not drift and was not touched**: hall twenty-two cards, twenty-eight people, four dark hours; custody twenty-two items; works model 1,140 and 2,900; schedule 697 with 412 `RETAINED` and 285 `TRANSITIONAL`, dated 2 December, footnote `Current reading: 11 September 2042`; forty-four addresses inside September and outside November, all `TRANSITIONAL`; 41 against 39 for 3 to 27 Sallow Court, unclosed; reclassification tally 241/456 printed in Chapter 117 and **not spoken by anybody in this batch**; the second reading taken 29 November, issued 1 December, and called neither December nor November in this batch.
- **The Load Reader's five limits are not stated in this batch.** One limit is shown failing twice and no more: the stair at number six still shakes after two minutes (121) and a reading that cannot be carried out of the place it was taken in (121, 128 — where nothing comes up through a box in Ash Street because the ground under Ash Street is not the ground anybody is being asked about). The boundary is not restated and the volume's central distinction is not restated, paraphrased, improved or handed to anybody.
- **`Before I say anything else` appears zero times in Chapters 121–130.** It is in Volume 03 at 108, 112, 114, 116 and 120, and in this batch the two references to it are a man naming it as *step four* (122) and Anum naming it as *step four* while telling Elias he is using it in the wrong room (125). **The volume-wide count is five chapters, not four, and Chapter 108 is a Batch 0001 instance that the Batch 0002 records did not count; it is left standing and is not a defect.**

## The interface notice, verbatim, and it is the only one in the batch

```text
Loan offered: Mara Okafor, twenty-one years ahead
Gift name: Still Hand
Gift: Keep one living body from failing while another person carries or repairs it.
Boundary: Do not use my hand on anybody who is on a list. Do not use my hand to make a schedule come out right.
Return: When the last one is out of the building, and you say out loud that it is the last one.
```

**The refusal is the answer, in her words, in the room, before anything was done with it, and it uses three of her four standing refusals**: *no predicted outcome, no number and no category, anywhere in any document about that wall*; *she will not write a rule, only a decision*; and the *estimated survival window* box she filled with a sentence because a technical term in a box reads as a finding. **She does not ask for a category, a number, a schedule, or a form, and she is not a doctor in this scene — she is a woman being asked about eleven strangers, and the ask was the wrong shape.**

## What the batch did to each character, and what it did not

- **Elias Rook** loses the cover of a rule he helped make. He told Mara, he stopped, he counted, she said yes inside four minutes, and he asked a different question: one about eleven people she had never seen, in a room with the program's form in it, having himself invited the program's observer. **The practice is not the thing that broke. The asking was.** He does not use a gift, he is refused the thing he reached for, and he is told the cost of the asking by three separate people in four days. He does not look at the 1998 log, does not hand over the four dates, does not put a Tidegate address anywhere near the residents' file, and ends the batch with a lamp in his coat. **Nobody lets him off.**
- **Mara Okafor** keeps all four standing refusals, gives the practice a yes inside four minutes on Saturday and a yes to being told on Tuesday, refuses the future woman neither angrily nor gratefully, and says *nobody inherits me* in front of five people. **Nothing romantic happens and no future version of either of them promises them anything.** She telephones on Christmas Day and tells him not to look for somebody to be angry at.
- **Tomas Vale** is not asked, not consulted, not approached, and does not appear. He is named twice: as the man whose boundary in November bars him, and as the man who wrote a method that Elias is using in the wrong room. **The eight pages are not shown, not quoted, and not connected to anything.**
- **Joon Park** refuses to copy a street, rewrites the four names twice, says the only sentence he can say about asking a person, says the two dates in a room on Christmas morning unasked, and asks the one question of the batch. **He is not a narrator and he is not a functionary and he is never in the room on the 23rd.**
- **Nerys Hobbs** gives the four photographs, the torch, the four minutes a door, the ladder, and the January booking, and does not sign a structure or a sheet she cannot account for. **She is in a room and not a corridor.**
- **Nev Peart** copies eleven cards in his own hand, calls for a ladder instead of a carry, tells Hobbs at ten past seven on the Wednesday morning before he has said good morning to anybody, and squares a pile of cards the right way round without saying anything. **He has still not opened the shift book and the reason he keeps it is not said and remains Chapter 141's hinge.**
- **Selwyn Anum** produces a form he wrote in August, refuses twice about the mechanism, writes the answer on the back of his own form, and names step four while telling Elias it is the wrong room. **He never states the volume's central distinction.**
- **Winifred Alagoa** reads her sheet at a bollard for the eleventh year, says thirty-nine doors in a street, says why she has never gone round the block with a tape, and is not given four dates she did not ask for. **The doorway beat reserved for Chapter 142 is not staged and no earlier chapter in Volume 03 stages it.**
- **Tobias Ferrand** is a signature on one side of paper and says no figure. **Tobias Ferrand's nine years, his 285, his 412, his forty-four and his two streets are not in this batch.**
- **Ezinne Abioye, Marged Prosser, Ivor Harkness, Sable Arden, Nathan Rusk** do not appear and are not named.

## Fixes applied in this pass to its own drafts

- **`programme` was written in 27 places across all ten chapters and replaced with `program` in the same pass.** Two more British forms came from the same pass and were fixed in the same pass: `favour` and `recognised`. **The authoritative pattern was then re-run over the whole of `chapters/` and returns zero.** This is the seventh consecutive batch in which a British form has been caught by the pass that wrote it.
- **A narrator reference to the book** in Chapter 123 — *eleven times in this volume of your life* — rewritten to *eleven times in thirteen weeks*. Chapter 127 carried *the most expensive sentence anybody in this volume of his life had ever been the end of*, rewritten. **The widened scan including the bare singulars, `pre-canon`, `Stage [0-9]` and `canon` returns one hit in Volume 03 body text and it is the records-request series in the regional letter, which is a legitimate use.**
- **The run count was one day too high in five chapters** and the money followed it. Corrected to 20, 21, 21, 22, 23, 24, 24, with £110, £115.50, £121, £132. **This is the third time this repository has counted a run from a previous chapter's prose instead of from its start date, and the standing rule is unchanged: the run is derived from Saturday 29 November 2042 and never from a chapter.**
- **Four calendar errors, all found by deriving from day zero rather than from the table.** The works' sequence had a clearance on **Sunday 27 December**, which the yard cannot work. The January booking started on **"Tuesday the fifth of January"** and **5 January 2043 is a Monday**. A block was named as the twenty-eighth, which is a **Wednesday** in January and a **Sunday** in December. And Chapter 127 said **2 January is a Tuesday**; it is a Friday, and so is 16 January. The prompt for this batch itself said *the works yard is shut Friday 25 December as well as Sunday 27 December* — **27 December 2042 is a Saturday —** and said *the 25th is also a depot Friday*, **when the 25th is a Thursday and the depot Fridays in this batch are the 19th and the 26th.** **The prose resolves both in favour of the dates and the calendar: the yard is shut 25, 26, 27 and 28 December, shown on the typed line of the closure card behind glass, and the Friday halves on the depot notice are the 19th and the 26th and are empty in his week as every Friday half is.**
- **Chapter 121 had Elias knowing Rhoda Mistry's eleven before he had met her**, and knowing the date of a clearance that had not happened. Rewritten as an accident: the wind takes the sleeve door, the worked pile comes off the second nail, and the two names on the card are off the night of the sixteenth. **The wind, the second nail and Peart squaring the pile the right way round are now load-bearing.**
- **Chapter 125 was 2,534 words and was cut to 2,375 with no beat cut and no scene removed.** Five concrete places lost a clause: the gauge's stain, Tallow's sentence about permission, the restatement after the step-four line, the restatement after the one-question line, and the room coming apart. **The midpoint's two speeches are untouched.**
- **The hand-flat-on-a-table motif appeared four times** across the batch and now appears **once**, in Chapter 124, in Nerys Hobbs's mouth, with the reason on the page: *which she had never once done in any room he had been in.* Elias's is now his hand put down hard on the trestle in Chapter 125; Inez's in Chapter 130 is her hand kept on the lid of the lock-box; the Chapter 128 one is two fingers on a box lid.
- **Chapter 124 recited a Load Reader limit** in Elias's mouth to Hobbs and restated the volume's central distinction's neighborhood. Cut. It now says only that he told her the terms of the other thing on 11 December in that room before he acted on it, and that the promise about no list is made fresh in that room.
- **Chapter 128's Ru Sabar delivered a neat formulation of the mechanism** — *the first document is the one they had to be asked about, and the second one is the one nobody has to be asked about* — cut and replaced with the heating in that room being off since two o'clock. **No bystander in this batch states anything the reader has not already been shown.**
- **Four small figure slips** found in the batch's own repair round: Hobbs's *nine years* changed to *twenty-two* so it cannot collide with Ferrand's nine; Mistry's list described as four years old and as three years old in two chapters, now three in both; Joon's *sixty days* for 9 November to 27 December, which is forty-eight; and Inez's *eleven days late* for an arrangement made on the 23rd and praised on the 26th, which is three.
- **Alagoa's own line was paraphrased back at her** in Chapter 129 — *a list of doors and not a list of people* — and is now *I have never once written a person on it*, which keeps the fact and stops the echo of Chapter 109.
- **The works' sequence was built so that no block is cleared twice**, so that the eleven doors of the night of 23 December, the eleven people on Mistry's list, and the eleven cards Peart made are one thing and not three. **A repair pass later moved the first December night off 21 December, because 21 December is a Sunday and the yard is shut Sundays, gave it Marden Row 2–10, and moved the Rennard block of 10 January to 22–32, so that the eight printed ranges are pairwise disjoint street by street.**

## Locks confirmed

Zero restatement of Chapter 106's sentence in this batch, and after the review-repair pass **zero in the whole of Volume 03**, the two instances at 114 and 120 having been rewritten in place. The sixth institutional pattern unspoken. Three wall questions untouched, bracket empty, 19 November the only date, wall full, nobody builds another wall, and the four-minute rule goes in Petar's book and **not** on the board. Who-benefits carried as conduct only: he does not open a folder, does not trade Rusk back, does not copy a street, does not give a woman four dates she did not ask for, does not look at the 1998 log, and does not take a Tidegate address out of the building. No doorway refusal beat staged. No `Choir`, no `faster`, no `SECURED SUBJECT` or `SUBJECT HELD` before Chapter 124. Perpetua unnamed. Tarin Voss not fixed. Sector 4C, nine, 2011, `K-7Q`, the 1998 page untouched. No future Nell, no Door Memory, no branch travel; the envelope unsent, four words. No Sable, no Rusk, no second thread, no new lender, no new mechanic, no new species. **The verification log is neither received, nor chased, nor asked after in a room, and the two dates are said aloud in a room and refuse to be said sooner.** The depot Tuesday is used once in this batch (125) and the Friday inventory half is empty in his week and is not gone. **Christmas Day and Boxing Day are both ordinary civic closures, shown on printed notices, with no hearing, no counter transaction, no determination, no yard, no method officer and no board surveyor on either day.**

## Review repair pass on Batch 0003, 2026-09-26 — the durable facts, for any later phase

A review returned six blocking findings, eleven medium and six low, and this pass applied all of them plus **two defects the review did not find and this pass found while re-deriving the calendar**, which are item 7 and item 8 below. No chapter was restarted, no scene replaced, no beat cut or added, no day in the volume moved, the Chapter 125 panel and both of its speeches untouched, the ending shape unchanged, and exactly one next-phase prompt still on disk. Detail in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0003/SUMMARY.md`. **These are the facts a later phase would otherwise inherit wrong.**

- **The works' booking is twenty-eight working days, Monday 5 January 2043 to Thursday 5 February 2043.** It was written as *twenty* in four chapters and in five state files, and twenty working days from 5 January ends in the last week of January, not in February. **The derivation is now printed in this file: 5 January to 5 February inclusive is thirty-two days containing four Sundays, and the yard is shut Sundays, which leaves twenty-eight on a six-day week.** 5 February 2043 is a Thursday. The date never moved; only the numeral, because the date is what Hobbs's point about the money hangs on. **The Chapter 101 gate slip about writing to the board's surveyor is a different twenty working days and was not touched.**
- **The works' sequence was wrong twice over and is now right in the strong form.** It carried **14–22 Cutbank Row on both 21 December and 17 January**, and **2–12 Rennard on both 22 December and 10 January**, while `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and the batch summary all certified *no block cleared twice*. Worse, **21 December 2042 is a Sunday and the yard is shut Sundays**, which is also the day Chapter 123 has him not cross the cut. The planner now reads **20 DEC 2–10 Marden Row · 22 DEC 2–12 Rennard · 23 DEC 4–14 Murrow crew 5 · 24 DEC 4–12 Marlow St · 6 JAN 14–22 Marlow St · 10 JAN 22–32 Rennard + 16–18 Murrow · 17 JAN 14–22 Cutbank Row + 31 Cutbank Row · 19 JAN 15–21 Rennard + 2–12 Cutbank Row**, and **every street's ranges are pairwise disjoint**, which is a stronger truth than the claim used to be. Marden Row is already in the district twice over — a stair on it in November watched from a gate, and the church hall off it — so a night on it adds no new fact. **Nothing load-bearing moved: the eleven doors of 23 December, the woman at 31 Cutbank Row last on the seventeenth, two blocks on the tenth, two more on the seventeenth, two Saturdays and a Monday in January, and the booking that closes in February are all exactly where they were.**
- **No night clearance falls on a Sunday, and 21 December never had one.** Weekdays derived from day zero: 20 Dec Saturday, 22 Dec Monday, 23 Dec Tuesday, 24 Dec Wednesday, 6 Jan Tuesday, 10 Jan Saturday, 17 Jan Saturday, 19 Jan Monday. The January four are two Saturdays and a Monday, which is what Hobbs says in Chapter 126 and what Inez says in Chapter 128.
- **Peart's door total was three hundred and something and the eight printed blocks come to ninety-one.** Now **ninety and something**, and the inclusive count is printed here so the figure can never be re-broken. His *nine, eleven, eleven and the rest* is correct as printed and is the inclusive count of the first three nights.
- **The hand lamp has one price, and it is the batteries.** Bought Christmas Eve for **a pound and something**; **two batteries, a pound ten, which is the running cost and the only figure the lamp is ever given.** Chapter 130 said *two pounds ten to run* in one line and *a pound and ten* for the batteries in another, and this file contradicted itself between two of its own lines. It now has one figure everywhere, in prose and in all five state and workspace files. On Christmas Day it is **in his coat with nothing in it**, which is what Chapter 127 now says, and the batteries went in on the 26th.
- **The four dates he carried to the bollard are the four December nights — 20, 22, 23 and 24 December 2042 — and three of them are already gone by the 27th.** The state files had them as *the four January nights*, which would have had him offering a woman a schedule four days old about her own street. Chapter 129 says out loud that Rennard comes on the twenty-second and Murrow on the twenty-third, and those are December. Corrected in `state/open-threads.md` item 145, in the chapter summary and in the Batch 0004 prompt.
- **A woman of forty at *number twenty-two* since 1988 in Chapter 129 collided with Winifred Alagoa, who is of 22 Cutbank Row and has been there thirty-eight years.** Now **number eighteen**, which is inside Sallow Court's three-to-twenty-seven and collides with nothing. This is the third time this repository has paid for the same address.
- **The fares multiply.** Chapter 124 paired *twenty-two fares at two pounds seventy-five* with *a hundred and twenty-one pounds*; twenty-two single fares are sixty pounds fifty, and 121 pounds is twenty-two **round trips** at five-fifty. It now says twenty-two round trips at five pounds fifty. The unit that carries every cumulative figure in this volume is the round trip, and 110 at twenty days, 115.50 at twenty-one, 121 at twenty-two and 132 at twenty-four all check against it.
- **Chapter 106's sentence had been restated twice, not once, and both are now gone.** A method officer at Chapter 114 and Rusk at Chapter 120 each reached for *none of the three is the same quantity* in a second character's mouth, which is the exact failure the lock exists to prevent. Both lines were rewritten in place — Anum now says the three of them are true and none will tell the other two what it means, and Rusk says three documents and three owners and you have been in a room with all three. **The volume says it once, at Chapter 106, and the Batch 0004 prompt now names this failure mode so a fresh synonym is not reached for instead.**
- **The thread was never live in the Ash Street hall and Chapter 128 no longer implies that it was.** It said *off in that building since the fourteenth of December*, which invents an on-date. Chapter 114 states the rule in the plainest terms available — the hall on Ash Street was not. Chapter 128 now says it had never once been on in that building and it was not on now.
- **A sixty-word paragraph was printed twice, byte for byte**, as Petar's reading of the wall at Chapter 123 and again at Chapter 127, while Chapter 130 paraphrased the same ritual a third way. The full reading stands at Chapter 123, which is where it belongs and the only place a reader needs it. Chapter 127 now reports the repetition in its own words and gives the day its own texture, and Chapter 130's summary is left as the narrator's compression of a ritual the reader has now seen once at full length.
- **Chapter 129 cancelled itself out and the order is now the other way round.** He told her the number *because it was on a street and not in a room* and then said, ten lines later, that he was not going to be the next voice on that corner. **The restraint now comes first and the number second**, and the sentence that carried the Chapter 106 echo is rewritten — *two is a fact about two pieces of paper and not about anybody* — so that the nearest approach in this batch to restating Chapter 106 is a remark about an arithmetic gap between two printed lists and not about the conversion.
- **Chapter 129 attributed to the 18 December bridge a sentence that is not on it.** What he says on the bridge is that the reason the four are not coming out of her margin is her consent and not a moral argument. That is now what Chapter 129 attributes, and the aphorism about a third place is his own extension of it rather than a quotation.
- **Three figures in Chapter 121 were unsourced or wrong and are now sourced or consistent.** *Nine hundred doors* contradicted the sheet's thousand elsewhere in the batch; *nineteen hundred people on the alignment* was a third competing headcount against the board's 2,900 model, in a batch whose argument is that nobody can count people — it is now the board's own model, named as a model; and the stair paragraph was a near-verbatim clone of Chapter 118 three days earlier, sharing the rail, the hand and a *told him nothing whatever about* clause, and is now its own paragraph with a different object list and one honest beat of growth at the end of it.
- **Smaller things, all applied:** *Miss Okafor* in Chapter 124, where she is *Mara* in dialogue three chapters running; the four names on *the back of the same forearm* in Chapter 130, where canon has them on the back of the **right hand** and the two dates on the inside of the **left forearm**; *wrote* as filler, five times in Chapter 125 and three times in two sentences at Chapter 122, now two and one; the depot notice in Chapter 128, which restated Chapter 121's paragraph almost word for word and now reads as the callback it is; and **the left hand, which is a working limitation in every scene, was missing from Chapter 128** — the one scene where it does the most work, since a man who cannot hold a sheet of paper up cannot read a letter to nine people off it, so he puts it on the table and turns it round at the end of every paragraph.
- **The Batch 0003 prompt's calendar was defective and the prose was right**, which is the second time in this volume that has been true. It called Friday 25 December Christmas Day and Saturday 26 December Boxing Day, made the 25th a depot Friday, and said the yard shuts *five days, not six* where two typed notices in the prose print four, 25 to 28 December. The prompt is corrected from day zero. **It also carried no warning against a Sunday night clearance, which is what allowed the 21 December row to be written at all.**
- **The Batch 0004 prompt's day table was also defective, and in the same way.** It labelled **3 January the first January night** and 6 January the second, when the printed sequence's first January night is 6 January and there is no night at all on 3 January; and its working-week sentence ran the New Year week one day out, shutting the yard on *Saturday 2, Sunday 3* and opening it Monday 4, when 2 and 3 January are a Friday and a Saturday, 4 January is the Sunday and is the yard's only shut day in the batch. Both were found by re-deriving from day zero rather than by reading the table, which is the third time in this volume that a prompt's own day table has been the thing that was wrong while the prose was right. The day table, the working-week sentence and the sequence table are now all derived and all agree with Chapters 121, 123, 126 and 128.
- **The cards gap is systemic and is recorded rather than papered over.** `outline/batches/` has cards for six of fourteen batches, and neither `volume-03-batch-0003.md` nor `-0004.md` exists, so for this batch the prompt is the only assignment of record. **No cards file was invented here**: the prompt for Batch 0004 is complete, dated and internally consistent, and a cards file written after the fact would be a second source of truth for a batch that has not started.
- **Re-measured after the pass.** Chapters 121–130 are **20,919 words**: 121 (2,399) · 122 (1,915) · 123 (1,903) · 124 (1,982) · 125 (2,373) · 126 (2,022) · 127 (2,037) · 128 (1,988) · 129 (2,049) · 130 (2,251). All ten inside 1,800–2,400 and the batch inside 20,000–24,000. **Chapter 121 came back to the 2,400 ceiling by cutting five connective clauses and a redundant relative, because the rule in this repository is *cut, not add* and a repair pass may not buy prose with a new length debt.** Chapters 114 and 120 are 2,050 and 1,871 and one line of each changed.
- **The British-form pattern and the widened narrator pattern were re-run over the whole of `chapters/` after the pass and return zero.** The interface notice count is still exactly one in this batch and still exactly three in the volume.

# Volume 03 continuity — BATCH 0004 AS WRITTEN, recorded 2026-09-26 by the Volume 03 Batch 0004 prose phase

**This section is what Chapters 131–140 actually did. Where the pre-batch prompt restates canon and this section disagrees, this section wins. Every new name, street, form, figure, date and document the ten chapters invented is here, because the next batch will inherit it whether or not it is written down.**

## Measured result

**Chapters 131–140, *Four Nights*, days 114–123, Monday 29 December 2042 to Wednesday 7 January 2043. 20,775 words, measured with `wc -w` and not estimated.** Per chapter: 131 (2,388) · 132 (1,862) · 133 (1,931) · 134 (2,392) · 135 (2,251) · 136 (1,926) · 137 (2,025) · 138 (2,299) · 139 (1,853) · 140 (1,848). **All ten inside 1,800–2,400 and the batch inside 20,000–24,000.**

**Exactly one interface notice in the batch, Chapter 134, and it is a boundary only. Volume 03 has now spent all three of its notices: 113 accepted, 125 refused, 134 a boundary and nothing else. There is no fourth and no later batch may spend one.** Rusk does not appear. Sable does not appear. **Tobias Ferrand appears once, in person, on 2 January, and says recorded figures only.** `Choir` is not spoken, written, or on any reproduced document. The word `faster` does not appear in this batch. **No gift is used anywhere in the ten chapters** — the thread is felt live and quiet and is never used for anything.

## The ten beats, in order

131 **Five Door Numbers** · Mon 29 Dec day 114 · yard reopens behind a closure card nobody took down. **The fourth reading is fixed for Thursday 1 January** and Anum says the reading will not care who is standing on the street, and nobody asked him. **Hobbs refuses him the block in a shut-door room** and gives him a fifth of what he asked for. **He walks 14–22 Marlow Street in daylight and a woman at number eighteen tells him there are nine kitchens behind five door numbers**, and he does not write it down. **Peart tells him what a lamp is for: a door.** The notice's date fixed.

132 **Three Minutes, And Then She Was Not Going To Be In It** · Tue 30 Dec day 115 · depot Tuesday at ten to six, Dunleavy's book, twenty minutes, hour of sleep for life. **The four-minute practice, used on himself for the first time: he tells Mara he is going to ask, stops, counts, and she says yes at three minutes and then says she is not going to be the person he tells what it means.** **Mara's own work: eleven of the sixty on the Marden Row round are invoiceable through the health center and forty-nine are not, and a committee in the second week of January has not read a letter, and she stops him in a corridor when he starts to make the forty-nine into a thing he is right about.** **Peart: two of the eleven are on the 6 January block, and he will not say which doors.** Joon on a payphone refuses to give the dates on a telephone.

133 **A Notice With No Addresses On It** · Wed 31 Dec day 116 · last working day of the year. **Peart pins up a printed works' night schedule on the gate: four January nights, four blocks, four crew numbers, no address on it, and a line saying it is not a program document**, after three weeks and two arguments with a woman from the board's communications. **The document he did not hand to a woman at a bollard is now on a nail and in a newsagent's shop, and the part a woman actually needs — which house — is not on it and will not be, and his restraint did not protect her from anything; it protected her from him.** The woman at thirty-one and the seventeenth. The run is over.

134 **Two Times On A Page And Nothing Between Them** · **Thu 1 Jan day 117 · THE THIRD AND LAST INTERFACE NOTICE, A BOUNDARY ONLY.** The panel, the two times, the forty-four, the reissue page, Hollis Vane's observation and the two hours of finding out that there was nothing to do about it are all below under their own heads.

135 **The Second Of January Is A Friday And A List About Crews** · Fri 2 Jan day 118 · **the near end passes and the log does not come.** Tallow: a near end is a service target, the estimate said four to six weeks, the far end is the sixteenth. **Nobody chases it and nobody asks Joon.** **Ferrand comes for twenty minutes with no folder and issues the operational list, and the Retention Schedule of 2 December is held and no further schedule is to be issued.** **The terrible use, put side by side on purpose in a cold corridor, thought about, not done, and not absolved.** Hobbs will not sign the list.

136 **What A Notice Does Not Carry** · Sat 3 Jan day 119 · counter shut, yard open, board shut, all on paper. **Alagoa reads the notice four times at the bollard and says the thing nobody in the program said in nine months: it does not have December on it, and the woman at number nine Rennard who went on the twenty-second is on no nail and in no list.** The woman of forty asks whether thirty-nine is the right one. **A woman of seventy tells Elias he is not a man who knows and keeps it, and not to stand there like one.**

137 **A Lamp On A Table Gets Voted On** · Sun 4 Jan day 120 · the yard's one shut day, the closure card still up and out of date. **A lamp goes on the table in the front room and the hall votes on it: nine for, four against, one abstention, eleven pounds and fifty of a float of about twenty, for the light over the stairs**, and it goes in the book and not on the board. **Inez says her two refusals, current, and neither touches a lamp.** **Joon refuses to ask what happened on Thursday morning and says why, and says the far end once to a nine-year-old who asked.** **Petar's second list has a name on it in his mother's hand since 2029 and the 1998 box stays shut.**

138 **Two Documents On One Wall And Neither One Taken Down** · Mon 5 Jan day 121 · the booking's first working day. **Peart pins the program's list up beside his own sequence and takes neither down, and says the day they stop agreeing is a Tuesday in February and it is not his to decide.** The `DOORS` column against a card for every door. **The board's surveyor, unnamed, third visit, is given all four reading dates in ninety seconds and writes one word in his notebook: *Present*.** **Peart gives him a job: count the door numbers and write the five on the back of your hand.** **Hobbs will not fill in the form for a person present, and names the cost.**

139 **Five On The Back Of My Hand** · Tue 6 Jan day 122 · **the first of the four January nights, 14–22 Marlow Street, crew 4.** Eleven hours on a road. **Nobody is hurt.** The interiors of the five doors are four men's business and are not depicted. **At about eleven a crew lamp battery goes flat and he gives his lamp and holds the frame for about two hours, and Peart sees it from the middle of the road once and looks away, and nothing is written about it anywhere.** At about four in the morning two men spend an hour at one of the five doors and he does not know which and does not ask. **Five on the back of his left hand in biro, written with his right hand.** One line of four signed.

140 **What He Did Not Write Down** · Wed 7 Jan day 123 · the morning after. He tells a room of nineteen what happened, in the words it happened in, and puts his hand out. **Inez refuses to merge his lamp with the building's.** Cliff asks the one question. **Joon says the first night is the first night with nothing on his list of things this building did not do, and asks nobody to call it a win.** **He writes one line in the notebook and leaves five things out, and the reason is not a rule.**

## The interface notice at Chapter 134, verbatim, and it is the last one in the volume

```text
Loan offered: Tomas Vale, nineteen years ahead
Gift name:
Gift:
Boundary: The reading is mine to give. The list of who is on it is not.
Return:
```

**Three of the five fields are empty. There is no gift in it, no condition attached to it, and nothing in the return. The boundary is the whole of what came back.** This is the same approved shape as the Chapter 113 panel with the fields left blank, and **no later phase may print a fourth notice in this volume.**

**The asking is the innocent action and nobody says so.** The sequence is on the page and in nobody's mouth: **06:10**, his hand on the brick at the north corner of Marlow Street before the barriers went up, and **07:15**, the reader on the alignment. The street shut at 06:20; the reader was off it at 07:50; the output was on the trestle at a quarter to eleven; the fourth occasion's check box is signed; the slip is issued. **He writes the two times on the inside back cover of the notebook under the two January dates and writes nothing between them, and no character says one word about how any of it happened, and no narrator either, and no ordinal is counted aloud by anybody.** Joon Park, told nothing, chooses not to ask and says so in a room on the Sunday night, and says what asking would cost.

**The securing band moved two streets: it came off Rennard and off Cutbank Row and went back the way it came in September.** **Forty-four addresses change class. Every one of them was `RETAINED` on 2 December. Every one of them is on Rennard or on Cutbank Row. Hobbs's split, given once at Chapter 138 and not again: thirty on Cutbank Row, the rest on Rennard.** The reader can derive the new totals from the printed December figures and the forty-four; **no character says either number in a room.** **This is the event the outline specifies and it is not Chapter 119: Chapter 119's softening is not mentioned, restated, extended, scaled or explained anywhere in this batch, and no chapter in these ten mentions it at all.**

## The sixth institutional pattern, in Hollis Vane's own words, once, at Chapter 134

**Hollis Vane, about sixty, nineteen years the custodian of exhibits to a board of inquiry** (Volumes 01–02 canon, unchanged), **came from the Meridian on purpose on New Year's Day, on a train, and said so in the first thirty seconds.** His reason is correct and procedural: **a copy of the Retention Schedule went into his file in the Meridian on 17 December inside the Tidal Board's query envelope, a copy that goes into his file is an exhibit, and an exhibit can only be superseded by a document that says which document it supersedes and on what date it takes effect.** The 1 January reissue page does neither, so he cannot supersede it, and the copy in his folder carries 17 December and not 2 December.

**His observation, once, in his own mouth, about his own trade and not about Tidegate:** every document he keeps is a picture of a day; a photograph of a street with a date on the back is worth having; he has never once been asked to tell somebody what a picture was a picture of on a day that was not the day it was taken; **there is no field for that anywhere in the Office, not on this, not on his, not on anything a form has ever asked him to keep**; the man who drew the picture did not have to know what the light was going to do; a picture of a day is not supposed to want to know about any other day; nobody designed a form to be asked that, because nobody ever thought anybody would come and ask. And then: **"The cabinet. I am the cabinet."**

**The Retention Schedule's missing person-column is the third of the five series patterns and is not new and is not presented as new anywhere in this batch.** Chapter 134 says in one sentence that there was a second thing in the room, that it was on page two of a document in a folder two miles away, that it was not what Vane had just said, and that Elias Rook did not say it and was glad afterwards. **No narrator states it, no character states it, no ordinal is counted aloud, and no chapter connects the two.** That is the whole permitted handling.

## New names, streets, forms, figures and objects invented here and not before

- **The works' night schedule, 31 December 2042**, printed by the yard, pinned to the gate beside the closure card with a bent drawing pin, and six copies to Mrs Postridge at 2 Cutbank Row. Four rows: 6 JAN 14–22 Marlow St crew 4 · 10 JAN 22–32 Rennard + 16–18 Murrow crew 5 · 17 JAN 14–22 Cutbank Row + 31 Cutbank Row crew 4 · 19 JAN 15–21 Rennard + 2–12 Cutbank Row crew 4. **A line at the foot reads `Printed by the yard. Not a program document.`** **No door number appears on it, and that is Peart's own decision in about nine seconds, stated in Chapter 133: a printed sheet a newsagent will hand round, with twenty-one door numbers on it, lets a person at the gate write down which house a crew is going into on a Tuesday night.** It went to two shops, a cafe and the church hall by 3 January.
- **The operational list, 2 January 2043**, ref `BWP/TG/4SD/2042/OP/1`, program letterhead, one page, columns `NIGHT / BLOCK / CREW / DOORS`, values 4·5, 5·8, 4·6, 4·10. **No occupancy class, no status, no address, no date of any reading, no person.** It is what a crew is handed at seven in the evening and it is the first thing the program has issued under the Boxing Day letter.
- **The reissue page, 1 January 2043**: one page, a program reference, a check box signed by the method officer, a signature block. It **does not withdraw the schedule** and it is not a schedule.
- **The December tally line is now wrong and is not crossed out.** `IF ACTIONED — TRANSITIONAL 241 · RETAINED 456` was printed on 1 December for a movement that has not happened, and the movement that did happen went the other way, and there is no hand in the room with the standing to draw a line through another office's printed figure, and the method officer says he is not going to be the man who scratches it out and wants that said in a room while he is in it.
- **Hobbs's booking sheet**: one page, **four ruled lines, one per night**, signed and dated with the hour at about ten past seven in the morning after each clearance. **Two men in an office have asked her to add a line for anything left behind and she has said no to both**, and the reason is on the page: a line for items left behind turns a sheet about a street into a sheet about a suspicion.
- **The board's surveyor**, **unnamed, as he has been unnamed since Chapter 116.** Third visit to the district. Given all four reading dates — 11 September twenty to seven, 29 November quarter past six, 1 January quarter past seven — in about ninety seconds by Hobbs, wrote them in a small hardback notebook with a rubber band round it, said *that is four occasions and my query is one date*, walked the alignment end to end in an hour and ten minutes, and wrote one word in the notebook and turned the notebook round: **Present.** "It is on a hundred thousand forms in Civic buildings and it has never once meant that anybody is owed anything."
- **Nine kitchens behind five door numbers on 14–22 Marlow Street.** Told to Elias on 29 December by the woman at number eighteen with a folded pram against the wall, at her own door, not written down, and she watched him not open the notebook. **Peart counted nine households on that block in daylight in December; Hobbs has the nine and has never had it in writing.** Nobody prints nine on a document and nobody says the two numbers in a room.
- **Five on the back of his left hand in biro, written with his right hand** just after the barriers came down on the morning of 6 January. **The job came from Peart on the afternoon of Monday 5 January in about forty words**: count the door numbers, not the people; write the five on the back of your hand; tell nobody, and if somebody does ask, say five and say it came off a printed list and not off anybody's head.
- **A woman of about forty at 18 Sallow Court**, since 1988, who wanted a number in December and wanted it to be the right one, and who asked Alagoa on 3 January whether thirty-nine is the right one and was told no, it is the one she has, and that she would not say so even if she knew. **A woman of about seventy at the corner of Cutbank Row** who told Elias he is not a man who knows and keeps it, and not to stand there like one. **A woman of about seventy at 15 Rennard** who went out on the night of 22 December with a bag and her cat in a basket and is on no nail and in no list. Both are new and unnamed and are not on any list.
- **The hall's vote, Sunday 4 January**, in Petar Lisk's book on the page after 22 November, the four minutes above it: **nine for, four against, one abstention, eleven pounds and fifty of a float of about twenty pounds**, for a rechargeable works lamp bought off the shelf of things people bring back, for the light over the stairs. **The four minutes did not go on the board and the board had no room.**
- **Petar Lisk's two lists** are in the back of a squared pad. **The second list has carried one name since 2029 in his mother's hand**, the name is not anybody in the building, Petar has never met him, and Petar will not give the name to Elias because *the list is the list of people who have a reason to open it and the name is on the list*. **The 1998 working log stayed shut.** The box came off the top shelf on Boxing Day and went back on Sunday 28 December and did not come down again in this batch.

## Figures derived in this batch, all from the day map, none copied

- **Cliff Marner 84 → 93** (day less thirty). **Della Marner and the man behind the wall 114 → 123** (day). All correct in every one of the ten chapters.
- **The Tidegate run is over and stays over.** It stopped on Wednesday 24 December at **twenty-four riding days, two Sundays out of it, and £132.** He pays single fares at **two pounds seventy-five each way, eleven minutes each way**, and **no cumulative figure is printed anywhere in this batch**, because the standing rule is that the run is derived from 29 November and never restarted. **Pounds only; no dollars.**
- **The four readings**, and this is the fixed order: **occasion 1, 11 September 2042, taken 06:40, works clearance 05:50–07:05, issued 11 September 08:10 · occasion 2, 29 November 2042, taken 06:15, clearance 05:50–07:30, issued 1 December 09:40 · occasion 3 does not exist · occasion 4, 1 January 2043, street shut 06:20, reader on the alignment 07:15, off it 07:50, output on the trestle about 10:45, check box signed.** The ref is `BWP/TG/4SD/2042` on all three. **Chapter 138 has the surveyor write all four and Chapter 138 is the only chapter in the volume in which the four dates are read out in one breath**, by the engineer, in ninety seconds, to a man with a folder.
- **Not drift and not touched**: hall twenty-two cards, twenty-eight people, four dark hours (nineteen people are in the front room at nine on the morning of 7 January, which is a room, not a count); custody twenty-two items; works model 1,140 and 2,900; schedule 697 with 412 `RETAINED` and 285 `TRANSITIONAL`, dated 2 December, footnote `Current reading: 11 September 2042`; forty-four addresses inside September and outside November, all `TRANSITIONAL`; the December tally 241/456; 41 against 39 for 3 to 27 Sallow Court, still unclosed; the second reading is the one taken on 29 November and is called neither December nor November.
- **A CORRECTION TO THIS FILE, made by deriving rather than by copying.** The Batch 0002 and Batch 0003 records in this file say the eight printed blocks "come to ninety-one" and that Peart's *ninety and something* is that inclusive count. **They do not.** Inclusive of the street-facing door numbers, the eight printed ranges come to **fifty-one** (2–10 Marden Row 5 · 2–12 Rennard 6 · 4–14 Murrow 6 · 4–12 Marlow St 5 · 14–22 Marlow St 5 · 22–32 Rennard 6 + 16–18 Murrow 2 · 14–22 Cutbank Row 5 + 31 Cutbank Row 1 · 15–21 Rennard 4 + 2–12 Cutbank Row 6). **Peart's figure is therefore not a count of the printed ranges and this batch does not pretend it is.** The prose reconciles it once, in Peart's mouth at Chapter 138, and the reconciliation is now canon: **a range of numbers is a street and a card is a door, and a flat has a door and does not have a number on the street**, which is why the program's `DOORS` column and the works' card count have never once agreed and never have been reconciled. **No chapter may print a door total, and the *ninety and something* of Chapter 121 stands as the works' own count of doors including flats.** The Batch 0002 and Batch 0003 statements that the inclusive count is ninety-one are wrong and are superseded by this paragraph.
- **`Before I say anything else` appears zero times in Chapters 131–140.** The volume-wide count is still five chapters (108, 112, 114, 116, 120) and Chapter 108 is the Batch 0001 instance the Batch 0002 records missed, left standing as before.
- **A pre-existing residual this pass found and did not touch.** The word **`fastest`** stands once in Volume 03, in Chapter 109, in Joon Park's line *which is the fastest he ever does anything*. It is a different token from the banned `faster`, it is Batch 0001's, and the ban is on `faster`. **It is recorded here rather than silently ignored, and no writer should spend a repair pass on a pre-existing adverb in a closed batch.**

## What the batch did to each character, and what it did not

- **Elias Rook** asks the boundary to be repeated at 06:10 on 1 January because he told a woman on 30 December that he was going to ask, and she said yes at three minutes, and the asking is what the morning is built around, and nobody in the room says so and he does not write it down. He pays a return condition the panel did not re-state. **He looks at the terrible use on purpose in a cold corridor on 2 January, puts a page of forty-four addresses next to a thread that tells him when a man is near, does not do it, and is told by nobody that he was right to stop.** He is refused the 6 January block by an engineer, given a job counting door numbers, stands on a road for eleven hours, holds one door frame for two of them, and ends the batch with a notebook containing one line and five things he has decided not to write down. **He does not get the gift, the girl, or an ending, and nobody lets him off.**
- **Mara Okafor** keeps all four standing refusals, says yes to being told inside four minutes for the first time in a corridor about a thing she is not going to be in, refuses him the right to make her round into a moral point, and is the most competent person in the batch. **Her chapter 125 refusal is not revisited, not softened, and not a wound.**
- **Tomas Vale** is not asked, not consulted, not approached and does not appear. He is named in the Chapter 134 panel and once in the corridor at Chapter 135 as the man who cannot be consulted about it. **The eight pages are not shown and not connected to anything.**
- **Joon Park** holds the custody schedule at twenty-two, refuses to ask what happened on Thursday morning and explains the price of asking, says the far end once to a boy who asked in a room, and closes the batch with a list of four things this building did not do that has, for the first time, no line for a night. **He is still the only we-do-not-know-that voice and he is not a narrator.**
- **Inez Sato** keeps both standing refusals current, says them out loud in a room so nobody can get clever, puts a lamp on a table and lets the hall vote on it, refuses in Chapter 140 to merge the building's lamp with his, and closes the lock-box.
- **Hollis Vane** appears once, says the volume's sixth institutional pattern once in his own words, takes the blame for it in his own mouth, and is gone. **He is not a friend, an ally or a convert, and no chapter says he is.**
- **Nerys Hobbs** refuses him the block, gives him a fifth of it, tells the surveyor the four dates, refuses twice to add an items-left-behind line, refuses to fill in the form for a person present and names the cost, refuses to sign the program's list, signs her own line at ten past seven, and says in the last scene of Chapter 138 that she is going to empty Rennard and Cutbank Row anyway, that she is not going to do it well, and that both are true.
- **Nev Peart** prints the notice, argues with communications, gives Elias the counting job, says what a lamp is for, says two of the eleven are on the block and not which, sees the door frame held and looks away and writes nothing. **The shift book is on the bench, untouched, and the reason he keeps it is still unspoken and is still Chapter 141's hinge.**
- **Selwyn Anum** fixes the date in one line on 27 December, tells Elias the reading will not care, fills the check box, dates the reissue, refuses to have a word put on it before somebody says the word, and refuses to scratch out another office's printed figure.
- **Wendy Tallow** gives the near end honestly and is not asked to chase anything.
- **Tobias Ferrand** appears in person for twenty minutes, says four things, says 285 and 1,140 and about 2,900 and nine years, does not deny the reissue page, says he has nothing to say to the forty-four that would be true, and says that in February there will be no line in any document of his with Elias's name on it and that he can live with that. **He states the volume's central distinction nowhere and no synonym of it is put in his mouth.**
- **Winifred Alagoa** reads the notice four times, says the thing about December, does not give away the forty-one, and does not take a sentence out of the street into a room.
- **Ezinne Abioye, Marged Prosser, Ivor Harkness, Ru Sabar, Ansel Duarte, Nadi Bell, Lena Mir, Marigold Truscott, Osei Kwarteng** do not appear or are not named in this batch. **Perpetua Oyelaran is not named.**

## Fixes applied in this pass to its own drafts

- **Six chapters came in over or under the ordinary target on the first measure and were brought back by cutting, never by adding**: 131 at 2,464 → 2,388; 132 at 1,862 (in range, kept); 136 at 1,442 → 1,926; 137 at 2,016 (in range, kept); 139 at 1,280 → 1,853; 134 at 2,563 → 2,392. **No scene was removed from any chapter and no beat was cut that had a turn in it**; the expansions in 136 and 139 are new concrete material on the page (the woman at number nine Rennard, the woman of forty asking about thirty-nine, the crew briefing, the man from the flat over number eighteen, the kettle, the lamp frame).
- **The hand-flat-on-a-table motif had crept in again at Chapter 134** as a narrator note about how often a character does it, which is the gesture-becomes-punctuation failure this file records from Volume 02. **It is gone and has been replaced by Nerys Hobbs telling the method officer to write the number in his own hand and giving the professional reason, which is better and prefigures Chapter 141.**
- **Chapter 136 had a narrator sentence that reached for the volume's central argument** — a document being true and still leaving a person out of it. **It is cut.** Alagoa now states a fact about a notice (a January notice does not carry December) and the narrator draws no thesis from it.
- **Ferrand was given a line in Chapter 135 about a crew sheet that carries a name** and it was a second phrasing of Chapter 106 in a second character's mouth, which is the exact failure the lock exists to prevent. **Cut and replaced** with a procedural consequence: a crew sheet with a name on it is a document somebody will eventually be asked to produce in a room, and he has been in that room.
- **Four arithmetic and calendar slips, all found by deriving rather than by reading a table**: Chapter 133 called 31 December the last working day before Monday 5 January when 1 and 2 January are both working days; Chapter 131 and Chapter 134 disagreed about when the street shuts on 1 January (05:40 against 06:20) and the prose now says 06:20 in both; Chapter 138 gave the surveyor the first reading as *twenty past six* when the issue slip says **06:40**; and Chapter 138 had a *thirty hours to go* between Monday 17:10 and a first door at 19:30 on Tuesday.
- **Three internal contradictions between chapters**: Hobbs's booking sheet was six ruled lines in one chapter and four in another and is four everywhere now, with one line signed after 6 January and none before; the street was walked at 20:05 in Chapter 140 and about 05:30 in Chapter 139; Chapter 136 said a shelf had not been touched since September in the same breath as somebody touching it.
- **The left hand, a working limitation in every scene, was nearly used where it cannot be.** He writes the five on the back of it with his right hand, and the prose says so.
- **US spelling: the authoritative pattern was run over the whole of `chapters/` after the prose and returns zero. The widened narrator-reference scan including the bare singulars, `pre-canon`, `Stage [0-9]` and `canon` returns zero for Volume 03. `Choir` and `faster` are absent from the batch.**

## Locks confirmed

Zero restatement of Chapter 106's sentence in Chapters 131–140 and, after this pass, **zero in the whole of Volume 03.** The Load Reader's five limits are not stated in this batch; the boundary appears once, in the Chapter 134 panel, and **a use may show one limit failing and that is the whole permitted method** — a panel with three empty fields, and a Thursday morning that produced nothing he could carry. The sixth institutional pattern is Vane's, once, in his own words, with no ordinal counted aloud. Three wall questions untouched, bracket empty, the nineteenth of November still the only date, wall full, nobody builds another wall, and the vote and the four minutes go in Petar's book and not on the board. **Who-benefits carried as conduct only: he does not hand over the notice, does not carry a woman's sentence into a room, does not open the 1998 log, does not give the four December dates away, does not name the man on Petar's list, does not ask for a woman's sheet, and does not put the two times on a page into a room.** No doorway refusal beat staged. `SECURED SUBJECT` and `SUBJECT HELD` not used. Perpetua unnamed. Tarin Voss not fixed. Sector 4C, nine, 2011, `K-7Q` and the 1998 page untouched. No future Nell, no Door Memory, no branch travel; the envelope unsent, four words. No Sable, no Rusk, no second thread, no new lender, no new mechanic, no new species. **The verification log is neither received, nor chased, nor asked after in a room, and the two dates are said aloud in a room twice more and refuse to be said sooner.** The depot Tuesday is used once in this batch (132); the Friday inventory half is empty in his week and is not gone. **No hearing, no counter transaction, no determination, no method officer and no board surveyor on 3 or 4 January, and 1 and 2 January are ordinary working days shown on no notice at all.** The hall is not a headquarters, no Tidegate address is read out in the front room, and no Tidegate resident moves to Ash Street.
